# Cheatsheet

Every function, function-like macro and variable in burrow's public headers, 7280 of them, one line each, grouped by header. Each line is the declaration, without its lifetime annotations, and a sentence about it. Where the function or variable has a Go original, the sentence is the first one of Go's doc comment for it. Where it has none, the sentence comes from the comment above it in the header.

This page is generated by `tools/burrow-cheatsheet` from the headers and from Go's sources at go1.27.3, and CI fails if it falls behind them, so edit those and run the tool rather than editing this.

## burrow/archive/tar.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `archive/tar` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error tar_err_header` – tar.ErrHeader: a header that cannot be parsed.
- `extern const Error tar_err_write_too_long` – tar.ErrWriteTooLong: more written to a file than its header's size.
- `extern const Error tar_err_field_too_long` – tar.ErrFieldTooLong: a name or a PAX header too long to store.
- `extern const Error tar_err_write_after_close` – tar.ErrWriteAfterClose: a write to a closed Writer.
- `extern const Error tar_err_insecure_path` – tar.ErrInsecurePath: what tar_reader_next says about a name that is not local, when GODEBUG has tarinsecurepath=0.
- `Str tar_format_string(TarFormat f, Alloc *a)` – Format.String: "USTAR", "(USTAR | PAX)", or "\<unknown\>" for none.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TAR_HEADER`
- `void tar_header_free(Alloc *a, TarHeader *h)` – Gives back everything a header holds and then the header itself, when they came from a as the headers from tar_reader_next and tar_file_info_header do.
- `FsFileInfo tar_header_file_info(TarHeader *h)` – FileInfo returns an fs.FileInfo for the Header.
- `TarHeader *tar_file_info_header(Alloc *a, FsFileInfo fi, Str link, Error *err)` – FileInfoHeader creates a partially-populated Header from fi.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TAR_READER`
- `TarReader *tar_new_reader(Alloc *a, IoReader r)` – NewReader creates a new Reader reading from r.
- `TarHeader *tar_reader_next(TarReader *tr, Error *err)` – Next advances to the next entry in the tar archive.
- `Int tar_reader_read(TarReader *tr, Slice b, Error *err)` – Read reads from the current file in the tar archive.
- `IoReader tar_reader_as_io_reader(TarReader *tr)` – The reader as an IoReader, borrowing tr.
- `void tar_reader_free(TarReader *tr)` – Gives the reader back to its allocator.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TAR_WRITER`
- `TarWriter *tar_new_writer(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewWriter creates a new Writer writing to w.
- `Error tar_writer_flush(TarWriter *tw)` – Flush finishes writing the current file's block padding.
- `Error tar_writer_write_header(TarWriter *tw, const TarHeader *h)` – WriteHeader writes hdr and prepares to accept the file's contents.
- `Int tar_writer_write(TarWriter *tw, Slice b, Error *err)` – Write writes to the current file in the tar archive.
- `Error tar_writer_add_fs(TarWriter *tw, Fs fsys)` – AddFS adds the files from fs.FS to the archive.
- `Error tar_writer_close(TarWriter *tw)` – Close closes the tar archive by flushing the padding, and writing the footer.
- `IoWriter tar_writer_as_io_writer(TarWriter *tw)` – The writer as an IoWriter, borrowing tw.
- `void tar_writer_free(TarWriter *tw)` – Gives the writer back to its allocator.

## burrow/archive/zip.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `archive/zip` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error zip_err_format` – zip.ErrFormat: not a ZIP archive, or a broken one.
- `extern const Error zip_err_algorithm` – zip.ErrAlgorithm: a compression method nothing is registered for.
- `extern const Error zip_err_checksum` – zip.ErrChecksum: a file whose contents do not match its CRC-32.
- `extern const Error zip_err_insecure_path` – zip.ErrInsecurePath: what zip_new_reader and zip_open_reader say about an archive with a name that is not local, when GODEBUG has zipinsecurepath=0.
- `void zip_register_compressor(uint16_t method, ZipCompressor comp)` – RegisterCompressor registers custom compressors for a specified method ID.
- `void zip_register_decompressor(uint16_t method, ZipDecompressor dcomp)` – RegisterDecompressor allows custom decompressors for a specified method ID.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZIP_FILE_HEADER`
- `FsFileInfo zip_file_header_file_info(ZipFileHeader *h)` – FileInfo returns an fs.FileInfo for the FileHeader.
- `FsFileMode zip_file_header_mode(const ZipFileHeader *h)` – Mode returns the permission and mode bits for the FileHeader.
- `void zip_file_header_set_mode(ZipFileHeader *h, FsFileMode mode)` – SetMode changes the permission and mode bits for the FileHeader.
- `Time zip_file_header_mod_time(const ZipFileHeader *h)` – ModTime returns the modification time in UTC using the legacy ModifiedDate and ModifiedTime fields.
- `void zip_file_header_set_mod_time(ZipFileHeader *h, Time t)` – SetModTime sets the Modified, ModifiedTime, and ModifiedDate fields to the given time in UTC.
- `ZipFileHeader *zip_file_info_header(Alloc *a, FsFileInfo fi, Error *err)` – FileInfoHeader creates a partially-populated FileHeader from an fs.FileInfo.
- `void zip_file_header_free(Alloc *a, ZipFileHeader *h)` – Gives back a header from zip_file_info_header and its name.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZIP_FILE`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZIP_READER`
- `ZipReader *zip_new_reader(Alloc *a, IoReaderAt r, int64_t size, Error *err)` – NewReader returns a new Reader reading from r, which is assumed to have the given size in bytes.
- `void zip_reader_register_decompressor(ZipReader *r, uint16_t method, ZipDecompressor dcomp)` – RegisterDecompressor registers or overrides a custom decompressor for a specific method ID.
- `FsFile zip_reader_open(ZipReader *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file in the ZIP archive, using the semantics of fs.FS.Open: paths are always slash separated, with no leading / or ../ elements.
- `Fs zip_reader_as_fs(ZipReader *r)` – The reader as an Fs, borrowing r.
- `void zip_reader_free(ZipReader *r)` – Gives back the reader, its files and all they hold.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZIP_READ_CLOSER`
- `ZipReadCloser *zip_open_reader(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – OpenReader will open the Zip file specified by name and return a ReadCloser.
- `FsFile zip_read_closer_open(ZipReadCloser *rc, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file in the ZIP archive, using the semantics of fs.FS.Open: paths are always slash separated, with no leading / or ../ elements.
- `void zip_read_closer_register_decompressor(ZipReadCloser *rc, uint16_t method, ZipDecompressor dcomp)` – RegisterDecompressor registers or overrides a custom decompressor for a specific method ID.
- `Error zip_read_closer_close(ZipReadCloser *rc)` – Close closes the Zip file, rendering it unusable for I/O.
- `IoReadCloser zip_file_open(ZipFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Open returns a ReadCloser that provides access to the File's contents.
- `IoSectionReader zip_file_open_raw(ZipFile *f, Error *err)` – OpenRaw returns a Reader that provides access to the File's contents without decompression.
- `int64_t zip_file_data_offset(ZipFile *f, Error *err)` – DataOffset returns the offset of the file's possibly-compressed data, relative to the beginning of the zip file.
- `FsFileInfo zip_file_file_info(ZipFile *f)` – FileInfo returns an fs.FileInfo for the FileHeader.
- `FsFileMode zip_file_mode(const ZipFile *f)` – Mode returns the permission and mode bits for the FileHeader.
- `void zip_file_set_mode(ZipFile *f, FsFileMode mode)` – SetMode changes the permission and mode bits for the FileHeader.
- `Time zip_file_mod_time(const ZipFile *f)` – ModTime returns the modification time in UTC using the legacy ModifiedDate and ModifiedTime fields.
- `void zip_file_set_mod_time(ZipFile *f, Time t)` – SetModTime sets the Modified, ModifiedTime, and ModifiedDate fields to the given time in UTC.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZIP_WRITER`
- `ZipWriter *zip_new_writer(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewWriter returns a new Writer writing a zip file to w.
- `void zip_writer_set_offset(ZipWriter *w, int64_t n)` – SetOffset sets the offset of the beginning of the zip data within the underlying writer.
- `Error zip_writer_flush(ZipWriter *w)` – Flush flushes any buffered data to the underlying writer.
- `Error zip_writer_set_comment(ZipWriter *w, Str comment)` – SetComment sets the end-of-central-directory comment field.
- `void zip_writer_register_compressor(ZipWriter *w, uint16_t method, ZipCompressor comp)` – RegisterCompressor registers or overrides a custom compressor for a specific method ID.
- `IoWriter zip_writer_create(ZipWriter *w, Str name, Error *err)` – Create adds a file to the zip file using the provided name.
- `IoWriter zip_writer_create_header(ZipWriter *w, ZipFileHeader *fh, Error *err)` – CreateHeader adds a file to the zip archive using the provided FileHeader for the file metadata.
- `IoWriter zip_writer_create_raw(ZipWriter *w, ZipFileHeader *fh, Error *err)` – CreateRaw adds a file to the zip archive using the provided FileHeader and returns a Writer to which the file contents should be written.
- `Error zip_writer_copy(ZipWriter *w, ZipFile *f)` – Copy copies the file f (obtained from a Reader) into w.
- `Error zip_writer_add_fs(ZipWriter *w, Fs fsys)` – AddFS adds the files from fs.FS to the archive.
- `Error zip_writer_close(ZipWriter *w)` – Close finishes writing the zip file by writing the central directory.
- `void zip_writer_free(ZipWriter *w)` – Gives the writer and all it holds back to its allocator.

## burrow/bufio.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `bufio` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error bufio_err_invalid_unread_byte`
- `extern const Error bufio_err_invalid_unread_rune` – "bufio: invalid use of UnreadByte"
- `extern const Error bufio_err_buffer_full` – "bufio: invalid use of UnreadRune"
- `extern const Error bufio_err_negative_count` – "bufio: buffer full"
- `extern const Error bufio_err_too_long`
- `extern const Error bufio_err_negative_advance` – the token is bigger than the limit
- `extern const Error bufio_err_advance_too_far` – a split function advanced by \< 0
- `extern const Error bufio_err_bad_read_count` – ...
- `extern const Error bufio_err_final_token` – ErrFinalToken is a special sentinel error value.
- `BufioReader *bufio_new_reader_size(Alloc *a, IoReader rd, Int size)` – NewReaderSize returns a new Reader whose buffer has at least the specified size.
- `BufioReader *bufio_new_reader(Alloc *a, IoReader rd)` – NewReader returns a new Reader whose buffer has the default size.
- `void bufio_reader_free(BufioReader *b)` – Gives the reader and its buffer back to the allocator, once the last owner has called it.
- `void bufio_reader_reset(BufioReader *b, IoReader r)` – Reset discards any buffered data, resets all state, and switches the buffered reader to read from r.
- `Int bufio_reader_size(BufioReader *b)` – Size returns the size of the underlying buffer in bytes.
- `Int bufio_reader_buffered(BufioReader *b)` – Buffered returns the number of bytes that can be read from the current buffer.
- `Slice bufio_reader_peek(BufioReader *b, Int n, Error *err)` – Peek returns the next n bytes without advancing the reader.
- `Int bufio_reader_discard(BufioReader *b, Int n, Error *err)` – Discard skips the next n bytes, returning the number of bytes discarded.
- `Int bufio_reader_read(BufioReader *b, Slice p, Error *err)` – Read reads data into p.
- `Byte bufio_reader_read_byte(BufioReader *b, Error *err)` – ReadByte reads and returns a single byte.
- `Error bufio_reader_unread_byte(BufioReader *b)` – UnreadByte unreads the last byte.
- `Rune bufio_reader_read_rune(BufioReader *b, Int *size, Error *err)` – ReadRune reads a single UTF-8 encoded Unicode character and returns the rune and its size in bytes.
- `Error bufio_reader_unread_rune(BufioReader *b)` – UnreadRune unreads the last rune.
- `Slice bufio_reader_read_slice(BufioReader *b, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadSlice reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a slice pointing at the bytes in the buffer.
- `Slice bufio_reader_read_line(BufioReader *b, bool *is_prefix, Error *err)` – ReadLine is a low-level line-reading primitive.
- `Slice bufio_reader_read_bytes(BufioReader *b, Alloc *a, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadBytes reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a slice containing the data up to and including the delimiter.
- `Str bufio_reader_read_string(BufioReader *b, Alloc *a, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadString reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a string containing the data up to and including the delimiter.
- `int64_t bufio_reader_write_to(BufioReader *b, IoWriter w, Error *err)` – WriteTo implements io.WriterTo.
- `IoReader bufio_reader_as_io_reader(BufioReader *b)`
- `IoByteReader bufio_reader_as_io_byte_reader(BufioReader *b)`
- `IoByteScanner bufio_reader_as_io_byte_scanner(BufioReader *b)`
- `IoRuneReader bufio_reader_as_io_rune_reader(BufioReader *b)`
- `IoRuneScanner bufio_reader_as_io_rune_scanner(BufioReader *b)`
- `IoWriterTo bufio_reader_as_io_writer_to(BufioReader *b)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BUFIO_READER`
- `BufioWriter *bufio_new_writer_size(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, Int size)` – NewWriterSize returns a new Writer whose buffer has at least the specified size.
- `BufioWriter *bufio_new_writer(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewWriter returns a new Writer whose buffer has the default size.
- `void bufio_writer_free(BufioWriter *b)` – Frees without flushing, so flush first.
- `void bufio_writer_reset(BufioWriter *b, IoWriter w)` – Reset discards any unflushed buffered data, clears any error, and resets b to write its output to w.
- `Error bufio_writer_flush(BufioWriter *b)` – Flush writes any buffered data to the underlying io.Writer.
- `Int bufio_writer_size(BufioWriter *b)` – Size returns the size of the underlying buffer in bytes.
- `Int bufio_writer_available(BufioWriter *b)` – Available returns how many bytes are unused in the buffer.
- `Int bufio_writer_buffered(BufioWriter *b)` – Buffered returns the number of bytes that have been written into the current buffer.
- `Slice bufio_writer_available_buffer(BufioWriter *b)` – AvailableBuffer returns an empty buffer with b.Available() capacity.
- `Int bufio_writer_write(BufioWriter *b, Slice p, Error *err)` – Write writes the contents of p into the buffer.
- `Error bufio_writer_write_byte(BufioWriter *b, Byte c)` – WriteByte writes a single byte.
- `Int bufio_writer_write_rune(BufioWriter *b, Rune r, Error *err)` – WriteRune writes a single Unicode code point, returning the number of bytes written and any error.
- `Int bufio_writer_write_string(BufioWriter *b, Str s, Error *err)` – WriteString writes a string.
- `int64_t bufio_writer_read_from(BufioWriter *b, IoReader r, Error *err)` – ReadFrom implements io.ReaderFrom.
- `IoWriter bufio_writer_as_io_writer(BufioWriter *b)`
- `IoByteWriter bufio_writer_as_io_byte_writer(BufioWriter *b)`
- `IoStringWriter bufio_writer_as_io_string_writer(BufioWriter *b)`
- `IoReaderFrom bufio_writer_as_io_reader_from(BufioWriter *b)`

- `Rune bufio_read_writer_read_rune(BufioReadWriter rw, Int *size, Error *err)` – ReadRune reads a single UTF-8 encoded Unicode character and returns the rune and its size in bytes.
- `Error bufio_read_writer_unread_rune(BufioReadWriter rw)` – UnreadRune unreads the last rune.

- `Slice bufio_read_writer_read_line(BufioReadWriter rw, bool *is_prefix, Error *err)` – ReadLine is a low-level line-reading primitive.
- `Slice bufio_read_writer_read_bytes(BufioReadWriter rw, Alloc *a, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadBytes reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a slice containing the data up to and including the delimiter.

- `Error bufio_read_writer_write_byte(BufioReadWriter rw, Byte c)` – WriteByte writes a single byte.
- `Int bufio_read_writer_write_rune(BufioReadWriter rw, Rune r, Error *err)` – WriteRune writes a single Unicode code point, returning the number of bytes written and any error.
- `Int bufio_read_writer_write_string(BufioReadWriter rw, Str s, Error *err)` – WriteString writes a string.
- `int64_t bufio_read_writer_read_from(BufioReadWriter rw, IoReader r, Error *err)` – ReadFrom implements io.ReaderFrom.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BUFIO_READ_WRITER`
- `Int bufio_scan_bytes(Slice data, bool at_eof, Slice *token, Error *err)` – ScanBytes is a split function for a Scanner that returns each byte as a token.
- `Int bufio_scan_runes(Slice data, bool at_eof, Slice *token, Error *err)` – ScanRunes is a split function for a Scanner that returns each UTF-8-encoded rune as a token.
- `Int bufio_scan_lines(Slice data, bool at_eof, Slice *token, Error *err)` – ScanLines is a split function for a Scanner that returns each line of text, stripped of any trailing end-of-line marker.

- `Str bufio_scanner_text(BufioScanner *s)` – Text returns the most recent token generated by a call to Scanner.Scan as a newly allocated string holding its bytes.
- `Error bufio_scanner_err(BufioScanner *s)` – Err returns the first non-EOF error that was encountered by the Scanner.
- `void bufio_scanner_buffer(BufioScanner *s, Slice buf, Int max)` – Buffer controls memory allocation by the Scanner.

## burrow/bytes.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `bytes` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool bytes_equal(Slice a, Slice b)` – Equal reports whether a and b are the same length and contain the same bytes.
- `Int bytes_compare(Slice a, Slice b)` – Compare returns an integer comparing two byte slices lexicographically.
- `bool bytes_equal_fold(Slice s, Slice t)` – EqualFold reports whether s and t, interpreted as UTF-8 strings, are equal under simple Unicode case-folding, which is a more general form of case-insensitivity.
- `bool bytes_has_prefix(Slice s, Slice prefix)` – HasPrefix reports whether the byte slice s begins with prefix.
- `bool bytes_has_suffix(Slice s, Slice suffix)` – HasSuffix reports whether the byte slice s ends with suffix.
- `bool bytes_contains(Slice b, Slice subslice)` – Contains reports whether subslice is within b.
- `bool bytes_contains_any(Slice b, Str chars)` – ContainsAny reports whether any of the UTF-8-encoded code points in chars are within b.
- `bool bytes_contains_rune(Slice b, Rune r)` – ContainsRune reports whether the rune is contained in the UTF-8-encoded byte slice b.
- `bool bytes_contains_func(Slice b, RuneFunc f)` – ContainsFunc reports whether any of the UTF-8-encoded code points r within b satisfy f(r).
- `Int bytes_count(Slice s, Slice sep)` – Count counts the number of non-overlapping instances of sep in s.
- `Int bytes_index(Slice s, Slice sep)` – Index returns the index of the first instance of sep in s, or -1 if sep is not present in s.
- `Int bytes_index_any(Slice s, Str chars)` – IndexAny interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded Unicode code points.
- `Int bytes_index_byte(Slice b, Byte c)` – IndexByte returns the index of the first instance of c in b, or -1 if c is not present in b.
- `Int bytes_index_func(Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – IndexFunc interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `Int bytes_index_rune(Slice s, Rune r)` – IndexRune interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `Int bytes_last_index(Slice s, Slice sep)` – LastIndex returns the index of the last instance of sep in s, or -1 if sep is not present in s.
- `Int bytes_last_index_any(Slice s, Str chars)` – LastIndexAny interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded Unicode code points.
- `Int bytes_last_index_byte(Slice s, Byte c)` – LastIndexByte returns the index of the last instance of c in s, or -1 if c is not present in s.
- `Int bytes_last_index_func(Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – LastIndexFunc interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `Slice bytes_cut(Slice s, Slice sep, Slice *after, bool *found)` – Cut slices s around the first instance of sep, returning the text before and after sep.
- `Slice bytes_cut_last(Slice s, Slice sep, Slice *after, bool *found)` – CutLast slices s around the last instance of sep, returning the text before and after sep.
- `Slice bytes_cut_prefix(Slice s, Slice prefix, bool *found)` – CutPrefix returns s without the provided leading prefix byte slice and reports whether it found the prefix.
- `Slice bytes_cut_suffix(Slice s, Slice suffix, bool *found)` – CutSuffix returns s without the provided ending suffix byte slice and reports whether it found the suffix.
- `Slice bytes_trim(Slice s, Str cutset)` – Trim returns a subslice of s by slicing off all leading and trailing UTF-8-encoded code points contained in cutset.
- `Slice bytes_trim_left(Slice s, Str cutset)` – TrimLeft returns a subslice of s by slicing off all leading UTF-8-encoded code points contained in cutset.
- `Slice bytes_trim_right(Slice s, Str cutset)` – TrimRight returns a subslice of s by slicing off all trailing UTF-8-encoded code points that are contained in cutset.
- `Slice bytes_trim_func(Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimFunc returns a subslice of s by slicing off all leading and trailing UTF-8-encoded code points c that satisfy f(c).
- `Slice bytes_trim_left_func(Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimLeftFunc treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a subslice of s by slicing off all leading UTF-8-encoded code points c that satisfy f(c).
- `Slice bytes_trim_right_func(Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimRightFunc returns a subslice of s by slicing off all trailing UTF-8-encoded code points c that satisfy f(c).
- `Slice bytes_trim_space(Slice s)` – TrimSpace returns a subslice of s by slicing off all leading and trailing white space, as defined by Unicode.
- `Slice bytes_trim_prefix(Slice s, Slice prefix)` – TrimPrefix returns s without the provided leading prefix string.
- `Slice bytes_trim_suffix(Slice s, Slice suffix)` – TrimSuffix returns s without the provided trailing suffix string.
- `Slice bytes_split(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep)` – Split slices s into all subslices separated by sep and returns a slice of the subslices between those separators.
- `Slice bytes_split_n(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep, Int n)` – SplitN slices s into subslices separated by sep and returns a slice of the subslices between those separators.
- `Slice bytes_split_after(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep)` – SplitAfter slices s into all subslices after each instance of sep and returns a slice of those subslices.
- `Slice bytes_split_after_n(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep, Int n)` – SplitAfterN slices s into subslices after each instance of sep and returns a slice of those subslices.
- `Slice bytes_fields(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – Fields interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `Slice bytes_fields_func(Alloc *a, Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – FieldsFunc interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `IterSeq bytes_lines(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – Lines returns an iterator over the newline-terminated lines in the byte slice s.
- `IterSeq bytes_split_seq(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep)` – SplitSeq returns an iterator over all subslices of s separated by sep.
- `IterSeq bytes_split_after_seq(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep)` – SplitAfterSeq returns an iterator over subslices of s split after each instance of sep.
- `IterSeq bytes_fields_seq(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – FieldsSeq returns an iterator over subslices of s split around runs of whitespace characters, as defined by unicode.IsSpace.
- `IterSeq bytes_fields_func_seq(Alloc *a, Slice s, RuneFunc f)` – FieldsFuncSeq returns an iterator over subslices of s split around runs of Unicode code points satisfying f(c).
- `void bytes_seq_free(Alloc *a, IterSeq seq)` – Frees the state of a sequence from one of the functions above, which must have been made with a.
- `Slice bytes_clone(Alloc *a, Slice b)` – Clone returns a copy of b\[:len(b)\].
- `Slice bytes_join(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice sep)` – Join concatenates the elements of s to create a new byte slice.
- `Slice bytes_repeat(Alloc *a, Slice b, Int count)` – Repeat returns a new byte slice consisting of count copies of b.
- `Slice bytes_replace(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice old, Slice repl, Int n)` – Replace returns a copy of the slice s with the first n non-overlapping instances of old replaced by new.
- `Slice bytes_replace_all(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice old, Slice repl)` – ReplaceAll returns a copy of the slice s with all non-overlapping instances of old replaced by new.
- `Slice bytes_map(Alloc *a, RuneMapFunc mapping, Slice s)` – Map returns a copy of the byte slice s with all its characters modified according to the mapping function.
- `Slice bytes_runes(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – Runes interprets s as a sequence of UTF-8-encoded code points.
- `Slice bytes_to_upper(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – ToUpper returns a copy of the byte slice s with all Unicode letters mapped to their upper case.
- `Slice bytes_to_lower(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – ToLower returns a copy of the byte slice s with all Unicode letters mapped to their lower case.
- `Slice bytes_to_title(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – ToTitle treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with all the Unicode letters mapped to their title case.
- `Slice bytes_to_upper_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Slice s)` – ToUpperSpecial treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with all the Unicode letters mapped to their upper case, giving priority to the special casing rules.
- `Slice bytes_to_lower_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Slice s)` – ToLowerSpecial treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with all the Unicode letters mapped to their lower case, giving priority to the special casing rules.
- `Slice bytes_to_title_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Slice s)` – ToTitleSpecial treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with all the Unicode letters mapped to their title case, giving priority to the special casing rules.
- `Slice bytes_to_valid_utf8(Alloc *a, Slice s, Slice replacement)` – ToValidUTF8 treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with each run of bytes representing invalid UTF-8 replaced with the bytes in replacement, which may be empty.
- `Slice bytes_title(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – Title treats s as UTF-8-encoded bytes and returns a copy with all Unicode letters that begin words mapped to their title case.
- `extern const Error bytes_err_too_large` – ErrTooLarge is passed to panic if memory cannot be allocated to store data in a buffer.
- `BytesBuffer *bytes_new_buffer(Alloc *a, Slice buf)` – NewBuffer creates and initializes a new Buffer using buf as its initial contents.
- `BytesBuffer *bytes_new_buffer_string(Alloc *a, Str s)` – NewBufferString creates and initializes a new Buffer using string s as its initial contents.
- `void bytes_buffer_free(BytesBuffer *b)` – Gives back the memory the buffer allocated, and the buffer itself when it came from bytes_new_buffer.
- `Slice bytes_buffer_bytes(BytesBuffer *b)` – Bytes returns a slice of length b.Len() holding the unread portion of the buffer.
- `Str bytes_buffer_string(BytesBuffer *b, Alloc *a)` – String returns the contents of the unread portion of the buffer as a string.
- `Int bytes_buffer_len(BytesBuffer *b)` – Len returns the number of bytes of the unread portion of the buffer; b.Len() == len(b.Bytes()).
- `Int bytes_buffer_cap(BytesBuffer *b)` – Cap returns the capacity of the buffer's underlying byte slice, that is, the total space allocated for the buffer's data.
- `Int bytes_buffer_available(BytesBuffer *b)` – Available returns how many bytes are unused in the buffer.
- `Slice bytes_buffer_available_buffer(BytesBuffer *b)` – AvailableBuffer returns an empty buffer with b.Available() capacity.
- `bool bytes_buffer_grow(BytesBuffer *b, Int n)` – Grow grows the buffer's capacity, if necessary, to guarantee space for another n bytes.
- `void bytes_buffer_truncate(BytesBuffer *b, Int n)` – Truncate discards all but the first n unread bytes from the buffer but continues to use the same allocated storage.
- `void bytes_buffer_reset(BytesBuffer *b)` – Reset resets the buffer to be empty, but it retains the underlying storage for use by future writes.
- `Int bytes_buffer_write(BytesBuffer *b, Slice p, Error *err)` – Write appends the contents of p to the buffer, growing the buffer as needed.
- `Int bytes_buffer_write_string(BytesBuffer *b, Str s, Error *err)` – WriteString appends the contents of s to the buffer, growing the buffer as needed.
- `Error bytes_buffer_write_byte(BytesBuffer *b, Byte c)` – WriteByte appends the byte c to the buffer, growing the buffer as needed.
- `Int bytes_buffer_write_rune(BytesBuffer *b, Rune r, Error *err)` – WriteRune appends the UTF-8 encoding of Unicode code point r to the buffer, returning the number of bytes written and a nil error.
- `int64_t bytes_buffer_read_from(BytesBuffer *b, IoReader r, Error *err)` – ReadFrom reads data from r until EOF and appends it to the buffer, growing the buffer as needed.
- `int64_t bytes_buffer_write_to(BytesBuffer *b, IoWriter w, Error *err)` – WriteTo writes data to w until the buffer is drained or an error occurs.
- `Int bytes_buffer_read(BytesBuffer *b, Slice p, Error *err)` – Read reads the next len(p) bytes from the buffer or until the buffer is drained.
- `Slice bytes_buffer_peek(BytesBuffer *b, Int n, Error *err)` – Peek returns the next n bytes without advancing the buffer.
- `Slice bytes_buffer_next(BytesBuffer *b, Int n)` – Next returns a slice containing the next n bytes from the buffer, advancing the buffer as if the bytes had been returned by Buffer.Read.
- `Byte bytes_buffer_read_byte(BytesBuffer *b, Error *err)` – ReadByte reads and returns the next byte from the buffer.
- `Rune bytes_buffer_read_rune(BytesBuffer *b, Int *size, Error *err)` – ReadRune reads and returns the next UTF-8-encoded Unicode code point from the buffer.
- `Error bytes_buffer_unread_byte(BytesBuffer *b)` – UnreadByte unreads the last byte returned by the most recent successful read operation that read at least one byte.
- `Error bytes_buffer_unread_rune(BytesBuffer *b)` – UnreadRune unreads the last rune returned by Buffer.ReadRune.
- `Slice bytes_buffer_read_bytes(BytesBuffer *b, Alloc *a, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadBytes reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a slice containing the data up to and including the delimiter.
- `Str bytes_buffer_read_string(BytesBuffer *b, Alloc *a, Byte delim, Error *err)` – ReadString reads until the first occurrence of delim in the input, returning a string containing the data up to and including the delimiter.
- `IoReader bytes_buffer_as_io_reader(BytesBuffer *b)`
- `IoWriter bytes_buffer_as_io_writer(BytesBuffer *b)`
- `IoByteReader bytes_buffer_as_io_byte_reader(BytesBuffer *r)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BYTES_BUFFER`
- `BytesReader *bytes_new_reader(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – NewReader returns a new Reader reading from b.
- `void bytes_reader_reset(BytesReader *r, Slice s)` – Reset resets the Reader to be reading from b.
- `Int bytes_reader_len(BytesReader *r)` – Len returns the number of bytes of the unread portion of the slice.
- `int64_t bytes_reader_size(BytesReader *r)` – Size returns the original length of the underlying byte slice.
- `Int bytes_reader_read(BytesReader *r, Slice b, Error *err)` – Read implements the io.Reader interface.
- `Int bytes_reader_read_at(BytesReader *r, Slice b, int64_t off, Error *err)` – ReadAt implements the io.ReaderAt interface.
- `Byte bytes_reader_read_byte(BytesReader *r, Error *err)` – ReadByte implements the io.ByteReader interface.
- `Error bytes_reader_unread_byte(BytesReader *r)` – UnreadByte complements Reader.ReadByte in implementing the io.ByteScanner interface.
- `Rune bytes_reader_read_rune(BytesReader *r, Int *size, Error *err)` – ReadRune implements the io.RuneReader interface.
- `Error bytes_reader_unread_rune(BytesReader *r)` – UnreadRune complements Reader.ReadRune in implementing the io.RuneScanner interface.
- `int64_t bytes_reader_seek(BytesReader *r, int64_t offset, Int whence, Error *err)` – Seek implements the io.Seeker interface.
- `int64_t bytes_reader_write_to(BytesReader *r, IoWriter w, Error *err)` – WriteTo implements the io.WriterTo interface.
- `IoReader bytes_reader_as_io_reader(BytesReader *r)`
- `IoSeeker bytes_reader_as_io_seeker(BytesReader *r)`
- `IoByteReader bytes_reader_as_io_byte_reader(BytesReader *r)`
- `IoRuneReader bytes_reader_as_io_rune_reader(BytesReader *r)`
- `IoRuneScanner bytes_reader_as_io_rune_scanner(BytesReader *r)`
- `IoReaderAt bytes_reader_as_io_reader_at(BytesReader *r)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BYTES_READER`

## burrow/chan.h

- `Chan *chan_make(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, Int cap)` – make(chan T, cap).

- `bool chan_recv(Chan *c, void *out)` – v, ok := \<-c.

- `Int chan_len(const Chan *c)` – len(c) and cap(c).
- `Int chan_cap(const Chan *c)`
- `const Type *chan_elem(const Chan *c)` – What the channel carries, for code that was handed one and has to ask.
- `Int chan_select(SelectCase *cases, Int n)` – Runs exactly one of the cases and answers which one, as an index into \`cases\`.

## burrow/compress/gzip.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `compress/gzip` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error gzip_err_checksum` – ErrChecksum is returned when reading GZIP data that has an invalid checksum.
- `extern const Error gzip_err_header` – ErrHeader is returned when reading GZIP data that has an invalid header.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_GZIP_READER`
- `GzipReader *gzip_new_reader(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Error *err)` – NewReader creates a new Reader reading the given reader.
- `Error gzip_reader_reset(GzipReader *z, IoReader r)` – Reset discards the Reader z's state and makes it equivalent to the result of its original state from NewReader, but reading from r instead.
- `void gzip_reader_multistream(GzipReader *z, bool ok)` – Multistream controls whether the reader supports multistream files.
- `Int gzip_reader_read(GzipReader *z, Slice p, Error *err)` – Read implements io.Reader, reading uncompressed bytes from its underlying reader.
- `Error gzip_reader_close(GzipReader *z)` – Close closes the Reader.
- `IoReader gzip_reader_as_io_reader(GzipReader *z)` – The reader as an IoReader and an IoReadCloser, borrowing z.
- `IoReadCloser gzip_reader_as_io_read_closer(GzipReader *z)`
- `void gzip_reader_free(GzipReader *z)` – Gives the reader, its header and everything else it holds back to its allocator.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_GZIP_WRITER`
- `GzipWriter *gzip_new_writer(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewWriter returns a new Writer.
- `GzipWriter *gzip_new_writer_level(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, Int level, Error *err)` – NewWriterLevel is like NewWriter but specifies the compression level instead of assuming DefaultCompression.
- `void gzip_writer_reset(GzipWriter *z, IoWriter w)` – Reset discards the Writer z's state and makes it equivalent to the result of its original state from NewWriter or NewWriterLevel, but writing to w instead.
- `Int gzip_writer_write(GzipWriter *z, Slice data, Error *err)` – Write writes a compressed form of p to the underlying io.Writer.
- `Error gzip_writer_flush(GzipWriter *z)` – Flush flushes any pending compressed data to the underlying writer.
- `Error gzip_writer_close(GzipWriter *z)` – Close closes the Writer by flushing any unwritten data to the underlying io.Writer and writing the GZIP footer.
- `IoWriter gzip_writer_as_io_writer(GzipWriter *z)` – The writer as an IoWriter and an IoWriteCloser, borrowing z.
- `IoWriteCloser gzip_writer_as_io_write_closer(GzipWriter *z)`
- `void gzip_writer_free(GzipWriter *z)` – Gives the writer and its compressor back to its allocator.

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `compress/zlib` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error zlib_err_checksum` – ErrChecksum is returned when reading ZLIB data that has an invalid checksum.

- `extern const Error zlib_err_header` – ErrHeader is returned when reading ZLIB data that has an invalid header.
- `Error zlib_resetter_reset(ZlibResetter rs, IoReader r, Slice dict)` – Reset discards any buffered data and resets the Resetter as if it was newly initialized with the given reader.
- `IoReadCloser zlib_new_reader(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Error *err)` – NewReader creates a new ReadCloser.
- `IoReadCloser zlib_new_reader_dict(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Slice dict, Error *err)` – NewReaderDict is like NewReader but uses a preset dictionary.
- `ZlibResetter zlib_reader_as_resetter(IoReadCloser rc)` – The Resetter a reader from zlib_new_reader has.
- `void zlib_reader_free(IoReadCloser rc)` – Gives a reader from zlib_new_reader and everything it holds back to its allocator.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ZLIB_WRITER`
- `ZlibWriter *zlib_new_writer(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewWriter creates a new Writer.
- `ZlibWriter *zlib_new_writer_level(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, Int level, Error *err)` – NewWriterLevel is like NewWriter but specifies the compression level instead of assuming DefaultCompression.
- `ZlibWriter *zlib_new_writer_level_dict(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, Int level, Slice dict, Error *err)` – NewWriterLevelDict is like NewWriterLevel but specifies a dictionary to compress with.
- `Int zlib_writer_write(ZlibWriter *z, Slice data, Error *err)` – Write writes a compressed form of p to the underlying io.Writer.
- `Error zlib_writer_flush(ZlibWriter *z)` – Flush flushes the Writer to its underlying io.Writer.

- `List *list_new(Alloc *a)` – New returns an initialized list.
- `List *list_init(List *l)` – Init initializes or clears list l.
- `Int list_len(const List *l)` – Len returns the number of elements of list l.
- `ListElement *list_front(const List *l)` – Front returns the first element of list l or nil if the list is empty.
- `ListElement *list_back(const List *l)` – Back returns the last element of list l or nil if the list is empty.
- `ListElement *list_element_next(const ListElement *e)` – Next returns the next list element or nil.
- `ListElement *list_element_prev(const ListElement *e)` – Prev returns the previous list element or nil.
- `Any list_remove(List *l, ListElement *e)` – Remove removes e from l if e is an element of list l.
- `ListElement *list_push_front(List *l, Any v)` – PushFront inserts a new element e with value v at the front of list l and returns e.
- `ListElement *list_push_back(List *l, Any v)` – PushBack inserts a new element e with value v at the back of list l and returns e.
- `ListElement *list_insert_before(List *l, Any v, ListElement *mark)` – InsertBefore inserts a new element e with value v immediately before mark and returns e.
- `ListElement *list_insert_after(List *l, Any v, ListElement *mark)` – InsertAfter inserts a new element e with value v immediately after mark and returns e.
- `void list_move_to_front(List *l, ListElement *e)` – MoveToFront moves element e to the front of list l.
- `void list_move_to_back(List *l, ListElement *e)` – MoveToBack moves element e to the back of list l.
- `void list_move_before(List *l, ListElement *e, ListElement *mark)` – MoveBefore moves element e to its new position before mark.
- `void list_move_after(List *l, ListElement *e, ListElement *mark)` – MoveAfter moves element e to its new position after mark.
- `bool list_push_back_list(List *l, const List *other)` – PushBackList inserts a copy of another list at the back of list l.
- `bool list_push_front_list(List *l, const List *other)` – PushFrontList inserts a copy of another list at the front of list l.
- `void list_free_element(List *l, ListElement *e)` – Gives e back to the allocator of list l, taking it out of l first if it is still in it.
- `void list_free(List *l)` – Gives every element still in l back and leaves l empty and usable.

## burrow/container/ring.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `container/ring` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Ring *ring_new(Alloc *a, Int n)` – New creates a ring of n elements.
- `Ring *ring_next(Ring *r)` – Next returns the next ring element.
- `Ring *ring_prev(Ring *r)` – Prev returns the previous ring element.
- `Ring *ring_move(Ring *r, Int n)` – Move moves n % r.Len() elements backward (n \< 0) or forward (n \>= 0) in the ring and returns that ring element.
- `Ring *ring_link(Ring *r, Ring *s)` – Link connects ring r with ring s such that r.Next() becomes s and returns the original value for r.Next().
- `Ring *ring_unlink(Ring *r, Int n)` – Unlink removes n % r.Len() elements from the ring r, starting at r.Next().
- `Int ring_len(const Ring *r)` – Len computes the number of elements in ring r.
- `void ring_do(Ring *r, RingDoFunc f)` – Do calls function f on each element of the ring, in forward order.
- `void ring_free(Alloc *a, Ring *r)` – Gives every element of the ring r back to a, which must be the allocator all of them came from.

## burrow/context.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `context` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_CONTEXT` – The descriptor for Go's context.Context, so that an Any can hold one and a struct with a context field in it can be described.
- `extern const Error context_canceled` – Canceled is the error returned by Context.Err when the context is canceled for some reason other than its deadline passing.
- `extern const Error context_deadline_exceeded` – DeadlineExceeded is the error returned by Context.Err when the context is canceled due to its deadline passing.
- `Context context_background(void)` – Background returns a non-nil, empty Context.
- `Context context_todo(void)` – TODO returns a non-nil, empty Context.
- `bool context_deadline(Context c, Time *deadline)` – Deadline returns the time when work done on behalf of this context should be canceled.
- `Chan *context_done(Context c)` – Done returns a channel that's closed when work done on behalf of this context should be canceled.
- `Error context_err(Context c)` – If Done is not yet closed, Err returns nil.
- `Any context_value(Context c, Any key)` – Value returns the value associated with this context for key, or nil if no value is associated with key.
- `Context context_with_cancel(Alloc *a, Context parent, ContextCancelFunc *cancel)` – WithCancel returns a derived context that points to the parent context but has a new Done channel.
- `Context context_with_cancel_cause(Alloc *a, Context parent, ContextCancelCauseFunc *cancel)` – WithCancelCause behaves like WithCancel but returns a CancelCauseFunc instead of a CancelFunc.
- `Error context_cause(Context c)` – Cause returns a non-nil error explaining why c was canceled.
- `Context context_without_cancel(Alloc *a, Context parent)` – WithoutCancel returns a derived context that points to the parent context and is not canceled when parent is canceled.
- `Context context_after_func(Alloc *a, Context parent, Func f, StopFunc *stop)` – AfterFunc arranges to call f in its own goroutine after ctx is canceled.
- `Context context_with_deadline(Alloc *a, Context parent, Time d, ContextCancelFunc *cancel)` – WithDeadline returns a derived context that points to the parent context but has the deadline adjusted to be no later than d.
- `Context context_with_timeout(Alloc *a, Context parent, Duration timeout, ContextCancelFunc *cancel)` – WithTimeout returns WithDeadline(parent, time.Now().Add(timeout)).
- `Context context_with_deadline_cause(Alloc *a, Context parent, Time d, Error cause, ContextCancelFunc *cancel)` – WithDeadlineCause behaves like WithDeadline but also sets the cause of the returned Context when the deadline is exceeded.
- `Context context_with_timeout_cause(Alloc *a, Context parent, Duration timeout, Error cause, ContextCancelFunc *cancel)` – WithTimeoutCause behaves like WithTimeout but also sets the cause of the returned Context when the timeout expires.
- `Context context_with_value(Alloc *a, Context parent, Any key, Any val)` – WithValue returns a derived context that points to the parent Context.
- `void context_release(Context c)` – Gives a context made by one of the With functions back to its allocator, and leaves the Context dangling, so it is the last thing done with one.

## burrow/core.h

- `Str str_from_cstr(const char *s)` – O(n) in the length of s and it does not allocate.
- `Str str_from_bytes(const void *p, Int n)` – Also borrows.
- `char *str_to_cstr(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Allocates, copies, and appends the NUL.
- `bool str_has_nul(Str s)`
- `int str_cmp(Str a, Str b)` – Byte order, which is what Go's \< and \> on strings do and what sort.Strings uses.
- `bool str_eq(Str a, Str b)`
- `Str str_clone(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Fresh memory holding the same bytes, so that the copy outlives whatever the original pointed into.
- `bool str_is_empty(Str s)` – Here so that nobody writes s.p == NULL, which is the wrong question.
- `Byte str_at(Str s, Int i)` – Go's s\[i\], including what Go does when i is out of range, which is stop.
- `StrIter str_runes(Str s)`
- `bool str_next_rune(StrIter *it, Int *index, Rune *r)`

## burrow/crypto/cipher.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `crypto/cipher` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Int cipher_block_block_size(CipherBlock b)` – BlockSize returns the cipher's block size.
- `void cipher_block_encrypt(CipherBlock b, Slice dst, Slice src)` – Encrypt encrypts the first block in src into dst.
- `void cipher_block_decrypt(CipherBlock b, Slice dst, Slice src)` – Decrypt decrypts the first block in src into dst.
- `void cipher_stream_xor_key_stream(CipherStream s, Slice dst, Slice src)` – XORKeyStream XORs each byte in the given slice with a byte from the cipher's key stream.
- `Int cipher_block_mode_block_size(CipherBlockMode m)` – BlockSize returns the mode's block size.
- `void cipher_block_mode_crypt_blocks(CipherBlockMode m, Slice dst, Slice src)` – CryptBlocks encrypts or decrypts a number of blocks.
- `Int cipher_aead_nonce_size(CipherAEAD x)` – NonceSize returns the size of the nonce that must be passed to Seal and Open.
- `Int cipher_aead_overhead(CipherAEAD x)` – Overhead returns the maximum difference between the lengths of a plaintext and its ciphertext.
- `Slice cipher_aead_seal(CipherAEAD x, Alloc *a, Slice dst, Slice nonce, Slice plaintext, Slice additional_data)` – Seal encrypts and authenticates plaintext, authenticates the additional data and appends the result to dst, returning the updated slice.
- `Slice cipher_aead_open(CipherAEAD x, Alloc *a, Slice dst, Slice nonce, Slice ciphertext, Slice additional_data, Error *err)` – Open decrypts and authenticates ciphertext, authenticates the additional data and, if successful, appends the resulting plaintext to dst, returning the updated slice.
- `CipherBlockMode cipher_new_cbc_encrypter(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewCBCEncrypter returns a BlockMode which encrypts in cipher block chaining mode, using the given Block.
- `CipherBlockMode cipher_new_cbc_decrypter(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewCBCDecrypter returns a BlockMode which decrypts in cipher block chaining mode, using the given Block.
- `void cipher_cbc_set_iv(CipherBlockMode m, Slice iv)` – The SetIV method Go's CBC modes have outside the BlockMode interface, which starts m again from iv without making a new one.
- `CipherStream cipher_new_ctr(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewCTR returns a Stream which encrypts/decrypts using the given Block in counter mode.
- `CipherStream cipher_new_ofb(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewOFB returns a Stream that encrypts or decrypts using the block cipher b in output feedback mode.
- `CipherStream cipher_new_cfb_encrypter(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewCFBEncrypter returns a Stream which encrypts with cipher feedback mode, using the given Block.
- `CipherStream cipher_new_cfb_decrypter(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Slice iv)` – NewCFBDecrypter returns a Stream which decrypts with cipher feedback mode, using the given Block.
- `CipherAEAD cipher_new_gcm(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Error *err)` – NewGCM returns the given 128-bit, block cipher wrapped in Galois Counter Mode with the standard nonce length.
- `CipherAEAD cipher_new_gcm_with_nonce_size(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Int size, Error *err)` – NewGCMWithNonceSize returns the given 128-bit, block cipher wrapped in Galois Counter Mode, which accepts nonces of the given length.
- `CipherAEAD cipher_new_gcm_with_tag_size(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Int tag_size, Error *err)` – NewGCMWithTagSize returns the given 128-bit, block cipher wrapped in Galois Counter Mode, which generates tags with the given length.
- `CipherAEAD cipher_new_gcm_with_random_nonce(Alloc *a, CipherBlock b, Error *err)` – NewGCMWithRandomNonce returns the given cipher wrapped in Galois Counter Mode, with randomly-generated nonces.
- `Int cipher_stream_reader_read(CipherStreamReader r, Slice dst, Error *err)` – StreamReader.Read: reads into dst from r and XORs what came back with s.
- `IoReader cipher_stream_reader_as_io_reader(CipherStreamReader *r)` – r as an io.Reader.
- `Int cipher_stream_writer_write(CipherStreamWriter w, Slice src, Error *err)` – StreamWriter.Write: src through s and then to w in one write.
- `Error cipher_stream_writer_close(CipherStreamWriter w)` – Close closes the underlying Writer and returns its Close return value, if the Writer is also an io.Closer.
- `IoWriter cipher_stream_writer_as_io_writer(CipherStreamWriter *w)` – w as an io.Writer.

- `const EcdhCurve *ecdh_p256(void)` – P256 returns a Curve which implements NIST P-256 (FIPS 186-3, section D.2.3), also known as secp256r1 or prime256v1.
- `const EcdhCurve *ecdh_p384(void)` – P384 returns a Curve which implements NIST P-384 (FIPS 186-3, section D.2.4), also known as secp384r1.
- `const EcdhCurve *ecdh_p521(void)` – P521 returns a Curve which implements NIST P-521 (FIPS 186-3, section D.2.5), also known as secp521r1.
- `const EcdhCurve *ecdh_x25519(void)` – X25519 returns a Curve which implements the X25519 function over Curve25519 (RFC 7748, Section 5).

- `EcdhPrivateKey *ecdh_curve_new_private_key(const EcdhCurve *c, Alloc *a, Slice key, Error *err)` – NewPrivateKey checks that key is valid and returns a PrivateKey.
- `EcdhPublicKey *ecdh_curve_new_public_key(const EcdhCurve *c, Alloc *a, Slice key, Error *err)` – NewPublicKey checks that key is valid and returns a PublicKey.

- `Slice ecdh_private_key_ecdh(const EcdhPrivateKey *k, Alloc *a, const EcdhPublicKey *remote, Error *err)` – ECDH performs an ECDH exchange and returns the shared secret.
- `Slice ecdh_private_key_bytes(const EcdhPrivateKey *k, Alloc *a)` – Bytes returns a copy of the encoding of the private key.
- `const EcdhCurve *ecdh_private_key_curve(const EcdhPrivateKey *k)` – PrivateKey.Curve.
- `bool ecdh_private_key_equal(const EcdhPrivateKey *k, CryptoPrivateKey x)` – Equal returns whether x represents the same private key as k.
- `const EcdhPublicKey *ecdh_private_key_public_key(const EcdhPrivateKey *k)` – PrivateKey.PublicKey: the public key of k, which is part of k.
- `CryptoPublicKey ecdh_private_key_public(const EcdhPrivateKey *k)` – Public implements the implicit interface of all standard library private keys.

- `EllipticCurve elliptic_p256(void)` – P256 returns a Curve which implements NIST P-256 (FIPS 186-3, section D.2.3), also known as secp256r1 or prime256v1.

- `EllipticCurve elliptic_p521(void)` – P521 returns a Curve which implements NIST P-521 (FIPS 186-3, section D.2.5), also known as secp521r1.

- `Slice elliptic_marshal_compressed(Alloc *a, EllipticCurve curve, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – MarshalCompressed converts a point on the curve into the compressed form specified in SEC 1, Version 2.0, Section 2.3.3.

- `BigInt *elliptic_curve_params_double(EllipticCurveParams *curve, Alloc *a, const BigInt *x1, const BigInt *y1, BigInt **y)` – Double implements Curve.Double.
- `BigInt *elliptic_curve_params_scalar_mult(EllipticCurveParams *curve, Alloc *a, const BigInt *bx, const BigInt *by, Slice k, BigInt **y)` – ScalarMult implements Curve.ScalarMult.

- `NetConn tls_conn_as_net_conn(TlsConn *c)` – Not in Go: c as a net.Conn, which is what Go gets by using the \*Conn as one.
- `TlsConn *tls_conn_from_net_conn(NetConn c)` – Not in Go: the Conn in c when c is one tls_conn_as_net_conn made, which is what Go's c.(\*tls.Conn) gives, and NULL for any other connection.
- `void tls_conn_free(TlsConn *c)` – Not in Go: gives c back to the allocator it came from, with everything it kept.
- `TlsQUICConn *tls_quic_client(Alloc *a, const TlsQUICConfig *config)` – QUICClient returns a new TLS client side connection using QUICTransport as the underlying transport.
- `TlsQUICConn *tls_quic_server(Alloc *a, const TlsQUICConfig *config)` – QUICServer returns a new TLS server side connection using QUICTransport as the underlying transport.
- `Error tls_quic_conn_start(TlsQUICConn *q, Context ctx)` – Start starts the client or server handshake protocol.
- `TlsQUICEvent tls_quic_conn_next_event(TlsQUICConn *q)` – NextEvent returns the next event occurring on the connection.
- `Error tls_quic_conn_close(TlsQUICConn *q)` – Close closes the connection and stops any in-progress handshake.
- `Error tls_quic_conn_handle_data(TlsQUICConn *q, TlsQUICEncryptionLevel level, Slice data)` – HandleData handles handshake bytes received from the peer.
- `Error tls_quic_conn_send_session_ticket(TlsQUICConn *q, TlsQUICSessionTicketOptions opts)` – SendSessionTicket sends a session ticket to the client.
- `Error tls_quic_conn_store_session(TlsQUICConn *q, TlsSessionState *session)` – StoreSession stores a session previously received in a QUICStoreSession event in the ClientSessionCache.
- `TlsConnectionState tls_quic_conn_connection_state(TlsQUICConn *q)` – ConnectionState returns basic TLS details about the connection.
- `void tls_quic_conn_set_transport_parameters(TlsQUICConn *q, Slice params)` – SetTransportParameters sets the transport parameters to send to the peer.
- `void tls_quic_conn_free(TlsQUICConn *q)` – Not in Go: closes q, waits for its handshake to be over, and gives it back to the allocator it came from with everything it kept.

## burrow/crypto/x509.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `crypto/x509` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str x509_signature_algorithm_string(X509SignatureAlgorithm algo, Alloc *a)` – SignatureAlgorithm.String: "SHA256-RSA" and so on, or the number in a for one that is not in the list.
- `Str x509_public_key_algorithm_string(X509PublicKeyAlgorithm algo, Alloc *a)` – PublicKeyAlgorithm.String: "RSA", "DSA", "ECDSA", "Ed25519" or "ML-DSA", or the number in a for anything else.
- `Str x509_key_usage_string(X509KeyUsage i, Alloc *a)` – KeyUsage.String: the name RFC 5280 gives a single bit, such as "digitalSignature" or "cRLSign", and "KeyUsage(3)" from a for anything that is not exactly one of them.
- `Str x509_ext_key_usage_string(X509ExtKeyUsage i, Alloc *a)` – ExtKeyUsage.String: the short name, such as "serverAuth" or "msSGC", and "ExtKeyUsage(14)" from a for a number that is not one.
- `X509OID x509_parse_oid(Alloc *a, Str oid, Error *err)` – ParseOID parses a Object Identifier string, represented by ASCII numbers separated by dots.
- `X509OID x509_oid_from_ints(Alloc *a, Slice oid, Error *err)` – OIDFromInts creates a new OID using ints, each integer is a separate component.
- `X509OID x509_oid_from_asn1_oid(Alloc *a, Asn1ObjectIdentifier asn1_oid, Error *err)` – OIDFromASN1OID creates a new OID using asn1OID.
- `bool x509_oid_equal(X509OID oid, X509OID other)` – Equal returns true when oid and other represents the same Object Identifier.
- `bool x509_oid_equal_asn1_oid(X509OID oid, Asn1ObjectIdentifier other)` – EqualASN1OID returns whether an OID equals an asn1.ObjectIdentifier.
- `Str x509_oid_string(X509OID oid, Alloc *a)` – String returns the string representation of the Object Identifier.
- `Slice x509_oid_append_text(X509OID o, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements encoding.TextAppender
- `Slice x509_oid_marshal_text(X509OID o, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements encoding.TextMarshaler
- `Error x509_oid_unmarshal_text(X509OID *o, Alloc *a, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements encoding.TextUnmarshaler
- `Slice x509_oid_append_binary(X509OID o, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendBinary implements encoding.BinaryAppender
- `Slice x509_oid_marshal_binary(X509OID o, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalBinary implements encoding.BinaryMarshaler
- `Error x509_oid_unmarshal_binary(X509OID *o, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – UnmarshalBinary implements encoding.BinaryUnmarshaler
- `X509OID x509_ext_key_usage_oid(X509ExtKeyUsage eku, Alloc *a)` – OID returns the ASN.1 object identifier of the EKU.
- `RsaPrivateKey *x509_parse_pkcs1_private_key(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParsePKCS1PrivateKey parses an RSA private key in PKCS #1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Slice x509_marshal_pkcs1_private_key(Alloc *a, RsaPrivateKey *key)` – MarshalPKCS1PrivateKey converts an RSA private key to PKCS #1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `RsaPublicKey *x509_parse_pkcs1_public_key(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParsePKCS1PublicKey parses an RSA public key in PKCS #1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Slice x509_marshal_pkcs1_public_key(Alloc *a, const RsaPublicKey *key)` – MarshalPKCS1PublicKey converts an RSA public key to PKCS #1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `EcdsaPrivateKey *x509_parse_ec_private_key(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseECPrivateKey parses an EC private key in SEC 1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Slice x509_marshal_ec_private_key(Alloc *a, const EcdsaPrivateKey *key, Error *err)` – MarshalECPrivateKey converts an EC private key to SEC 1, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Any x509_parse_pkcs8_private_key(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParsePKCS8PrivateKey parses an unencrypted private key in PKCS #8, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Slice x509_marshal_pkcs8_private_key(Alloc *a, Any key, Error *err)` – MarshalPKCS8PrivateKey converts a private key to PKCS #8, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Any x509_parse_pkix_public_key(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParsePKIXPublicKey parses a public key in PKIX, ASN.1 DER form.
- `Slice x509_marshal_pkix_public_key(Alloc *a, Any pub, Error *err)` – MarshalPKIXPublicKey converts a public key to PKIX, ASN.1 DER form.
- `X509Certificate *x509_parse_certificate(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseCertificate parses a single certificate from the given ASN.1 DER data.
- `Slice x509_parse_certificates(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseCertificates parses one or more certificates from the given ASN.1 DER data.
- `bool x509_certificate_equal(const X509Certificate *c, const X509Certificate *other)` – Certificate.Equal: whether c and other have the same raw bytes.
- `Error x509_certificate_check_signature_from(const X509Certificate *c, const X509Certificate *parent)` – CheckSignatureFrom verifies that the signature on c is a valid signature from parent.
- `Error x509_certificate_check_signature(const X509Certificate *c, X509SignatureAlgorithm algo, Slice signed_data, Slice signature)` – CheckSignature verifies that signature is a valid signature over signed from c's public key.
- `Error x509_certificate_check_crl_signature(const X509Certificate *c, const PkixCertificateList *crl)` – CheckCRLSignature checks that the signature in crl is from c.
- `extern const Error x509_err_unsupported_algorithm` – ErrUnsupportedAlgorithm results from attempting to perform an operation that involves algorithms that are not currently implemented.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_INSECURE_ALGORITHM_ERROR`
- `Str x509_insecure_algorithm_error_error(X509InsecureAlgorithmError e, Alloc *a)` – InsecureAlgorithmError.Error: "x509: cannot verify signature: insecure algorithm SHA1-RSA" and so on, from a.
- `Error x509_insecure_algorithm_error_as_error(X509InsecureAlgorithmError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, from a.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_CONSTRAINT_VIOLATION_ERROR`
- `extern const Error x509_constraint_violation_error`
- `Str x509_constraint_violation_error_error(X509ConstraintViolationError e)` – ConstraintViolationError.Error: "x509: invalid signature: parent certificate cannot sign this kind of certificate".
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_UNHANDLED_CRITICAL_EXTENSION`
- `extern const Error x509_unhandled_critical_extension`
- `Str x509_unhandled_critical_extension_error(X509UnhandledCriticalExtension e)` – UnhandledCriticalExtension.Error: "x509: unhandled critical extension".
- `X509CertificateRequest *x509_parse_certificate_request(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseCertificateRequest parses a single certificate request from the given ASN.1 DER data.
- `Error x509_certificate_request_check_signature(const X509CertificateRequest *c)` – CheckSignature reports whether the signature on c is valid.
- `X509RevocationList *x509_parse_revocation_list(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseRevocationList parses a X509 v2 Certificate Revocation List from the given ASN.1 DER data.
- `Error x509_revocation_list_check_signature_from(const X509RevocationList *rl, const X509Certificate *parent)` – CheckSignatureFrom verifies that the signature on rl is a valid signature from issuer.
- `PkixCertificateList *x509_parse_crl(Alloc *a, Slice crl_bytes, Error *err)` – ParseCRL parses a CRL from the given bytes.
- `PkixCertificateList *x509_parse_dercrl(Alloc *a, Slice der, Error *err)` – ParseDERCRL parses a DER encoded CRL from the given bytes.
- `Slice x509_create_certificate(Alloc *a, IoReader rand, const X509Certificate *template_, const X509Certificate *parent, Any pub, CryptoSigner priv, Error *err)` – CreateCertificate creates a new X.509 v3 certificate based on a template.
- `Slice x509_create_certificate_request(Alloc *a, IoReader rand, const X509CertificateRequest *template_, CryptoSigner priv, Error *err)` – CreateCertificateRequest creates a new certificate request based on a template.
- `Slice x509_create_revocation_list(Alloc *a, IoReader rand, const X509RevocationList *template_, const X509Certificate *issuer, CryptoSigner priv, Error *err)` – CreateRevocationList creates a new X.509 v2 Certificate Revocation List, according to RFC 5280, based on template.
- `Slice x509_certificate_create_crl(const X509Certificate *c, Alloc *a, IoReader rand, CryptoSigner priv, Slice revoked_certs, Time now, Time expiry, Error *err)` – CreateCRL returns a DER encoded CRL, signed by this Certificate, that contains the given list of revoked certificates.
- `extern const Error x509_incorrect_password_error` – IncorrectPasswordError is returned when an incorrect password is detected.
- `bool x509_is_encrypted_pem_block(const PemBlock *b)` – IsEncryptedPEMBlock returns whether the PEM block is password encrypted according to RFC 1423.
- `Slice x509_decrypt_pem_block(Alloc *a, const PemBlock *b, Slice password, Error *err)` – DecryptPEMBlock takes a PEM block encrypted according to RFC 1423 and the password used to encrypt it and returns a slice of decrypted DER encoded bytes.
- `PemBlock *x509_encrypt_pem_block(Alloc *a, IoReader rand, Str block_type, Slice data, Slice password, X509PEMCipher alg, Error *err)` – EncryptPEMBlock returns a PEM block of the specified type holding the given DER encoded data encrypted with the specified algorithm and password according to RFC 1423.
- `X509CertPool *x509_new_cert_pool(Alloc *a)` – NewCertPool returns a new, empty CertPool.
- `void x509_cert_pool_free(X509CertPool *s)` – Gives back the memory of s and the certificates it parsed.
- `X509CertPool *x509_cert_pool_clone(const X509CertPool *s, Alloc *a)` – Clone returns a copy of s.
- `void x509_cert_pool_add_cert(X509CertPool *s, const X509Certificate *cert)` – AddCert adds a certificate to a pool.
- `void x509_cert_pool_add_cert_with_constraint(X509CertPool *s, const X509Certificate *cert, X509CertConstraint constraint)` – AddCertWithConstraint adds a certificate to the pool with the additional constraint.
- `bool x509_cert_pool_append_certs_from_pem(X509CertPool *s, Slice pem_certs)` – AppendCertsFromPEM attempts to parse a series of PEM encoded certificates.
- `Slice x509_cert_pool_subjects(const X509CertPool *s, Alloc *a)` – Subjects returns a list of the DER-encoded subjects of all of the certificates in the pool.
- `bool x509_cert_pool_equal(const X509CertPool *s, const X509CertPool *other)` – Equal reports whether s and other are equal.
- `X509CertPool *x509_system_cert_pool(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – SystemCertPool returns a copy of the system cert pool.
- `void x509_set_fallback_roots(X509CertPool *roots)` – SetFallbackRoots sets the roots to use during certificate verification, if no custom roots are specified and a platform verifier or a system certificate pool is not available (for instance in a container which does not have a root certificate bundle).
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_CERTIFICATE_INVALID_ERROR`
- `Str x509_certificate_invalid_error_error(X509CertificateInvalidError e, Alloc *a)` – CertificateInvalidError.Error, such as "x509: certificate has expired or is not yet valid: " and the detail, from a.
- `Error x509_certificate_invalid_error_as_error(X509CertificateInvalidError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, from a, with its own copy of the detail.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_HOSTNAME_ERROR`
- `Str x509_hostname_error_error(X509HostnameError h, Alloc *a)` – HostnameError.Error, such as "x509: certificate is valid for example.com, not example.org", from a.
- `Error x509_hostname_error_as_error(X509HostnameError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, from a, with its own copy of the host.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_UNKNOWN_AUTHORITY_ERROR`
- `Str x509_unknown_authority_error_error(X509UnknownAuthorityError e, Alloc *a)` – UnknownAuthorityError.Error: "x509: certificate signed by unknown authority", from a.
- `Error x509_unknown_authority_error_as_error(X509UnknownAuthorityError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, from a.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_X509_SYSTEM_ROOTS_ERROR`
- `Str x509_system_roots_error_error(X509SystemRootsError e, Alloc *a)` – SystemRootsError.Error: "x509: failed to load system roots and no roots provided", then "; " and err's message when there is one, from a.
- `Error x509_system_roots_error_unwrap(X509SystemRootsError e)` – SystemRootsError.Unwrap: e.err.
- `Error x509_system_roots_error_as_error(X509SystemRootsError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, from a.
- `Slice x509_certificate_verify(const X509Certificate *c, Alloc *a, X509VerifyOptions opts, Error *err)` – Verify attempts to verify c by building one or more chains from c to a certificate in opts.Roots, using certificates in opts.Intermediates if needed.
- `Error x509_certificate_verify_hostname(const X509Certificate *c, Str h)` – VerifyHostname returns nil if c is a valid certificate for the named host.

- `DriverValue sql_null_int16_value(SqlNullInt16 n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Value implements the driver.Valuer interface.
