- `DriverValue sql_null_byte_value(SqlNullByte n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Value implements the driver.Valuer interface.
- `Error sql_null_float64_scan(SqlNullFloat64 *n, Alloc *a, Any value)` – Scan implements the Scanner interface.
- `DriverValue sql_null_float64_value(SqlNullFloat64 n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Value implements the driver.Valuer interface.
- `Error sql_null_bool_scan(SqlNullBool *n, Alloc *a, Any value)` – Scan implements the Scanner interface.
- `DriverValue sql_null_bool_value(SqlNullBool n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Value implements the driver.Valuer interface.
- `Error sql_null_time_scan(SqlNullTime *n, Alloc *a, Any value)` – Scan implements the Scanner interface.
- `DriverValue sql_null_time_value(SqlNullTime n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Value implements the driver.Valuer interface.
- `Any sql_null_string_any(SqlNullString *n)` – The Anys for a scan or an argument list.
- `Any sql_null_int64_any(SqlNullInt64 *n)`
- `Any sql_null_int32_any(SqlNullInt32 *n)`
- `Any sql_null_int16_any(SqlNullInt16 *n)`
- `Any sql_null_byte_any(SqlNullByte *n)`
- `Any sql_null_float64_any(SqlNullFloat64 *n)`
- `Any sql_null_bool_any(SqlNullBool *n)`
- `Any sql_null_time_any(SqlNullTime *n)`
- `Error sql_null_scan(SqlNull *n, Alloc *a, Any value)`
- `DriverValue sql_null_value(SqlNull n, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – nil when not valid, and otherwise the T run through its own Value method if it has one and then driver_default_parameter_converter.
- `Any sql_null_any(SqlNull *n)`
- `Error sql_convert_assign(Alloc *a, DriverScanContext scan_ctx, Any dest, DriverValue src)` – ConvertAssign copies the value in src to the value pointed at by dest.
- `extern const Error sql_err_no_rows` – ErrNoRows is returned by Row.Scan when DB.QueryRow doesn't return a row.
- `extern const Error sql_err_conn_done` – ErrConnDone is returned by any operation that is performed on a connection that has already been returned to the connection pool.
- `extern const Error sql_err_tx_done` – ErrTxDone is returned by any operation that is performed on a transaction that has already been committed or rolled back.
- `SqlDB *sql_open(Str driver_name, Str data_source_name, Error *err)` – Open opens a database specified by its database driver name and a driver-specific data source name, usually consisting of at least a database name and connection information.
- `SqlDB *sql_open_db(DriverConnector c)` – OpenDB opens a database using a driver.Connector, allowing drivers to bypass a string based data source name.
- `Error sql_db_close(SqlDB *db)` – Close closes the database and prevents new queries from starting.
- `void sql_db_free(SqlDB *db)` – Closes db if it is open and lets go of it.
- `Error sql_db_ping_context(SqlDB *db, Context ctx)` – PingContext verifies a connection to the database is still alive, establishing a connection if necessary.
- `Error sql_db_ping(SqlDB *db)` – Ping verifies a connection to the database is still alive, establishing a connection if necessary.
- `void sql_db_set_max_idle_conns(SqlDB *db, Int n)` – SetMaxIdleConns sets the maximum number of connections in the idle connection pool.
- `void sql_db_set_max_open_conns(SqlDB *db, Int n)` – SetMaxOpenConns sets the maximum number of open connections to the database.
- `void sql_db_set_conn_max_lifetime(SqlDB *db, Duration d)` – SetConnMaxLifetime sets the maximum amount of time a connection may be reused.
- `void sql_db_set_conn_max_idle_time(SqlDB *db, Duration d)` – SetConnMaxIdleTime sets the maximum amount of time a connection may be idle.
- `SqlDBStats sql_db_stats(SqlDB *db)` – Stats returns database statistics.
- `Driver sql_db_driver(SqlDB *db)` – Driver returns the database's underlying driver.
- `SqlStmt *sql_db_prepare_context(SqlDB *db, Context ctx, Str query, Error *err)` – PrepareContext creates a prepared statement for later queries or executions.
- `SqlStmt *sql_db_prepare(SqlDB *db, Str query, Error *err)` – Prepare creates a prepared statement for later queries or executions.
- `SqlResult sql_db_exec_context(SqlDB *db, Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – ExecContext executes a query without returning any rows.
- `SqlResult sql_db_exec(SqlDB *db, Alloc *a, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – Exec executes a query without returning any rows.
- `SqlRows *sql_db_query_context(SqlDB *db, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – QueryContext executes a query that returns rows, typically a SELECT.
- `SqlRows *sql_db_query(SqlDB *db, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – Query executes a query that returns rows, typically a SELECT.
- `SqlRow sql_db_query_row_context(SqlDB *db, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args)` – QueryRowContext executes a query that is expected to return at most one row.
- `SqlRow sql_db_query_row(SqlDB *db, Str query, Slice args)` – QueryRow executes a query that is expected to return at most one row.
- `SqlTx *sql_db_begin_tx(SqlDB *db, Context ctx, const SqlTxOptions *opts, Error *err)` – BeginTx starts a transaction.
- `SqlTx *sql_db_begin(SqlDB *db, Error *err)` – Begin starts a transaction.
- `SqlConn *sql_db_conn(SqlDB *db, Context ctx, Error *err)` – Conn returns a single connection by either opening a new connection or returning an existing connection from the connection pool.
- `Error sql_conn_ping_context(SqlConn *c, Context ctx)` – PingContext verifies the connection to the database is still alive.
- `SqlResult sql_conn_exec_context(SqlConn *c, Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – ExecContext executes a query without returning any rows.
- `SqlRows *sql_conn_query_context(SqlConn *c, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – QueryContext executes a query that returns rows, typically a SELECT.
- `SqlRow sql_conn_query_row_context(SqlConn *c, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args)` – QueryRowContext executes a query that is expected to return at most one row.
- `SqlStmt *sql_conn_prepare_context(SqlConn *c, Context ctx, Str query, Error *err)` – PrepareContext creates a prepared statement for later queries or executions.
- `SqlTx *sql_conn_begin_tx(SqlConn *c, Context ctx, const SqlTxOptions *opts, Error *err)` – BeginTx starts a transaction.
- `Error sql_conn_raw(SqlConn *c, SqlRawFunc f)` – Raw executes f exposing the underlying driver connection for the duration of f.
- `Error sql_conn_close(SqlConn *c)` – Close returns the connection to the connection pool.
- `void sql_conn_free(SqlConn *c)` – Closes c if it is open and lets go of it.
- `Error sql_tx_commit(SqlTx *tx)` – Commit commits the transaction.
- `Error sql_tx_rollback(SqlTx *tx)` – Rollback aborts the transaction.
- `void sql_tx_free(SqlTx *tx)` – Rolls tx back if it has not ended and lets go of it.
- `SqlStmt *sql_tx_prepare_context(SqlTx *tx, Context ctx, Str query, Error *err)` – PrepareContext creates a prepared statement for use within a transaction.
- `SqlStmt *sql_tx_prepare(SqlTx *tx, Str query, Error *err)` – Prepare creates a prepared statement for use within a transaction.
- `SqlResult sql_tx_exec_context(SqlTx *tx, Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – ExecContext executes a query that doesn't return rows.
- `SqlResult sql_tx_exec(SqlTx *tx, Alloc *a, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – Exec executes a query that doesn't return rows.
- `SqlRows *sql_tx_query_context(SqlTx *tx, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – QueryContext executes a query that returns rows, typically a SELECT.
- `SqlRows *sql_tx_query(SqlTx *tx, Str query, Slice args, Error *err)` – Query executes a query that returns rows, typically a SELECT.
- `SqlRow sql_tx_query_row_context(SqlTx *tx, Context ctx, Str query, Slice args)` – QueryRowContext executes a query that is expected to return at most one row.
- `SqlRow sql_tx_query_row(SqlTx *tx, Str query, Slice args)` – QueryRow executes a query that is expected to return at most one row.
- `SqlStmt *sql_tx_stmt_context(SqlTx *tx, Context ctx, SqlStmt *stmt)` – StmtContext returns a transaction-specific prepared statement from an existing statement.
- `SqlStmt *sql_tx_stmt(SqlTx *tx, SqlStmt *stmt)` – Stmt returns a transaction-specific prepared statement from an existing statement.
- `SqlResult sql_stmt_exec_context(SqlStmt *s, Alloc *a, Context ctx, Slice args, Error *err)` – ExecContext executes a prepared statement with the given arguments and returns a Result summarizing the effect of the statement.
- `SqlResult sql_stmt_exec(SqlStmt *s, Alloc *a, Slice args, Error *err)` – Exec executes a prepared statement with the given arguments and returns a Result summarizing the effect of the statement.
- `SqlRows *sql_stmt_query_context(SqlStmt *s, Context ctx, Slice args, Error *err)` – QueryContext executes a prepared query statement with the given arguments and returns the query results as a \*Rows.
- `SqlRows *sql_stmt_query(SqlStmt *s, Slice args, Error *err)` – Query executes a prepared query statement with the given arguments and returns the query results as a \*Rows.
- `SqlRow sql_stmt_query_row_context(SqlStmt *s, Context ctx, Slice args)` – QueryRowContext executes a prepared query statement with the given arguments.
- `SqlRow sql_stmt_query_row(SqlStmt *s, Slice args)` – QueryRow executes a prepared query statement with the given arguments.
- `Error sql_stmt_close(SqlStmt *s)` – Close closes the statement.
- `void sql_stmt_free(SqlStmt *s)` – Closes s if it is open and lets go of it.
- `bool sql_rows_next(SqlRows *rs)` – Next prepares the next result row for reading with the Rows.Scan method.
- `bool sql_rows_next_result_set(SqlRows *rs)` – NextResultSet prepares the next result set for reading.
- `Error sql_rows_err(SqlRows *rs)` – Err returns the error, if any, that was encountered during iteration.
- `Slice sql_rows_columns(SqlRows *rs, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Columns returns the column names.
- `Slice sql_rows_column_types(SqlRows *rs, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ColumnTypes returns column information such as column type, length, and nullable.
- `Error sql_rows_scan(SqlRows *rs, Alloc *a, Slice dest)` – Scan copies the columns in the current row into the values pointed at by dest.
- `Error sql_rows_close(SqlRows *rs)` – Close closes the Rows, preventing further enumeration.
- `void sql_rows_free(SqlRows *rs)` – Closes rs if it is open and lets go of it.
- `Error sql_row_scan(SqlRow *r, Alloc *a, Slice dest)` – Scan copies the columns from the matched row into the values pointed at by dest.
- `Error sql_row_err(const SqlRow *r)` – Err provides a way for wrapping packages to check for query errors without calling Row.Scan.
- `Str sql_column_type_name(const SqlColumnType *ct)` – Name returns the name or alias of the column.
- `int64_t sql_column_type_length(const SqlColumnType *ct, bool *ok)` – Length returns the column type length for variable length column types such as text and binary field types.
- `int64_t sql_column_type_decimal_size(const SqlColumnType *ct, int64_t *scale, bool *ok)` – DecimalSize returns the scale and precision of a decimal type.
- `const Type *sql_column_type_scan_type(const SqlColumnType *ct)` – ScanType returns a Go type suitable for scanning into using Rows.Scan.
- `bool sql_column_type_nullable(const SqlColumnType *ct, bool *ok)` – Nullable reports whether the column may be null.
- `Str sql_column_type_database_type_name(const SqlColumnType *ct)` – DatabaseTypeName returns the database system name of the column type.
- `#define sql_db_exec_v(db, a, err, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_db_query_v(db, err, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_db_query_row_v(db, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_tx_exec_v(tx, a, err, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_tx_query_v(tx, err, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_tx_query_row_v(tx, query, ...)`
- `#define sql_stmt_exec_v(s, a, err, ...)`
- `#define sql_stmt_query_v(s, err, ...)`
- `#define sql_stmt_query_row_v(s, ...)`
- `#define sql_rows_scan_v(rs, a, ...)`
- `#define sql_row_scan_v(r, a, ...)`

## burrow/database/sql/driver.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `database/sql/driver` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error driver_err_skip` – ErrSkip may be returned by some optional interfaces' methods to indicate at runtime that the fast path is unavailable and the sql package should continue as if the optional interface was not implemented.
- `extern const Error driver_err_bad_conn` – ErrBadConn should be returned by a driver to signal to the \[database/sql\] package that a driver.\[Conn\] is in a bad state (such as the server having earlier closed the connection) and the \[database/sql\] package should retry on a new connection.
- `extern const Error driver_err_remove_argument` – ErrRemoveArgument may be returned from NamedValueChecker to instruct the \[database/sql\] package to not pass the argument to the driver query interface.
- `int64_t driver_rows_affected_last_insert_id(DriverRowsAffected v, Error *err)` – RowsAffected.LastInsertId, which always fails.
- `int64_t driver_rows_affected_rows_affected(DriverRowsAffected v, Error *err)`
- `DriverResult driver_rows_affected_as_result(DriverRowsAffected *v)` – v as a Result.
- `extern const DriverResult driver_result_no_rows` – ResultNoRows is a pre-defined Result for drivers to return when a DDL command (such as a CREATE TABLE) succeeds.
- `extern const DriverValueConverter driver_bool` – Bool is a ValueConverter that converts input values to bool.
- `extern const DriverValueConverter driver_int32` – Int32 is a ValueConverter that converts input values to int64, respecting the limits of an int32 value.
- `extern const DriverValueConverter driver_string` – String is a ValueConverter that converts its input to a string.
- `extern const DriverValueConverter driver_default_parameter_converter` – DefaultParameterConverter is the default implementation of ValueConverter that's used when a Stmt doesn't implement ColumnConverter.
- `DriverValue driver_null_convert_value(DriverNull n, Alloc *a, Any v, Error *err)`
- `DriverValueConverter driver_null_as_value_converter(DriverNull *n)` – n as a ValueConverter.
- `DriverValue driver_not_null_convert_value(DriverNotNull n, Alloc *a, Any v, Error *err)`
- `DriverValueConverter driver_not_null_as_value_converter(DriverNotNull *n)`
- `bool driver_is_value(Any v)` – IsValue reports whether v is a valid Value parameter type.
- `bool driver_is_scan_value(Any v)` – IsScanValue is equivalent to IsValue.

## burrow/debug/buildinfo.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `debug/buildinfo` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `DebugBuildInfo *buildinfo_read_file(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – ReadFile returns build information embedded in a Go binary file at the given path.
- `DebugBuildInfo *buildinfo_read(Alloc *a, IoReaderAt r, Error *err)` – Read returns build information embedded in a Go binary file accessed through the given ReaderAt.

## burrow/debug/dwarf.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `debug/dwarf` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str dwarf_attr_string(DwarfAttr i, Alloc *a)` – The name without the Attr, such as "Name", or "Attr(N)" for a value with no name, which is the only case that needs a.
- `Str dwarf_attr_go_string(DwarfAttr i, Alloc *a)` – "dwarf.AttrName", or "dwarf.Attr(N)".
- `Str dwarf_tag_string(DwarfTag i, Alloc *a)` – The name without the Tag, such as "Subprogram", or "Tag(N)".
- `Str dwarf_tag_go_string(DwarfTag i, Alloc *a)` – "dwarf.TagSubprogram" up to DWARF_TAG_TEMPLATE_ALIAS, and "dwarf." and the name after it, which is how Go's GoString has it.
- `Str dwarf_class_string(DwarfClass i, Alloc *a)` – "ClassAddress" and so on, with the Class, or "Class(N)".
- `Str dwarf_class_go_string(DwarfClass i, Alloc *a)` – "dwarf.ClassAddress", or "dwarf.Class(N)".
- `Str dwarf_decode_error_error(DwarfDecodeError e, Alloc *a)` – "decoding dwarf section info at offset 0x1c: too short", in a.
- `extern const Error dwarf_err_unknown_pc` – ErrUnknownPC is the error returned by LineReader.ScanPC when the seek PC is not covered by any entry in the line table.
- `DwarfData *dwarf_new(Alloc *a, Slice abbrev, Slice aranges, Slice frame, Slice info, Slice line, Slice pubnames, Slice ranges, Slice str, Error *err)` – New returns a new Data object initialized from the given parameters.
- `Error dwarf_data_add_types(DwarfData *d, Str name, Slice types)` – AddTypes will add one .debug_types section to the DWARF data.
- `Error dwarf_data_add_section(DwarfData *d, Str name, Slice contents)` – AddSection adds another DWARF section by name.
- `Any dwarf_entry_val(const DwarfEntry *e, DwarfAttr a)` – Val returns the value associated with attribute Attr in Entry, or nil if there is no such attribute.
- `DwarfField *dwarf_entry_attr_field(const DwarfEntry *e, DwarfAttr a)` – AttrField returns the Field associated with attribute Attr in Entry, or nil if there is no such attribute.
- `DwarfReader *dwarf_data_reader(DwarfData *d, Alloc *a)` – Reader returns a new Reader for Data.
- `DwarfEntry *dwarf_reader_next(DwarfReader *r, Error *err)` – Next reads the next entry from the encoded entry stream.
- `void dwarf_reader_seek(DwarfReader *r, DwarfOffset off)` – Seek positions the Reader at offset off in the encoded entry stream.
- `void dwarf_reader_skip_children(DwarfReader *r)` – SkipChildren skips over the child entries associated with the last Entry returned by Reader.Next.
- `Int dwarf_reader_address_size(DwarfReader *r)` – AddressSize returns the size in bytes of addresses in the current compilation unit.
- `BinaryByteOrder dwarf_reader_byte_order(DwarfReader *r)` – ByteOrder returns the byte order in the current compilation unit.
- `DwarfEntry *dwarf_reader_seek_pc(DwarfReader *r, uint64_t pc, Error *err)` – SeekPC returns the Entry for the compilation unit that includes pc, and positions the reader to read the children of that unit.
- `Slice dwarf_data_ranges(DwarfData *d, Alloc *a, const DwarfEntry *e, Error *err)` – Ranges returns the PC ranges covered by e, a slice of \[low,high) pairs.

- `Error dwarf_line_reader_next(DwarfLineReader *r, DwarfLineEntry *entry)` – Next sets \*entry to the next row in this line table and moves to the next row.
- `DwarfLineReaderPos dwarf_line_reader_tell(DwarfLineReader *r)` – Tell returns the current position in the line table.
- `void dwarf_line_reader_seek(DwarfLineReader *r, DwarfLineReaderPos pos)` – Seek restores the line table reader to a position returned by LineReader.Tell.
- `void dwarf_line_reader_reset(DwarfLineReader *r)` – Reset repositions the line table reader at the beginning of the line table.
- `Slice dwarf_line_reader_files(DwarfLineReader *r)` – Files returns the file name table of this compilation unit as of the current position in the line table.
- `Error dwarf_line_reader_seek_pc(DwarfLineReader *r, uint64_t pc, DwarfLineEntry *entry)` – SeekPC sets \*entry to the LineEntry that includes pc and positions the reader on the next entry in the line table.

## burrow/debug/elf.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `debug/elf` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `uint32_t elf_r_sym32(uint32_t info)` – R_SYM32, R_TYPE32 and R_INFO32, the symbol index and the type in the info of an ELF32 relocation and the info they make.
- `uint32_t elf_r_type32(uint32_t info)`
- `uint32_t elf_r_info32(uint32_t sym, uint32_t typ)`
- `ElfSymBind elf_st_bind(uint8_t info)` – ST_BIND, ST_TYPE and ST_VISIBILITY, the parts of a symbol's info and other, and ST_INFO, the info a binding and a type make.
- `ElfSymType elf_st_type(uint8_t info)`
- `uint8_t elf_st_info(ElfSymBind bind, ElfSymType typ)`
- `ElfSymVis elf_st_visibility(uint8_t other)`
- `uint32_t elf_r_sym64(uint64_t info)` – R_SYM64, R_TYPE64 and R_INFO, the same three for ELF64.
- `uint32_t elf_r_type64(uint64_t info)`
- `uint64_t elf_r_info(uint32_t sym, uint32_t typ)`
- `Str elf_format_error_error(const ElfFormatError *e, Alloc *a)` – FormatError.Error: "unknown ELF class 'ELFCLASSNONE' in record at byte 0x0", in a.
- `extern const Error elf_err_no_symbols` – ErrNoSymbols is returned by File.Symbols and File.DynamicSymbols if there is no such section in the File.
- `bool elf_version_index_is_hidden(ElfVersionIndex vi)` – IsHidden reports whether the symbol is hidden within the version.
- `uint16_t elf_version_index_index(ElfVersionIndex vi)` – Index returns the version index.
- `ElfFile *elf_new_file(Alloc *a, IoReaderAt r, Error *err)` – NewFile creates a new File for accessing an ELF binary in an underlying reader.
- `ElfFile *elf_open(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file using os.Open and prepares it for use as an ELF binary.
- `Error elf_file_close(ElfFile *f)` – Close closes the File.
- `void elf_file_free(ElfFile *f)` – Closes f as elf_file_close does and gives back everything it holds.
- `ElfSection *elf_file_section_by_type(ElfFile *f, ElfSectionType typ)` – SectionByType returns the first section in f with the given type, or nil if there is no such section.
- `ElfSection *elf_file_section(ElfFile *f, Str name)` – Section returns a section with the given name, or nil if no such section exists.
- `Slice elf_section_data(ElfSection *s, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Data reads and returns the contents of the ELF section.
- `IoReadSeeker elf_section_open(ElfSection *s, Alloc *a)` – Open returns a new ReadSeeker reading the ELF section.
- `Int elf_section_read_at(ElfSection *s, Slice p, int64_t off, Error *err)` – Section.ReadAt, which reads the contents as they are in the file through reader_at, and panics as a nil interface does in Go for a compressed section.
- `IoReadSeeker elf_prog_open(ElfProg *p, Alloc *a)` – Open returns a new ReadSeeker reading the ELF program body.
- `Int elf_prog_read_at(ElfProg *p, Slice b, int64_t off, Error *err)` – Prog.ReadAt, through reader_at.
- `void elf_reader_free(IoReadSeeker r)` – Gives back a reader elf_section_open or elf_prog_open made, and the decompressor inside it if there is one, to the allocator it came from.
- `DwarfData *elf_file_dwarf(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – File.DWARF: the DWARF in the file's debug sections, which may be compressed.
- `Slice elf_file_symbols(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Symbols returns the symbol table for f.
- `Slice elf_file_dynamic_symbols(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – DynamicSymbols returns the dynamic symbol table for f.
- `Slice elf_file_imported_symbols(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ImportedSymbols returns the names of all symbols referred to by the binary f that are expected to be satisfied by other libraries at dynamic load time.
- `Slice elf_file_imported_libraries(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ImportedLibraries returns the names of all libraries referred to by the binary f that are expected to be linked with the binary at dynamic link time.
- `Slice elf_file_dyn_string(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, ElfDynTag tag, Error *err)` – DynString returns the strings listed for the given tag in the file's dynamic section.
- `Slice elf_file_dyn_value(ElfFile *f, Alloc *a, ElfDynTag tag, Error *err)` – DynValue returns the values listed for the given tag in the file's dynamic section.
- `Slice elf_file_dynamic_versions(ElfFile *f, Error *err)` – DynamicVersions returns version information for a dynamic object.
- `Slice elf_file_dynamic_version_needs(ElfFile *f, Error *err)` – DynamicVersionNeeds returns version dependencies for a dynamic object.

## burrow/debug/gosym.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `debug/gosym` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool gosym_sym_static(const GosymSym *s)` – Static reports whether this symbol is static (not visible outside its file).
- `Str gosym_sym_package_name(const GosymSym *s)` – PackageName returns the package part of the symbol name, or the empty string if there is none.
- `Str gosym_sym_receiver_name(const GosymSym *s)` – ReceiverName returns the receiver type name of this symbol, or the empty string if there is none.
- `Str gosym_sym_base_name(const GosymSym *s)` – BaseName returns the symbol name without the package or receiver name.
- `bool gosym_func_static(const GosymFunc *f)` – Static reports whether this symbol is static (not visible outside its file).
- `Str gosym_func_package_name(const GosymFunc *f)` – PackageName returns the package part of the symbol name, or the empty string if there is none.
- `Str gosym_func_receiver_name(const GosymFunc *f)` – ReceiverName returns the receiver type name of this symbol, or the empty string if there is none.
- `Str gosym_func_base_name(const GosymFunc *f)` – BaseName returns the symbol name without the package or receiver name.
- `GosymLineTable *gosym_new_line_table(Alloc *a, Slice data, uint64_t text)` – NewLineTable returns a new PC/line table corresponding to the encoded data.
- `void gosym_line_table_free(GosymLineTable *t)` – Gives back a table gosym_new_line_table made.
- `Int gosym_line_table_pc_to_line(GosymLineTable *t, uint64_t pc)` – PCToLine returns the line number for the given program counter.
- `uint64_t gosym_line_table_line_to_pc(GosymLineTable *t, Int line, uint64_t maxpc)` – LineToPC returns the program counter for the given line number, considering only program counters before maxpc.
- `GosymTable *gosym_new_table(Alloc *a, Slice symtab, GosymLineTable *pcln, Error *err)` – NewTable decodes the Go symbol table (the ".gosymtab" section in ELF), returning an in-memory representation.
- `void gosym_table_free(GosymTable *t)` – Gives back everything t holds.
- `GosymFunc *gosym_table_pc_to_func(GosymTable *t, uint64_t pc)` – PCToFunc returns the function containing the program counter pc, or nil if there is no such function.
- `Str gosym_table_pc_to_line(GosymTable *t, uint64_t pc, Int *line, GosymFunc **fn)` – PCToLine looks up line number information for a program counter.
- `uint64_t gosym_table_line_to_pc(GosymTable *t, Str file, Int line, GosymFunc **fn, Error *err)` – LineToPC looks up the first program counter on the given line in the named file.
- `GosymSym *gosym_table_lookup_sym(GosymTable *t, Str name)` – LookupSym returns the text, data, or bss symbol with the given name, or nil if no such symbol is found.
- `GosymFunc *gosym_table_lookup_func(GosymTable *t, Str name)` – LookupFunc returns the text, data, or bss symbol with the given name, or nil if no such symbol is found.
- `GosymSym *gosym_table_sym_by_addr(GosymTable *t, uint64_t addr)` – SymByAddr returns the text, data, or bss symbol starting at the given address.
- `Str gosym_unknown_file_error_error(GosymUnknownFileError e, Alloc *a)` – UnknownFileError.Error, in a.
- `Str gosym_unknown_line_error_error(const GosymUnknownLineError *e, Alloc *a)` – UnknownLineError.Error, in a.
- `Str gosym_decoding_error_error(const GosymDecodingError *e, Alloc *a)` – DecodingError.Error: "invalid pointer size '3' at byte 0x7", in a.

- `MachoFile *macho_new_file(Alloc *a, IoReaderAt r, Error *err)` – NewFile creates a new File for accessing a Mach-O binary in an underlying reader.
- `MachoFile *macho_open(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file using os.Open and prepares it for use as a Mach-O binary.
- `Error macho_file_close(MachoFile *f)` – Close closes the File.
- `void macho_file_free(MachoFile *f)` – Closes f as macho_file_close does and gives back everything it holds.
- `MachoSegment *macho_file_segment(MachoFile *f, Str name)` – Segment returns the first Segment with the given name, or nil if no such segment exists.

- `Slice macho_file_imported_libraries(MachoFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ImportedLibraries returns the paths of all libraries referred to by the binary f that are expected to be linked with the binary at dynamic link time.
- `MachoFatFile *macho_new_fat_file(Alloc *a, IoReaderAt r, Error *err)` – NewFatFile creates a new FatFile for accessing all the Mach-O images in a universal binary.
- `MachoFatFile *macho_open_fat(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – OpenFat opens the named file using os.Open and prepares it for use as a Mach-O universal binary.
- `Error macho_fat_file_close(MachoFatFile *ff)` – FatFile.Close: closes the file macho_open_fat opened.
- `void macho_fat_file_free(MachoFatFile *ff)` – Closes ff and gives back everything it holds, the File of each architecture included.
- `Error macho_fat_arch_close(MachoFatArch *fa)` – Close closes the File.
- `MachoSegment *macho_fat_arch_segment(MachoFatArch *fa, Str name)` – Segment returns the first Segment with the given name, or nil if no such segment exists.
- `MachoSection *macho_fat_arch_section(MachoFatArch *fa, Str name)` – Section returns the first section with the given name, or nil if no such section exists.
- `DwarfData *macho_fat_arch_dwarf(MachoFatArch *fa, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – DWARF returns the DWARF debug information for the Mach-O file.
- `Slice macho_fat_arch_imported_symbols(MachoFatArch *fa, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ImportedSymbols returns the names of all symbols referred to by the binary f that are expected to be satisfied by other libraries at dynamic load time.
- `Slice macho_fat_arch_imported_libraries(MachoFatArch *fa, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ImportedLibraries returns the paths of all libraries referred to by the binary f that are expected to be linked with the binary at dynamic link time.

## burrow/encoding/json/jsontext.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `encoding/json/jsontext` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONTEXT_OPTIONS`
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_allow_duplicate_names(bool v)` – AllowDuplicateNames specifies that JSON objects may contain duplicate member names.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_allow_invalid_utf8(bool v)` – AllowInvalidUTF8 specifies that JSON strings may contain invalid UTF-8, which will be mangled as the Unicode replacement character, U+FFFD.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_escape_for_html(bool v)` – EscapeForHTML specifies that '\<', '\>', and '&' characters within JSON strings should be escaped as a hexadecimal Unicode codepoint (e.g., \\u003c) so that the output is safe to embed within HTML.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_escape_for_js(bool v)` – EscapeForJS specifies that U+2028 and U+2029 characters within JSON strings should be escaped as a hexadecimal Unicode codepoint (e.g., \\u2028) so that the output is valid to embed within JavaScript.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_preserve_raw_strings(bool v)` – PreserveRawStrings specifies that when encoding a raw JSON string in a Token or Value, pre-escaped sequences in a JSON string are preserved to the output.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_canonicalize_raw_ints(bool v)` – CanonicalizeRawInts specifies that when encoding a raw JSON integer number (i.e., a number without a fraction and exponent) in a Token or Value, the number is canonicalized according to RFC 8785, section 3.2.2.3.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_canonicalize_raw_floats(bool v)` – CanonicalizeRawFloats specifies that when encoding a raw JSON floating-point number (i.e., a number with a fraction or exponent) in a Token or Value, the number is canonicalized according to RFC 8785, section 3.2.2.3.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_reorder_raw_objects(bool v)` – ReorderRawObjects specifies that when encoding a raw JSON object in a Value, the object members are reordered according to RFC 8785, section 3.2.3.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_space_after_colon(bool v)` – SpaceAfterColon specifies that the JSON output should emit a space character after each colon separator following a JSON object name.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_space_after_comma(bool v)` – SpaceAfterComma specifies that the JSON output should emit a space character after each comma separator following a JSON object value or array element.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_multiline(bool v)` – Multiline specifies that the JSON output should expand to multiple lines, where every JSON object member or JSON array element appears on a new, indented line according to the nesting depth.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_with_indent(Str indent)` – WithIndent specifies that the encoder should emit multiline output where each element in a JSON object or array begins on a new, indented line beginning with the indent prefix (see WithIndentPrefix) followed by one or more copies of indent according to the nesting depth.
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_with_indent_prefix(Str prefix)` – WithIndentPrefix specifies that the encoder should emit multiline output where each element in a JSON object or array begins on a new, indented line beginning with the indent prefix followed by one or more copies of indent (see WithIndent) according to the nesting depth.
- `extern const Error jsontext_err_duplicate_name` – ErrDuplicateName indicates that a JSON token could not be encoded or decoded because it results in a duplicate JSON object name.
- `extern const Error jsontext_err_non_string_name` – ErrNonStringName indicates that a JSON token could not be encoded or decoded because it is not a string, as required for JSON object names according to RFC 8259, section 4.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONTEXT_SYNTACTIC_ERROR`
- `Str jsontext_syntactic_error_error(const JsontextSyntacticError *e, Alloc *a)` – Go's Error method, built in a, such as

- `Error jsontext_syntactic_error_as_error(const JsontextSyntacticError *e, Alloc *a)` – An Error for a JsontextSyntacticError you filled in yourself.
- `Str jsontext_kind_string(JsontextKind k)` – String returns a string representation of k.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONTEXT_TOKEN`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_null`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_false`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_true`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_begin_object`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_end_object`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_begin_array`
- `extern const JsontextToken jsontext_end_array`
- `JsontextToken jsontext_bool(bool b)` – Bool constructs a Token representing a JSON boolean.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_string(Str s)` – String constructs a Token representing a JSON string.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_float(double n)` – Float constructs a Token representing a JSON number as a 64-bit floating-point number formatted according to ECMA-262, 10th edition, section 7.1.12.1 and RFC 8785, section 3.2.2.3.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_float32(float n)` – Float32 constructs a Token representing a JSON number as a 32-bit floating-point number formatted according to ECMA-262, 10th edition, section 7.1.12.1, with the exception that -0 is still formatted as -0.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_int(int64_t n)` – Int constructs a Token representing a JSON number from an int64.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_uint(uint64_t n)` – Uint constructs a Token representing a JSON number from a uint64.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_token_clone(JsontextToken t, Alloc *a)` – Clone returns a copy of the token with a value that is not backed by the Decoder buffer and therefore remains valid past subsequent Decoder calls.
- `void jsontext_token_free(JsontextToken t, Alloc *a)` – Gives back what jsontext_token_clone allocated.
- `JsontextKind jsontext_token_kind(JsontextToken t)` – Kind returns the token kind.
- `bool jsontext_token_bool(JsontextToken t)` – Bool returns the value for a JSON boolean.
- `Str jsontext_token_string(JsontextToken t, Alloc *a)` – String returns the unescaped string value for a JSON string.
- `double jsontext_token_float(JsontextToken t, Error *err)` – Float returns the floating-point value for a JSON number parsed according to 64 bits of precision.
- `float jsontext_token_float32(JsontextToken t, Error *err)` – Float32 returns the floating-point value for a JSON number parsed according to 32 bits of precision.
- `int64_t jsontext_token_int(JsontextToken t, Error *err)` – Int returns the signed integer value for a JSON number.
- `uint64_t jsontext_token_uint(JsontextToken t, Error *err)` – Uint returns the unsigned integer value for a JSON number.
- `JsontextValue jsontext_value_clone(JsontextValue v, Alloc *a)` – Clone returns a copy of v.
- `Str jsontext_value_string(JsontextValue v)` – String returns the string formatting of v.
- `bool jsontext_value_is_valid(JsontextValue v, Slice opts)` – IsValid reports whether the raw JSON value is syntactically valid according to the specified options.
- `bool jsontext_value_is_valid_v(JsontextValue v, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsontext_value_format(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, Slice opts)` – Format formats the raw JSON value in place.
- `Error jsontext_value_format_v(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsontext_value_compact(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, Slice opts)` – Compact removes all whitespace from the raw JSON value.
- `Error jsontext_value_compact_v(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsontext_value_indent(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, Slice opts)` – Indent reformats the whitespace in the raw JSON value so that each element in a JSON object or array begins on an indented line according to the nesting.
- `Error jsontext_value_indent_v(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsontext_value_canonicalize(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, Slice opts)` – Canonicalize canonicalizes the raw JSON value according to the JSON Canonicalization Scheme (JCS) as defined by RFC 8785.
- `Error jsontext_value_canonicalize_v(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `Slice jsontext_value_marshal_json(JsontextValue v, Error *err)` – MarshalJSON returns v as the JSON encoding of v.
- `Error jsontext_value_unmarshal_json(JsontextValue *v, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – UnmarshalJSON sets v as the JSON encoding of b.
- `JsontextKind jsontext_value_kind(JsontextValue v)` – Kind returns the starting token kind.
- `Slice jsontext_append_float(Alloc *a, Slice dst, double src, Int bits)` – AppendFloat appends src to dst as a JSON number per RFC 8259, section 6.
- `Slice jsontext_append_quote(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str src, Error *err)` – AppendQuote appends a double-quoted JSON string literal representing src to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice jsontext_append_unquote(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str src, Error *err)` – AppendUnquote appends the decoded interpretation of src as a double-quoted JSON string literal to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice jsontext_append_format(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str src, Slice opts, Error *err)` – AppendFormat formats the JSON value in src and appends it to dst according to the specified options.
- `Slice jsontext_append_format_v(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str src, Error *err, int n, ...)`
- `bool jsontext_pointer_is_valid(JsontextPointer p)` – IsValid reports whether p is a valid JSON Pointer according to RFC 6901.
- `bool jsontext_pointer_contains(JsontextPointer p, JsontextPointer pc)` – Contains reports whether the JSON value that p points to is equal to or contains the JSON value that pc points to.
- `JsontextPointer jsontext_pointer_parent(JsontextPointer p)` – Parent strips off the last token and returns the remaining pointer.
- `Str jsontext_pointer_last_token(JsontextPointer p, Alloc *a)` – LastToken returns the last token in the pointer.
- `JsontextPointer jsontext_pointer_append_token(JsontextPointer p, Alloc *a, Str tok)` – AppendToken appends a token to the end of p and returns the full pointer.
- `IterSeq jsontext_pointer_tokens(JsontextPointer p, Alloc *a)` – Tokens returns an iterator over the reference tokens in the JSON pointer, from first to last.
- `void jsontext_pointer_tokens_free(IterSeq seq, Alloc *a)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONTEXT_ENCODER`
- `JsontextEncoder *jsontext_new_encoder(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, Slice opts)` – NewEncoder constructs a new streaming encoder writing to w configured with the provided options.
- `JsontextEncoder *jsontext_new_encoder_v(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, int n, ...)`
- `void jsontext_encoder_free(JsontextEncoder *e)` – Gives the encoder back.
- `void jsontext_encoder_reset(JsontextEncoder *e, IoWriter w, Slice opts)` – Reset resets an encoder such that it is writing afresh to w and configured with the provided options.
- `void jsontext_encoder_reset_v(JsontextEncoder *e, IoWriter w, int n, ...)`
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_encoder_options(const JsontextEncoder *e)` – Options returns the options used to construct the encoder and may additionally contain semantic options passed to a \[encoding/json/v2.MarshalEncode\] call.
- `Error jsontext_encoder_write_token(JsontextEncoder *e, JsontextToken t)` – WriteToken writes the next token and advances the internal write offset.
- `Error jsontext_encoder_write_value(JsontextEncoder *e, JsontextValue v)` – WriteValue writes the next raw value and advances the internal write offset.
- `int64_t jsontext_encoder_output_offset(const JsontextEncoder *e)` – OutputOffset returns the current output byte offset.
- `Slice jsontext_encoder_available_buffer(JsontextEncoder *e)` – AvailableBuffer returns a zero-length buffer with a possible non-zero capacity.
- `Int jsontext_encoder_stack_depth(const JsontextEncoder *e)` – StackDepth returns the depth of the state machine for written JSON data.
- `JsontextKind jsontext_encoder_stack_index(const JsontextEncoder *e, Int i, int64_t *length)` – StackIndex returns information about the specified stack level.
- `JsontextPointer jsontext_encoder_stack_pointer(JsontextEncoder *e, Alloc *a)` – StackPointer returns a JSON Pointer (RFC 6901) to the most recently written value.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONTEXT_DECODER`
- `JsontextDecoder *jsontext_new_decoder(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Slice opts)` – NewDecoder constructs a new streaming decoder reading from r.
- `JsontextDecoder *jsontext_new_decoder_v(Alloc *a, IoReader r, int n, ...)`
- `void jsontext_decoder_free(JsontextDecoder *d)` – Gives the decoder back.
- `void jsontext_decoder_reset(JsontextDecoder *d, IoReader r, Slice opts)` – Reset resets a decoder such that it is reading afresh from r and configured with the provided options.
- `void jsontext_decoder_reset_v(JsontextDecoder *d, IoReader r, int n, ...)`
- `JsontextOptions jsontext_decoder_options(const JsontextDecoder *d)` – Options returns the options used to construct the decoder and may additionally contain semantic options passed to a \[encoding/json/v2.UnmarshalDecode\] call.
- `JsontextKind jsontext_decoder_peek_kind(JsontextDecoder *d)` – PeekKind retrieves the next token kind, but does not advance the read offset.
- `Error jsontext_decoder_skip_value(JsontextDecoder *d)` – SkipValue is semantically equivalent to calling Decoder.ReadValue and discarding the result, except that memory is not wasted trying to hold the entire result.
- `JsontextToken jsontext_decoder_read_token(JsontextDecoder *d, Error *err)` – ReadToken reads the next Token, advancing the read offset.
- `JsontextValue jsontext_decoder_read_value(JsontextDecoder *d, Error *err)` – ReadValue returns the next raw JSON value, advancing the read offset.
- `int64_t jsontext_decoder_input_offset(const JsontextDecoder *d)` – InputOffset returns the current input byte offset.
- `Slice jsontext_decoder_unread_buffer(const JsontextDecoder *d)` – UnreadBuffer returns the data remaining in the unread buffer, which may contain zero or more bytes.
- `Int jsontext_decoder_stack_depth(const JsontextDecoder *d)` – StackDepth returns the depth of the state machine for JSON data that has already been read.
- `JsontextKind jsontext_decoder_stack_index(const JsontextDecoder *d, Int i, int64_t *length)` – StackIndex returns information about the specified stack level.
- `JsontextPointer jsontext_decoder_stack_pointer(JsontextDecoder *d, Alloc *a)` – StackPointer returns a JSON Pointer (RFC 6901) to the most recently read value.

## burrow/encoding/json/v2.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `encoding/json/v2` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_stringify_numbers(bool v)` – StringifyNumbers specifies that types that would normally be encoded as a JSON number instead be encoded as a JSON string containing the equivalent JSON number value.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_deterministic(bool v)` – Deterministic specifies that marshaling the same input value will always serialize as the same output bytes.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_format_nil_slice_as_null(bool v)` – FormatNilSliceAsNull specifies that a nil Go slice should marshal as a JSON null instead of the default representation as an empty JSON array (or an empty JSON string in the case of ~\[\]byte).
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_format_nil_map_as_null(bool v)` – FormatNilMapAsNull specifies that a nil Go map should marshal as a JSON null instead of the default representation as an empty JSON object.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_omit_zero_struct_fields(bool v)` – OmitZeroStructFields specifies that zero-valued fields of Go struct should be omitted from the marshaled output.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_match_case_insensitive_names(bool v)` – MatchCaseInsensitiveNames specifies that JSON object members are matched against Go struct fields using a case-insensitive match of the name.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_reject_unknown_members(bool v)` – RejectUnknownMembers specifies that unknown members should be rejected when unmarshaling a JSON object.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_join_options(Slice srcs)` – JoinOptions coalesces the provided list of options into a single Options.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_join_options_v(int n, ...)`
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_default_options_v2(void)` – DefaultOptionsV2 is the full set of all options that define v2 semantics.
- `bool jsonv2_get_option(JsontextOptions opts, JsontextOptions (*setter)(bool v), bool *value)` – GetOption returns the value stored in opts with the provided setter, reporting whether the value is present.
- `bool jsonv2_get_option_str(JsontextOptions opts, JsontextOptions (*setter)(Str v), Str *value)` – json.GetOption for jsontext_with_indent and jsontext_with_indent_prefix.
- `extern const Error jsonv2_err_unknown_name` – ErrUnknownName indicates that a JSON object member could not be unmarshaled because the name is not known to the target Go struct.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONV2_SEMANTIC_ERROR`
- `Str jsonv2_semantic_error_error(const Jsonv2SemanticError *e, Alloc *a)` – Go's Error method, built in a, such as
- `Error jsonv2_semantic_error_unwrap(const Jsonv2SemanticError *e)` – e-\>err.
- `Error jsonv2_semantic_error_as_error(const Jsonv2SemanticError *e, Alloc *a)` – An Error for a Jsonv2SemanticError you filled in yourself.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONV2_MAP_STRING_ANY` – map\[string\]any and \[\]any, which is what an Any holding nothing becomes when it is unmarshalled from a JSON object or array.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_JSONV2_SLICE_ANY`
- `Jsonv2Marshalers *jsonv2_marshal_func(Alloc *a, const Type *t, Jsonv2MarshalFn fn, void *ctx)` – MarshalFunc constructs a type-specific marshaler that specifies how to marshal values of type T. T can be any type except a named pointer.
- `Jsonv2Marshalers *jsonv2_marshal_to_func(Alloc *a, const Type *t, Jsonv2MarshalToFn fn, void *ctx)` – MarshalToFunc constructs a type-specific marshaler that specifies how to marshal values of type T. T can be any type except a named pointer.
- `Jsonv2Unmarshalers *jsonv2_unmarshal_func(Alloc *a, const Type *t, Jsonv2UnmarshalFn fn, void *ctx)` – UnmarshalFunc constructs a type-specific unmarshaler that specifies how to unmarshal values of type T. T must be an unnamed pointer or an interface type.
- `Jsonv2Unmarshalers *jsonv2_unmarshal_from_func(Alloc *a, const Type *t, Jsonv2UnmarshalFromFn fn, void *ctx)` – UnmarshalFromFunc constructs a type-specific unmarshaler that specifies how to unmarshal values of type T. T must be an unnamed pointer or an interface type.
- `Jsonv2Marshalers *jsonv2_join_marshalers(Alloc *a, Slice ms)` – JoinMarshalers constructs a flattened list of marshal functions.
- `Jsonv2Marshalers *jsonv2_join_marshalers_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `Jsonv2Unmarshalers *jsonv2_join_unmarshalers(Alloc *a, Slice us)` – JoinUnmarshalers constructs a flattened list of unmarshal functions.
- `Jsonv2Unmarshalers *jsonv2_join_unmarshalers_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)`
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_with_marshalers(const Jsonv2Marshalers *m)` – WithMarshalers specifies a list of type-specific marshalers to use, which can be used to override the default marshal behavior for values of particular types.
- `JsontextOptions jsonv2_with_unmarshalers(const Jsonv2Unmarshalers *u)` – WithUnmarshalers specifies a list of type-specific unmarshalers to use, which can be used to override the default unmarshal behavior for values of particular types.
- `bool jsonv2_get_marshalers(JsontextOptions opts, const Jsonv2Marshalers **value)` – json.GetOption for the two above.
- `bool jsonv2_get_unmarshalers(JsontextOptions opts, const Jsonv2Unmarshalers **value)`
- `Slice jsonv2_marshal(Alloc *a, Any in, Slice opts, Error *err)` – Marshal serializes a Go value as a \[\]byte according to the provided marshal and encode options (while ignoring unmarshal or decode options).
- `Slice jsonv2_marshal_v(Alloc *a, Any in, Error *err, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsonv2_marshal_write(Alloc *a, IoWriter out, Any in, Slice opts)` – MarshalWrite serializes a Go value into an io.Writer according to the provided marshal and encode options (while ignoring unmarshal or decode options).
- `Error jsonv2_marshal_write_v(Alloc *a, IoWriter out, Any in, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsonv2_marshal_encode(JsontextEncoder *out, Any in, Slice opts)` – MarshalEncode serializes a Go value into an jsontext.Encoder according to the provided marshal or encode options (while ignoring unmarshal or decode options).
- `Error jsonv2_marshal_encode_v(JsontextEncoder *out, Any in, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal(Alloc *a, Slice in, Any out, Slice opts)` – Unmarshal decodes a \[\]byte input into a Go value according to the provided unmarshal and decode options (while ignoring marshal or encode options).
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal_v(Alloc *a, Slice in, Any out, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal_read(Alloc *a, IoReader in, Any out, Slice opts)` – UnmarshalRead deserializes a Go value from an io.Reader according to the provided unmarshal and decode options (while ignoring marshal or encode options).
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal_read_v(Alloc *a, IoReader in, Any out, int n, ...)`
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal_decode(Alloc *a, JsontextDecoder *in, Any out, Slice opts)` – UnmarshalDecode deserializes a Go value from a jsontext.Decoder according to the provided unmarshal or decode options (while ignoring marshal or encode options).
- `Error jsonv2_unmarshal_decode_v(Alloc *a, JsontextDecoder *in, Any out, int n, ...)`

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `encoding/xml` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `XmlEndElement xml_start_element_end(XmlStartElement e)` – End returns the corresponding XML end element.

- `XmlToken xml_copy_token(Alloc *a, XmlToken t)` – CopyToken returns a copy of a Token.
- `void xml_token_free(Alloc *a, XmlToken t)` – Gives back a token from xml_copy_token, or anything from the \_copy functions put into a token of the matching kind, to the a it came from.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_SYNTAX_ERROR`
- `Str xml_syntax_error_error(const XmlSyntaxError *e, Alloc *a)` – Go's message, built in a: XML syntax error on line 3: unexpected EOF
- `Error xml_syntax_error_as_error(Alloc *a, const XmlSyntaxError *e)` – e as an Error, with its msg copied, built in a.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_TAG_PATH_ERROR`

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE_ERROR`
- `Str xml_unsupported_type_error_error(const XmlUnsupportedTypeError *e, Alloc *a)` – Go's message, built in a: xml: unsupported type: map\[string\]string
- `XmlToken xml_token_reader_token(XmlTokenReader r, Error *err)` – TokenReader.Token.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_DECODER`
- `XmlDecoder *xml_new_decoder(Alloc *a, IoReader r)` – NewDecoder creates a new XML parser reading from r.
- `XmlDecoder *xml_new_token_decoder(Alloc *a, XmlTokenReader t)` – NewTokenDecoder creates a new XML parser using an underlying token stream.
- `void xml_decoder_free(XmlDecoder *d)` – Frees a decoder and everything it holds.
- `XmlTokenReader xml_decoder_as_token_reader(XmlDecoder *d)` – The decoder as a TokenReader, whose Token is xml_decoder_token.
- `XmlToken xml_decoder_token(XmlDecoder *d, Error *err)` – Token returns the next XML token in the input stream.
- `XmlToken xml_decoder_raw_token(XmlDecoder *d, Error *err)` – RawToken is like Decoder.Token but does not verify that start and end elements match and does not translate name space prefixes to their corresponding URLs.
- `Error xml_decoder_skip(XmlDecoder *d)` – Skip reads tokens until it has consumed the end element matching the most recent start element already consumed, skipping nested structures.
- `int64_t xml_decoder_input_offset(const XmlDecoder *d)` – InputOffset returns the input stream byte offset of the current decoder position.
- `Int xml_decoder_input_pos(const XmlDecoder *d, Int *column)` – InputPos returns the line of the current decoder position and the 1 based input position of the line.
- `Map *xml_html_entity(void)` – HTMLEntity is an entity map containing translations for the standard HTML entity characters.
- `extern const Slice xml_html_auto_close` – HTMLAutoClose is the set of HTML elements that should be considered to close automatically.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_ENCODER`
- `XmlEncoder *xml_new_encoder(Alloc *a, IoWriter w)` – NewEncoder returns a new encoder that writes to w.
- `void xml_encoder_free(XmlEncoder *e)` – Frees an encoder without flushing it.
- `void xml_encoder_indent(XmlEncoder *e, Str prefix, Str indent)` – Indent sets the encoder to generate XML in which each element begins on a new indented line that starts with prefix and is followed by one or more copies of indent according to the nesting depth.
- `Error xml_encoder_encode_token(XmlEncoder *e, XmlToken t)` – EncodeToken writes the given XML token to the stream.
- `Error xml_encoder_flush(XmlEncoder *e)` – Flush flushes any buffered XML to the underlying writer.
- `Error xml_encoder_close(XmlEncoder *e)` – Close the Encoder, indicating that no more data will be written.
- `Slice xml_marshal(Alloc *a, Any v, Error *err)` – Marshal returns the XML encoding of v.
- `Slice xml_marshal_indent(Alloc *a, Any v, Str prefix, Str indent, Error *err)` – MarshalIndent works like Marshal, but each XML element begins on a new indented line that starts with prefix and is followed by one or more copies of indent according to the nesting depth.
- `Error xml_encoder_encode(XmlEncoder *e, Any v)` – Encode writes the XML encoding of v to the stream.
- `Error xml_encoder_encode_element(XmlEncoder *e, Any v, XmlStartElement start)` – EncodeElement writes the XML encoding of v to the stream, using start as the outermost tag in the encoding.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_XML_UNMARSHAL_ERROR`
- `Str xml_unmarshal_error_error(XmlUnmarshalError e, Alloc *a)` – The message, which is e itself, copied into a.
- `Error xml_unmarshal(Alloc *a, Slice data, Any v)` – Unmarshal parses the XML-encoded data and stores the result in the value pointed to by v, which must be an arbitrary struct, slice, or string.
- `Error xml_decoder_decode(XmlDecoder *d, Any v)` – Decode works like Unmarshal, except it reads the decoder stream to find the start element.
- `Error xml_decoder_decode_element(XmlDecoder *d, Any v, const XmlStartElement *start)` – DecodeElement works like Unmarshal except that it takes a pointer to the start XML element to decode into v.
- `Error xml_escape_text(IoWriter w, Slice s)` – EscapeText writes to w the properly escaped XML equivalent of the plain text data s.
- `void xml_escape(IoWriter w, Slice s)` – Escape is like EscapeText but omits the error return value.

## burrow/error.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `errors` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_ERROR`
- `Str error_text(Error err)` – err.Error().
- `Error errors_new(Alloc *a, Str text)` – New returns an error that formats as the given text.
- `Error errors_unwrap(Error err)` – Unwrap returns the result of calling the Unwrap method on err, if err's type contains an Unwrap method returning error.
- `bool errors_is(Error err, Error target)` – Is reports whether any error in err's tree matches target.
- `const void *errors_as(Error err, const Type *target)` – As finds the first error in err's tree that matches target, and if one is found, sets target to that error value and returns true.
- `Error errors_join(Alloc *a, Slice errs)` – Join returns an error that wraps the given errors.
- `Error errors_join_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)` – Join without building a Slice first, which is what most call sites want:
- `Alloc *error_allocator(void)` – The allocator for the calling goroutine's errors, or the calling thread's when it is not running one.
- `ArenaMark error_mark(void)` – Where the calling goroutine's error arena is now, and back to there.
- `void error_release(ArenaMark m)`
- `Error error_retain(Alloc *a, Error err)` – A copy of err in a that does not depend on the error arena or on anything else err pointed at.
- `extern const Error errors_err_unsupported` – ErrUnsupported indicates that a requested operation cannot be performed, because it is unsupported.

- `void expvar_int_set(ExpvarInt *v, int64_t value)`
- `ExpvarVar expvar_int_as_var(ExpvarInt *v)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXPVAR_FLOAT`
- `double expvar_float_value(ExpvarFloat *v)`
- `Str expvar_float_string(ExpvarFloat *v, Alloc *a)`
- `void expvar_float_add(ExpvarFloat *v, double delta)` – Add adds delta to v.
- `void expvar_float_set(ExpvarFloat *v, double value)` – Set sets v to value.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_float_as_var(ExpvarFloat *v)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXPVAR_STRING`
- `Str expvar_string_value(ExpvarString *v, Alloc *a)` – String.Value, the string unquoted, copied into a.
- `Str expvar_string_string(ExpvarString *v, Alloc *a)` – String implements the Var interface.
- `bool expvar_string_set(ExpvarString *v, Str value)` – String.Set.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_string_as_var(ExpvarString *v)`
- `void expvar_string_free(ExpvarString *v)` – Gives back the copy expvar_string_set made.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXPVAR_FUNC`
- `Any expvar_func_value(ExpvarFunc f, Alloc *a)` – Func.Value, f's result.
- `Str expvar_func_string(ExpvarFunc f, Alloc *a)` – Func.String: f's result as JSON, or "" if encoding/json says no, as in Go.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_func_as_var(ExpvarFunc *f)` – f as a Var.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXPVAR_MAP`
- `ExpvarMap *expvar_map_init(ExpvarMap *v)` – Init removes all keys from the map.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_map_get(ExpvarMap *v, Str key)` – Map.Get, the nil Var when key is not there.
- `bool expvar_map_set(ExpvarMap *v, Str key, ExpvarVar av)` – Map.Set.
- `bool expvar_map_add(ExpvarMap *v, Str key, int64_t delta)` – Add adds delta to the \*\[Int\] value stored under the given map key.
- `bool expvar_map_add_float(ExpvarMap *v, Str key, double delta)` – AddFloat adds delta to the \*\[Float\] value stored under the given map key.
- `void expvar_map_delete(ExpvarMap *v, Str key)` – Delete deletes the given key from the map.
- `void expvar_map_do(ExpvarMap *v, ExpvarDoFunc f)` – Do calls f for each entry in the map.
- `Str expvar_map_string(ExpvarMap *v, Alloc *a)` – Map.String, the map as a JSON object.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_map_as_var(ExpvarMap *v)`
- `void expvar_map_free(ExpvarMap *v)` – Gives back the keys, the entries and what expvar_map_add made, and leaves an empty map.
- `void expvar_publish(Str name, ExpvarVar v)` – Publish declares a named exported variable.
- `ExpvarVar expvar_get(Str name)` – Get retrieves a named exported variable.
- `ExpvarInt *expvar_new_int(Str name)` – expvar.NewInt, NewFloat, NewMap and NewString: a new variable, published as name.
- `ExpvarFloat *expvar_new_float(Str name)`
- `ExpvarMap *expvar_new_map(Str name)`
- `ExpvarString *expvar_new_string(Str name)`
- `void expvar_do(ExpvarDoFunc f)` – Do calls f for each exported variable.
- `HttpHandler expvar_handler(void)` – Handler returns the expvar HTTP Handler.
- `void expvar_register(void)` – What importing the package does in Go: puts expvar_handler on http_default_serve_mux as "GET /debug/vars", or as "/debug/vars" when GODEBUG has httpmuxgo121=1.

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `flag` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error flag_err_help` – ErrHelp is the error returned if the -help or -h flag is invoked but no such flag is defined.
- `Str flag_unquote_usage(Alloc *a, const Flag *f, Str *usage)` – UnquoteUsage extracts a back-quoted name from the usage string for a flag and returns it and the un-quoted usage.
- `FlagFlagSet *flag_new_flag_set(Alloc *a, Str name, FlagErrorHandling error_handling)` – NewFlagSet returns a new, empty flag set with the specified name and error handling property.
- `void flag_flag_set_free(FlagFlagSet *f)` – Frees a set from flag_new_flag_set, its flags and the storage behind them.
- `void flag_flag_set_init(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, FlagErrorHandling error_handling)` – Init sets the name and error handling property for a flag set.
- `void flag_flag_set_destroy(FlagFlagSet *f)`
- `Str flag_flag_set_name(FlagFlagSet *f)` – Name returns the name of the flag set.
- `FlagErrorHandling flag_flag_set_error_handling(FlagFlagSet *f)` – ErrorHandling returns the error handling behavior of the flag set.
- `IoWriter flag_flag_set_output(FlagFlagSet *f)` – Output returns the destination for usage and error messages.
- `void flag_flag_set_set_output(FlagFlagSet *f, IoWriter output)` – SetOutput sets the destination for usage and error messages.
- `void flag_flag_set_bool_var(FlagFlagSet *f, bool *p, Str name, bool value, Str usage)` – BoolVar defines a bool flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_int_var(FlagFlagSet *f, Int *p, Str name, Int value, Str usage)` – IntVar defines an int flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_int64_var(FlagFlagSet *f, int64_t *p, Str name, int64_t value, Str usage)` – Int64Var defines an int64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_uint_var(FlagFlagSet *f, Uint *p, Str name, Uint value, Str usage)` – UintVar defines a uint flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.

- `void flag_flag_set_string_var(FlagFlagSet *f, Str *p, Str name, Str value, Str usage)` – StringVar defines a string flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_float64_var(FlagFlagSet *f, double *p, Str name, double value, Str usage)` – Float64Var defines a float64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_duration_var(FlagFlagSet *f, Duration *p, Str name, Duration value, Str usage)` – DurationVar defines a time.Duration flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `bool *flag_flag_set_bool(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, bool value, Str usage)` – Bool defines a bool flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Int *flag_flag_set_int(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Int value, Str usage)` – Int defines an int flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `int64_t *flag_flag_set_int64(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, int64_t value, Str usage)` – Int64 defines an int64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Uint *flag_flag_set_uint(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Uint value, Str usage)` – Uint defines a uint flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `uint64_t *flag_flag_set_uint64(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, uint64_t value, Str usage)` – Uint64 defines a uint64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Str *flag_flag_set_string(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Str value, Str usage)` – String defines a string flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `double *flag_flag_set_float64(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, double value, Str usage)` – Float64 defines a float64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Duration *flag_flag_set_duration(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Duration value, Str usage)` – Duration defines a time.Duration flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_text_var(FlagFlagSet *f, Any p, Str name, Any value, Str usage)` – TextVar defines a flag with a specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_func(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Str usage, FlagFunc fn)` – Func defines a flag with the specified name and usage string.
- `void flag_flag_set_bool_func(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Str usage, FlagFunc fn)` – BoolFunc defines a flag with the specified name and usage string without requiring values.
- `void flag_flag_set_var(FlagFlagSet *f, FlagValue value, Str name, Str usage)` – Var defines a flag with the specified name and usage string.
- `Error flag_flag_set_parse(FlagFlagSet *f, Slice arguments)` – Parse parses flag definitions from the argument list, which should not include the command name.
- `bool flag_flag_set_parsed(FlagFlagSet *f)` – Parsed reports whether f.Parse has been called.
- `Str flag_flag_set_arg(FlagFlagSet *f, Int i)` – Arg returns the i'th argument.
- `Slice flag_flag_set_args(FlagFlagSet *f)` – Args returns the non-flag arguments.
- `Int flag_flag_set_n_arg(FlagFlagSet *f)` – NArg is the number of arguments remaining after flags have been processed.
- `Int flag_flag_set_n_flag(FlagFlagSet *f)` – NFlag returns the number of flags that have been set.
- `Flag *flag_flag_set_lookup(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name)` – Lookup returns the Flag structure of the named flag, returning nil if none exists.
- `Error flag_flag_set_set(FlagFlagSet *f, Str name, Str value)` – Set sets the value of the named flag.
- `void flag_flag_set_visit(FlagFlagSet *f, FlagVisitFunc fn)` – Visit visits the flags in lexicographical order, calling fn for each.
- `void flag_flag_set_visit_all(FlagFlagSet *f, FlagVisitFunc fn)` – VisitAll visits the flags in lexicographical order, calling fn for each.
- `void flag_flag_set_print_defaults(FlagFlagSet *f)` – PrintDefaults prints, to standard error unless configured otherwise, the default values of all defined command-line flags in the set.
- `extern FlagFlagSet *flag_command_line` – CommandLine is the default set of command-line flags, parsed from os.Args.
- `extern Func flag_usage` – flag.Usage, what the command line set calls to explain itself.
- `void flag_parse(void)` – Parse parses the command-line flags from os.Args\[1:\].
- `bool flag_parsed(void)` – Parsed reports whether the command-line flags have been parsed.
- `Str flag_arg(Int i)` – Arg returns the i'th command-line argument.
- `Slice flag_args(void)` – Args returns the non-flag command-line arguments.
- `Int flag_n_arg(void)` – NArg is the number of arguments remaining after flags have been processed.
- `Int flag_n_flag(void)` – NFlag returns the number of command-line flags that have been set.
- `Flag *flag_lookup(Str name)` – Lookup returns the Flag structure of the named command-line flag, returning nil if none exists.
- `Error flag_set(Str name, Str value)` – Set sets the value of the named command-line flag.
- `void flag_visit(FlagVisitFunc fn)` – Visit visits the command-line flags in lexicographical order, calling fn for each.
- `void flag_visit_all(FlagVisitFunc fn)` – VisitAll visits the command-line flags in lexicographical order, calling fn for each.
- `void flag_print_defaults(void)` – PrintDefaults prints, to standard error unless configured otherwise, a usage message showing the default settings of all defined command-line flags.
- `void flag_bool_var(bool *p, Str name, bool value, Str usage)` – BoolVar defines a bool flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_int_var(Int *p, Str name, Int value, Str usage)` – IntVar defines an int flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `void flag_int64_var(int64_t *p, Str name, int64_t value, Str usage)` – Int64Var defines an int64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.

- `void flag_float64_var(double *p, Str name, double value, Str usage)` – Float64Var defines a float64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.

- `bool *flag_bool(Str name, bool value, Str usage)` – Bool defines a bool flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Int *flag_int(Str name, Int value, Str usage)` – Int defines an int flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `int64_t *flag_int64(Str name, int64_t value, Str usage)` – Int64 defines an int64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Uint *flag_uint(Str name, Uint value, Str usage)` – Uint defines a uint flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `uint64_t *flag_uint64(Str name, uint64_t value, Str usage)` – Uint64 defines a uint64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `Str *flag_string(Str name, Str value, Str usage)` – String defines a string flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.
- `double *flag_float64(Str name, double value, Str usage)` – Float64 defines a float64 flag with specified name, default value, and usage string.

## burrow/fmt.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `fmt` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Int fmt_fprintf(IoWriter w, Str format, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fprintf formats according to a format specifier and writes to w.
- `Int fmt_printf(Str format, Slice args, Error *err)` – Printf formats according to a format specifier and writes to standard output.
- `Str fmt_sprintf(Alloc *a, Str format, Slice args)` – Sprintf formats according to a format specifier and returns the resulting string.
- `Slice fmt_appendf(Alloc *a, Slice b, Str format, Slice args)` – Appendf formats according to a format specifier, appends the result to the byte slice, and returns the updated slice.
- `Int fmt_fprint(IoWriter w, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fprint formats using the default formats for its operands and writes to w.
- `Int fmt_print(Slice args, Error *err)` – Print formats using the default formats for its operands and writes to standard output.
- `Str fmt_sprint(Alloc *a, Slice args)` – Sprint formats using the default formats for its operands and returns the resulting string.
- `Slice fmt_append(Alloc *a, Slice b, Slice args)` – Append formats using the default formats for its operands, appends the result to the byte slice, and returns the updated slice.
- `Int fmt_fprintln(IoWriter w, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fprintln formats using the default formats for its operands and writes to w.
- `Int fmt_println(Slice args, Error *err)` – Println formats using the default formats for its operands and writes to standard output.
- `Str fmt_sprintln(Alloc *a, Slice args)` – Sprintln formats using the default formats for its operands and returns the resulting string.
- `Slice fmt_appendln(Alloc *a, Slice b, Slice args)` – Appendln formats using the default formats for its operands, appends the result to the byte slice, and returns the updated slice.
- `Error fmt_errorf(Str format, Slice args)` – Errorf formats according to a format specifier and returns the string as a value that satisfies error.
- `IoWriter fmt_state_writer(FmtState *st)` – A State as an io.Writer, so that a Format method can print into it with fmt_fprintf the way Go code does with fmt.Fprintf(f, ...).
- `Str fmt_format_string(Alloc *a, FmtState state, Rune verb)` – FormatString returns a string representing the fully qualified formatting directive captured by the State, followed by the argument verb.
- `Int fmt_scan(Alloc *a, Slice args, Error *err)` – Scan scans text read from standard input, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments.
- `Int fmt_scanln(Alloc *a, Slice args, Error *err)` – Scanln is similar to Scan, but stops scanning at a newline and after the final item there must be a newline or EOF.
- `Int fmt_scanf(Alloc *a, Str format, Slice args, Error *err)` – Scanf scans text read from standard input, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments as determined by the format.
- `Int fmt_sscan(Alloc *a, Str str, Slice args, Error *err)` – Sscan scans the argument string, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments.
- `Int fmt_sscanln(Alloc *a, Str str, Slice args, Error *err)` – Sscanln is similar to Sscan, but stops scanning at a newline and after the final item there must be a newline or EOF.
- `Int fmt_sscanf(Alloc *a, Str str, Str format, Slice args, Error *err)` – Sscanf scans the argument string, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments as determined by the format.
- `Int fmt_fscan(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fscan scans text read from r, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments.
- `Int fmt_fscanln(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fscanln is similar to Fscan, but stops scanning at a newline and after the final item there must be a newline or EOF.
- `Int fmt_fscanf(Alloc *a, IoReader r, Str format, Slice args, Error *err)` – Fscanf scans text read from r, storing successive space-separated values into successive arguments as determined by the format.
- `IoReader fmt_scan_state_reader(FmtScanState *st)` – A ScanState as an IoReader, so that a Scan method can scan into its own fields with fmt_fscan the way Go code does with fmt.Fscan(state, ...).
- `#define fmt_printf_v(...)`
- `#define fmt_fprintf_v(w, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sprintf_v(a, ...)`
- `#define fmt_appendf_v(a, b, ...)`
- `#define fmt_errorf_v(...)`
- `#define fmt_print_v(...)`
- `#define fmt_fprint_v(w, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sprint_v(a, ...)`
- `#define fmt_append_v(a, b, ...)`
- `#define fmt_println_v(...)`
- `#define fmt_fprintln_v(w, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sprintln_v(a, ...)`
- `#define fmt_appendln_v(a, b, ...)`
- `#define fmt_scan_v(a, err, ...)` – The scanning \_v forms take pointers, which BURROW_SCAN_OF turns into operands, and the error pointer comes second, after the allocator, since it cannot come last:
- `#define fmt_scanln_v(a, err, ...)`
- `#define fmt_scanf_v(a, err, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sscan_v(a, err, str, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sscanln_v(a, err, str, ...)`
- `#define fmt_sscanf_v(a, err, str, ...)`
- `#define fmt_fscan_v(a, err, r, ...)`
- `#define fmt_fscanln_v(a, err, r, ...)`
- `#define fmt_fscanf_v(a, err, r, ...)`

- `TokenPos ast_package_end(AstPackage *p)`
