- `SlogValue slog_duration_value(Duration v)` – DurationValue returns a Value for a time.Duration.
- `SlogValue slog_group_value(Slice as)` – GroupValue returns a new Value for a list of Attrs.
- `SlogValue slog_any_value(Any v)` – AnyValue returns a Value for the supplied value.
- `SlogKind slog_value_kind(SlogValue v)` – Kind returns v's Kind.
- `Any slog_value_any(const SlogValue *v)` – Any returns v's value as an any.
- `Str slog_value_string(SlogValue v, Alloc *a)` – String returns Value's value as a string, formatted like fmt.Sprint.
- `int64_t slog_value_int64(SlogValue v)` – Int64 returns v's value as an int64.
- `uint64_t slog_value_uint64(SlogValue v)` – Uint64 returns v's value as a uint64.
- `double slog_value_float64(SlogValue v)` – Float64 returns v's value as a float64.
- `bool slog_value_bool(SlogValue v)` – Bool returns v's value as a bool.
- `Duration slog_value_duration(SlogValue v)` – Duration returns v's value as a time.Duration.
- `Time slog_value_time(SlogValue v)` – Time returns v's value as a time.Time.
- `Slice slog_value_group(SlogValue v)` – Group returns v's value as a \[\]Attr.
- `Any slog_value_log_valuer(SlogValue v)` – LogValuer returns v's value as a LogValuer.
- `bool slog_value_equal(SlogValue v, SlogValue w)` – Equal reports whether v and w represent the same Go value.
- `SlogValue slog_value_resolve(SlogValue v)` – Resolve repeatedly calls LogValue on v while it implements LogValuer, and returns the result.
- `SlogAttr slog_string(Str key, Str value)` – String returns an Attr for a string value.
- `SlogAttr slog_int64(Str key, int64_t value)` – Int64 returns an Attr for an int64.
- `SlogAttr slog_int(Str key, Int value)` – Int converts an int to an int64 and returns an Attr with that value.
- `SlogAttr slog_uint64(Str key, uint64_t v)` – Uint64 returns an Attr for a uint64.
- `SlogAttr slog_float64(Str key, double v)` – Float64 returns an Attr for a floating-point number.
- `SlogAttr slog_bool(Str key, bool v)` – Bool returns an Attr for a bool.
- `SlogAttr slog_time(Str key, Time v)` – Time returns an Attr for a time.Time.
- `SlogAttr slog_duration(Str key, Duration v)` – Duration returns an Attr for a time.Duration.
- `SlogAttr slog_any(Str key, Any value)` – Any returns an Attr for the supplied value.
- `SlogAttr slog_group_attrs(Str key, Slice attrs)` – GroupAttrs returns an Attr for a Group Value consisting of the given Attrs.
- `SlogAttr slog_group(Alloc *a, Str key, Slice args)` – Group returns an Attr for a Group Value.
- `bool slog_attr_equal(SlogAttr a, SlogAttr b)` – Equal reports whether a and b have equal keys and values.
- `Str slog_attr_string(SlogAttr a, Alloc *al)` – Attr.String, "key=value", built in al.
- `Str slog_level_string(SlogLevel l, Alloc *a)` – String returns a name for the level.
- `Slice slog_level_marshal_json(SlogLevel l, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalJSON implements \[encoding/json.Marshaler\] by quoting the output of Level.String.
- `Error slog_level_unmarshal_json(SlogLevel *l, Alloc *a, Slice data)` – UnmarshalJSON implements \[encoding/json.Unmarshaler\] It accepts any string produced by Level.MarshalJSON, ignoring case.
- `Slice slog_level_append_text(SlogLevel l, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements encoding.TextAppender by calling Level.String.
- `Slice slog_level_marshal_text(SlogLevel l, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements encoding.TextMarshaler by calling Level.AppendText.
- `Error slog_level_unmarshal_text(SlogLevel *l, Alloc *a, Slice data)` – UnmarshalText implements encoding.TextUnmarshaler.
- `SlogLevel slog_level_level(SlogLevel l)` – Level returns the receiver.
- `SlogLevel slog_level_var_level(SlogLevelVar *v)` – Level returns v's level.
- `void slog_level_var_set(SlogLevelVar *v, SlogLevel l)` – Set sets v's level to l.
- `Str slog_level_var_string(SlogLevelVar *v, Alloc *a)` – "LevelVar(INFO)".
- `Slice slog_level_var_append_text(SlogLevelVar *v, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements encoding.TextAppender by calling Level.AppendText.
- `Slice slog_level_var_marshal_text(SlogLevelVar *v, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements encoding.TextMarshaler by calling LevelVar.AppendText.
- `Error slog_level_var_unmarshal_text(SlogLevelVar *v, Alloc *a, Slice data)` – UnmarshalText implements encoding.TextUnmarshaler by calling Level.UnmarshalText.
- `SlogLeveler slog_level_as_leveler(SlogLevel l)` – A fixed level as a Leveler.
- `SlogLeveler slog_level_var_as_leveler(SlogLevelVar *v)`
- `SlogRecord slog_new_record(Time t, SlogLevel level, Str msg, Uintptr pc)` – NewRecord creates a Record from the given arguments.
- `SlogRecord slog_record_clone(SlogRecord r)` – Clone returns a copy of the record with no shared state.
- `Int slog_record_num_attrs(const SlogRecord *r)` – NumAttrs returns the number of attributes in the Record.
- `void slog_record_attrs(const SlogRecord *r, bool (*f)(void *ctx, SlogAttr a), void *ctx)` – Attrs calls f on each Attr in the Record.
- `void slog_record_add_attrs(SlogRecord *r, Alloc *a, Slice attrs)` – AddAttrs appends the given Attrs to the Record's list of Attrs.
- `void slog_record_add(SlogRecord *r, Alloc *a, Slice args)` – Add converts the args to Attrs as described in Logger.Log, then appends the Attrs to the Record's list of Attrs.
- `bool slog_record_source(const SlogRecord *r, SlogSource *out)` – Source returns a new Source for the log event using r's PC.
- `void slog_handler_free(SlogHandler h)` – Frees a handler through its vtable.
- `extern const SlogHandler slog_discard_handler` – DiscardHandler discards all log output.
- `SlogTextHandler *slog_new_text_handler(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, const SlogHandlerOptions *opts)` – NewTextHandler creates a TextHandler that writes to w, using the given options.
- `SlogHandler slog_text_handler_as_handler(SlogTextHandler *h)`
- `bool slog_text_handler_enabled(SlogTextHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogLevel level)` – Enabled reports whether the handler handles records at the given level.
- `Error slog_text_handler_handle(SlogTextHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogRecord r)` – Handle formats its argument Record as a single line of space-separated key=value items.
- `SlogHandler slog_text_handler_with_attrs(SlogTextHandler *h, Slice attrs)` – WithAttrs returns a new TextHandler whose attributes consists of h's attributes followed by attrs.
- `SlogHandler slog_text_handler_with_group(SlogTextHandler *h, Str name)`
- `SlogJSONHandler *slog_new_json_handler(Alloc *a, IoWriter w, const SlogHandlerOptions *opts)` – NewJSONHandler creates a JSONHandler that writes to w, using the given options.
- `SlogHandler slog_json_handler_as_handler(SlogJSONHandler *h)`
- `bool slog_json_handler_enabled(SlogJSONHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogLevel level)` – Enabled reports whether the handler handles records at the given level.
- `Error slog_json_handler_handle(SlogJSONHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogRecord r)` – Handle formats its argument Record as a JSON object on a single line.
- `SlogHandler slog_json_handler_with_attrs(SlogJSONHandler *h, Slice attrs)` – WithAttrs returns a new JSONHandler whose attributes consists of h's attributes followed by attrs.
- `SlogHandler slog_json_handler_with_group(SlogJSONHandler *h, Str name)`
- `SlogMultiHandler *slog_new_multi_handler(Alloc *a, const SlogHandler *handlers, Int n)` – NewMultiHandler creates a MultiHandler with the given Handlers.
- `SlogHandler slog_multi_handler_as_handler(SlogMultiHandler *h)`
- `bool slog_multi_handler_enabled(SlogMultiHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogLevel level)`
- `Error slog_multi_handler_handle(SlogMultiHandler *h, Context ctx, SlogRecord r)`
- `SlogHandler slog_multi_handler_with_attrs(SlogMultiHandler *h, Slice attrs)`
- `SlogHandler slog_multi_handler_with_group(SlogMultiHandler *h, Str name)`
- `SlogLogger *slog_new(Alloc *a, SlogHandler h)` – New creates a new Logger with the given non-nil Handler.
- `void slog_logger_free(SlogLogger *l)` – Gives the logger back, and the handler too when the logger made it, which is what With and WithGroup do.
- `SlogHandler slog_logger_handler(SlogLogger *l)` – Handler returns l's Handler.
- `SlogLogger *slog_logger_with(SlogLogger *l, Slice args)` – With returns a Logger that includes the given attributes in each output operation.
- `SlogLogger *slog_logger_with_group(SlogLogger *l, Str name)` – WithGroup returns a Logger that starts a group, if name is non-empty.
- `bool slog_logger_enabled(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, SlogLevel level)` – Enabled reports whether l emits log records at the given context and level.
- `void slog_logger_log(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, SlogLevel level, Str msg, Slice args)` – Log emits a log record with the current time and the given level and message.
- `void slog_logger_log_attrs(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, SlogLevel level, Str msg, Slice attrs)` – LogAttrs is a more efficient version of Logger.Log that accepts only Attrs.
- `void slog_logger_debug(SlogLogger *l, Str msg, Slice args)` – Debug logs at LevelDebug.
- `void slog_logger_debug_context(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – DebugContext logs at LevelDebug with the given context.
- `void slog_logger_info(SlogLogger *l, Str msg, Slice args)` – Info logs at LevelInfo.
- `void slog_logger_info_context(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – InfoContext logs at LevelInfo with the given context.
- `void slog_logger_warn(SlogLogger *l, Str msg, Slice args)` – Warn logs at LevelWarn.
- `void slog_logger_warn_context(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – WarnContext logs at LevelWarn with the given context.
- `void slog_logger_error(SlogLogger *l, Str msg, Slice args)` – Error logs at LevelError.
- `void slog_logger_error_context(SlogLogger *l, Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – ErrorContext logs at LevelError with the given context.
- `SlogLogger *slog_default(void)` – Default returns the default Logger.
- `void slog_set_default(SlogLogger *l)` – SetDefault makes l the default Logger, which is used by the top-level functions Info, Debug and so on.
- `SlogLevel slog_set_log_logger_level(SlogLevel level)` – SetLogLoggerLevel controls the level for the bridge to the log package.
- `SlogLogger *slog_with(Slice args)` – With calls Logger.With on the default logger.
- `void slog_debug(Str msg, Slice args)` – Debug calls Logger.Debug on the default logger.
- `void slog_debug_context(Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – DebugContext calls Logger.DebugContext on the default logger.
- `void slog_info(Str msg, Slice args)` – Info calls Logger.Info on the default logger.
- `void slog_info_context(Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – InfoContext calls Logger.InfoContext on the default logger.
- `void slog_warn(Str msg, Slice args)` – Warn calls Logger.Warn on the default logger.
- `void slog_warn_context(Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – WarnContext calls Logger.WarnContext on the default logger.
- `void slog_error(Str msg, Slice args)` – Error calls Logger.Error on the default logger.
- `void slog_error_context(Context ctx, Str msg, Slice args)` – ErrorContext calls Logger.ErrorContext on the default logger.
- `void slog_log(Context ctx, SlogLevel level, Str msg, Slice args)` – Log calls Logger.Log on the default logger.
- `void slog_log_attrs(Context ctx, SlogLevel level, Str msg, Slice attrs)` – LogAttrs calls Logger.LogAttrs on the default logger.
- `LogLogger *slog_new_log_logger(Alloc *a, SlogHandler h, SlogLevel level)` – NewLogLogger returns a new log.Logger such that each call to its Output method dispatches a Record to the specified handler.
- `void slog_log_logger_free(Alloc *a, LogLogger *l)`
- `#define slog_logger_log_v(l, ctx, level, ...)` – slog_logger_info_v(l, msg, key, value, ...) and the rest.
- `#define slog_logger_debug_v(l, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_debug_context_v(l, ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_info_v(l, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_info_context_v(l, ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_warn_v(l, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_warn_context_v(l, ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_error_v(l, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_error_context_v(l, ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_log_v(ctx, level, ...)` – The same on the default logger.
- `#define slog_debug_v(...)`
- `#define slog_debug_context_v(ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_info_v(...)`
- `#define slog_info_context_v(ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_warn_v(...)`
- `#define slog_warn_context_v(ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_error_v(...)`
- `#define slog_error_context_v(ctx, ...)`
- `#define slog_logger_log_attrs_v(l, ctx, level, msg, ...)` – slog_logger_log_attrs_v(l, ctx, level, msg, attr, ...), every argument after the message an SlogAttr.
- `#define slog_log_attrs_v(ctx, level, msg, ...)`
- `#define slog_args_v(...)` – The arguments as a Slice of Any, for the functions that take one.
- `#define slog_logger_with_v(l, ...)` – slog_logger_with_v(l, key, value, ...) and slog_with_v(key, value, ...).
- `#define slog_with_v(...)`
- `#define slog_record_add_v(r, a, ...)` – slog_record_add_v(r, a, key, value, ...).
- `#define slog_group_v(...)` – slog_group_v(key, key, value, ...): slog.Group with its attributes on the stack, so the result lasts until the end of the enclosing block.

## burrow/log/syslog.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `log/syslog` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `SyslogWriter *syslog_new(Alloc *a, SyslogPriority priority, Str tag, Error *err)` – New establishes a new connection to the system log daemon.
- `SyslogWriter *syslog_dial(Alloc *a, Str network, Str raddr, SyslogPriority priority, Str tag, Error *err)` – Dial establishes a connection to a log daemon by connecting to address raddr on the specified network.
- `Int syslog_writer_write(SyslogWriter *w, Slice b, Error *err)` – Write sends a log message to the syslog daemon.
- `Error syslog_writer_close(SyslogWriter *w)` – Close closes a connection to the syslog daemon.
- `Error syslog_writer_emerg(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Emerg logs a message with severity LOG_EMERG, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_alert(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Alert logs a message with severity LOG_ALERT, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_crit(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Crit logs a message with severity LOG_CRIT, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_err(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Err logs a message with severity LOG_ERR, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_warning(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Warning logs a message with severity LOG_WARNING, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_notice(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Notice logs a message with severity LOG_NOTICE, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_info(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Info logs a message with severity LOG_INFO, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `Error syslog_writer_debug(SyslogWriter *w, Str m)` – Debug logs a message with severity LOG_DEBUG, ignoring the severity passed to New.
- `IoWriter syslog_writer_as_io_writer(SyslogWriter *w)` – Not in Go: the Writer as an io.Writer, which Go converts to without being asked.
- `void syslog_writer_free(SyslogWriter *w)` – Not in Go, which has a collector: closes w if it is open and gives back everything it holds.
- `LogLogger *syslog_new_logger(Alloc *a, SyslogPriority p, Int log_flag, Error *err)` – NewLogger creates a log.Logger whose output is written to the system log service with the specified priority, a combination of the syslog facility and severity.
- `void syslog_logger_free(Alloc *a, LogLogger *l)` – Not in Go: frees a logger from syslog_new_logger, and the writer it writes to, from the same a.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSLOG_WRITER` – The descriptor of SyslogWriter.

## burrow/map.h

- `Map *map_make(Alloc *a, const Type *key, const Type *val, Int hint)` – A map remembers the allocator it was made with.
- `Map *map_clone(Alloc *a, const Map *m)` – A copy of m made with a, holding the same entries.
- `Int map_len(const Map *m)` – len(m).
- `const Type *map_key_type(const Map *m)` – What the map was made with, for code that was handed a map and has to ask.
- `const Type *map_val_type(const Map *m)`
- `void *map_get(Map *m, const void *key)` – m\[key\], as a pointer to the value in the table, or NULL when the key is not there.
- `bool map_get2(Map *m, const void *key, void *out_val)` – v, ok := m\[key\].
- `bool map_set(Map *m, const void *key, const void *val)` – m\[key\] = val.
- `void map_del(Map *m, const void *key)` – delete(m, key).
- `void map_clear(Map *m)` – clear(m), which empties the map and keeps the memory, the same as Go's builtin.
- `void map_free(Map *m)` – Hands the map's memory back to the allocator it came from, and leaves the Map \* dangling, so it is the last thing you do with one.
- `MapIter map_iter(Map *m)` – for k, v := range m.
- `bool map_next(MapIter *it, const void **key, void **val)`

## burrow/maps.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `maps` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool maps_equal(Map *m1, Map *m2)` – Equal reports whether two maps contain the same key/value pairs.
- `bool maps_equal_func(Map *m1, Map *m2, MapsEqualFunc eq)` – EqualFunc is like Equal, but compares values using eq.
- `Map *maps_clone(Alloc *a, const Map *m)` – Clone returns a copy of m.
- `bool maps_copy(Map *dst, Map *src)` – Copy copies all key/value pairs in src adding them to dst.
- `void maps_delete_func(Map *m, MapsPredFunc del)` – DeleteFunc deletes any key/value pairs from m for which del returns true.
- `IterSeq2 maps_all(Map *m)` – All returns an iterator over key-value pairs from m.
- `IterSeq maps_keys(Map *m)` – Keys returns an iterator over keys in m.
- `IterSeq maps_values(Map *m)` – Values returns an iterator over values in m.
- `bool maps_insert(Map *m, IterSeq2 seq)` – Insert adds the key-value pairs from seq to m.
- `Map *maps_collect(Alloc *a, const Type *key, const Type *val, IterSeq2 seq)` – Collect collects key-value pairs from seq into a new map and returns it.

## burrow/math.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `math` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `uint64_t math_float64bits(double f)` – Float64bits returns the IEEE 754 binary representation of f, with the sign bit of f and the result in the same bit position, and Float64bits(Float64frombits(x)) == x.
- `double math_float64frombits(uint64_t b)` – Float64frombits returns the floating-point number corresponding to the IEEE 754 binary representation b, with the sign bit of b and the result in the same bit position.
- `uint32_t math_float32bits(float f)` – Float32bits returns the IEEE 754 binary representation of f, with the sign bit of f and the result in the same bit position.
- `float math_float32frombits(uint32_t b)` – Float32frombits returns the floating-point number corresponding to the IEEE 754 binary representation b, with the sign bit of b and the result in the same bit position.
- `double math_inf(Int sign)` – Inf returns positive infinity if sign \>= 0, negative infinity if sign \< 0.
- `double math_nan(void)` – NaN returns an IEEE 754 “not-a-number” value.
- `bool math_is_nan(double f)` – IsNaN reports whether f is an IEEE 754 “not-a-number” value.
- `bool math_is_inf(double f, Int sign)` – IsInf reports whether f is an infinity, according to sign.
- `bool math_signbit(double x)` – Signbit reports whether x is negative or negative zero.
- `double math_abs(double x)` – Abs returns the absolute value of x.
- `double math_copysign(double f, double sign)` – Copysign returns a value with the magnitude of f and the sign of sign.
- `double math_floor(double x)` – Floor returns the greatest integer value less than or equal to x.
- `double math_ceil(double x)` – Ceil returns the least integer value greater than or equal to x.
- `double math_trunc(double x)` – Trunc returns the integer value of x.
- `double math_round(double x)` – Round returns the nearest integer, rounding half away from zero.
- `double math_round_to_even(double x)` – RoundToEven returns the nearest integer, rounding ties to even.
- `double math_modf(double f, double *frac)` – Modf returns integer and fractional floating-point numbers that sum to f.
- `double math_dim(double x, double y)` – Dim returns the maximum of x-y or 0.
- `double math_max(double x, double y)` – Max returns the larger of x or y.
- `double math_min(double x, double y)` – Min returns the smaller of x or y.
- `double math_mod(double x, double y)` – Mod returns the floating-point remainder of x/y.
- `double math_remainder(double x, double y)` – Remainder returns the IEEE 754 floating-point remainder of x/y.
- `double math_fma(double x, double y, double z)` – FMA returns x \* y + z, computed with only one rounding.
- `double math_sqrt(double x)` – Sqrt returns the square root of x.
- `double math_cbrt(double x)` – Cbrt returns the cube root of x.
- `double math_hypot(double p, double q)` – Hypot returns Sqrt(p\*p + q\*q), taking care to avoid unnecessary overflow and underflow.
- `double math_pow(double x, double y)` – Pow returns x\*\*y, the base-x exponential of y.
- `double math_pow10(Int n)` – Pow10 returns 10\*\*n, the base-10 exponential of n.
- `double math_frexp(double f, Int *exp)` – Frexp breaks f into a normalized fraction and an integral power of two.
- `double math_ldexp(double frac, Int exp)` – Ldexp is the inverse of Frexp.
- `double math_logb(double x)` – Logb returns the binary exponent of x.
- `Int math_ilogb(double x)` – Ilogb returns the binary exponent of x as an integer.
- `double math_nextafter(double x, double y)` – Nextafter returns the next representable float64 value after x towards y.
- `float math_nextafter32(float x, float y)` – Nextafter32 returns the next representable float32 value after x towards y.
- `double math_exp(double x)` – Exp returns e\*\*x, the base-e exponential of x.
- `double math_exp2(double x)` – Exp2 returns 2\*\*x, the base-2 exponential of x.
- `double math_expm1(double x)` – Expm1 returns e\*\*x - 1, the base-e exponential of x minus 1.
- `double math_log(double x)` – Log returns the natural logarithm of x.
- `double math_log10(double x)` – Log10 returns the decimal logarithm of x.
- `double math_log2(double x)` – Log2 returns the binary logarithm of x.
- `double math_log1p(double x)` – Log1p returns the natural logarithm of 1 plus its argument x.
- `double math_sin(double x)` – Sin returns the sine of the radian argument x.
- `double math_cos(double x)` – Cos returns the cosine of the radian argument x.
- `double math_tan(double x)` – Tan returns the tangent of the radian argument x.
- `double math_sincos(double x, double *cos)` – Sincos returns Sin(x), Cos(x).
- `double math_asin(double x)` – Asin returns the arcsine, in radians, of x.
- `double math_acos(double x)` – Acos returns the arccosine, in radians, of x.
- `double math_atan(double x)` – Atan returns the arctangent, in radians, of x.
- `double math_atan2(double y, double x)` – Atan2 returns the arc tangent of y/x, using the signs of the two to determine the quadrant of the return value.
- `double math_sinh(double x)` – Sinh returns the hyperbolic sine of x.
- `double math_cosh(double x)` – Cosh returns the hyperbolic cosine of x.
- `double math_tanh(double x)` – Tanh returns the hyperbolic tangent of x.
- `double math_asinh(double x)` – Asinh returns the inverse hyperbolic sine of x.
- `double math_acosh(double x)` – Acosh returns the inverse hyperbolic cosine of x.
- `double math_atanh(double x)` – Atanh returns the inverse hyperbolic tangent of x.
- `double math_erf(double x)` – Erf returns the error function of x.
- `double math_erfc(double x)` – Erfc returns the complementary error function of x.
- `double math_erfinv(double x)` – Erfinv returns the inverse error function of x.
- `double math_erfcinv(double x)` – Erfcinv returns the inverse of Erfc(x).
- `double math_gamma(double x)` – Gamma returns the Gamma function of x.
- `double math_lgamma(double x, Int *sign)` – Lgamma returns the natural logarithm and sign (-1 or +1) of Gamma(x).
- `double math_j0(double x)` – J0 returns the order-zero Bessel function of the first kind.
- `double math_j1(double x)` – J1 returns the order-one Bessel function of the first kind.
- `double math_jn(Int n, double x)` – Jn returns the order-n Bessel function of the first kind.
- `double math_y0(double x)` – Y0 returns the order-zero Bessel function of the second kind.
- `double math_y1(double x)` – Y1 returns the order-one Bessel function of the second kind.
- `double math_yn(Int n, double x)` – Yn returns the order-n Bessel function of the second kind.

## burrow/math/big.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `math/big` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str big_accuracy_string(BigAccuracy i, Alloc *a)` – Accuracy.String: "Below", "Exact" or "Above", and "Accuracy(n)" for a value that is none of them.
- `Str big_rounding_mode_string(BigRoundingMode i, Alloc *a)` – RoundingMode.String: "ToNearestEven" and so on, and "RoundingMode(n)" for a value that is none of them.
- `void big_int_free(BigInt *x)` – Gives the words back to the allocator and leaves x zero, still on the same allocator.
- `BigInt *big_new_int(Alloc *a, int64_t x)` – NewInt allocates and returns a new Int set to x.
- `Int big_int_sign(const BigInt *x)` – Sign returns: - -1 if x \< 0; - 0 if x == 0; - +1 if x \> 0.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_int64(BigInt *z, int64_t x)` – SetInt64 sets z to x and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_uint64(BigInt *z, uint64_t x)` – SetUint64 sets z to x and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_set(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x)` – Set sets z to x and returns z.
- `Slice big_int_bits(const BigInt *x)` – Bits provides raw (unchecked but fast) access to x by returning its absolute value as a little-endian Word slice.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_bits(BigInt *z, Slice abs)` – SetBits provides raw (unchecked but fast) access to z by setting its value to abs, interpreted as a little-endian Word slice, and returning z.
- `BigInt *big_int_abs(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x)` – Abs sets z to |x| (the absolute value of x) and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_neg(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x)` – Neg sets z to -x and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_add(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Add sets z to the sum x+y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_sub(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Sub sets z to the difference x-y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_mul(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Mul sets z to the product x\*y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_mul_range(BigInt *z, int64_t a, int64_t b)` – MulRange sets z to the product of all integers in the range \[a, b\] inclusively and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_binomial(BigInt *z, int64_t n, int64_t k)` – Binomial sets z to the binomial coefficient C(n, k) and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_quo(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Quo sets z to the quotient x/y for y != 0 and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_rem(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Rem sets z to the remainder x%y for y != 0 and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_quo_rem(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y, BigInt *r)` – QuoRem sets z to the quotient x/y and r to the remainder x%y and returns the pair (z, r) for y != 0.
- `BigInt *big_int_div(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Div sets z to the quotient x/y for y != 0 and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_mod(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Mod sets z to the modulus x%y for y != 0 and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_div_mod(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y, BigInt *m)` – DivMod sets z to the quotient x div y and m to the modulus x mod y and returns the pair (z, m) for y != 0.
- `BigInt *big_int_divide(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y, BigInt *r, BigRoundingMode mode)` – Divide computes the integer quotient q and remainder r such that
- `Int big_int_cmp(const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Cmp compares x and y and returns: - -1 if x \< y; - 0 if x == y; - +1 if x \> y.
- `Int big_int_cmp_abs(const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – CmpAbs compares the absolute values of x and y and returns: - -1 if |x| \< |y|; - 0 if |x| == |y|; - +1 if |x| \> |y|.
- `int64_t big_int_int64(const BigInt *x)` – Int64 returns the int64 representation of x.
- `uint64_t big_int_uint64(const BigInt *x)` – Uint64 returns the uint64 representation of x.
- `bool big_int_is_int64(const BigInt *x)` – IsInt64 reports whether x can be represented as an int64.
- `bool big_int_is_uint64(const BigInt *x)` – IsUint64 reports whether x can be represented as a uint64.
- `double big_int_float64(const BigInt *x, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Float64 returns the float64 value nearest x, and an indication of any rounding that occurred.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_string(BigInt *z, Str s, Int base, bool *ok)` – SetString sets z to the value of s, interpreted in the given base, and returns z and a boolean indicating success.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_bytes(BigInt *z, Slice buf)` – SetBytes interprets buf as the bytes of a big-endian unsigned integer, sets z to that value, and returns z.
- `Slice big_int_bytes(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a)` – Bytes returns the absolute value of x as a big-endian byte slice.
- `Slice big_int_fill_bytes(const BigInt *x, Slice buf)` – FillBytes sets buf to the absolute value of x, storing it as a zero-extended big-endian byte slice, and returns buf.
- `Int big_int_bit_len(const BigInt *x)` – BitLen returns the length of the absolute value of x in bits.
- `Uint big_int_trailing_zero_bits(const BigInt *x)` – TrailingZeroBits returns the number of consecutive least significant zero bits of |x|.
- `BigInt *big_int_exp(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y, const BigInt *m)` – Exp sets z = x\*\*y mod |m| (i.e.
- `BigInt *big_int_gcd(BigInt *z, BigInt *x, BigInt *y, const BigInt *a, const BigInt *b)` – GCD sets z to the greatest common divisor of a and b and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_rand(BigInt *z, MathRandRand *rnd, const BigInt *n)` – Rand sets z to a pseudo-random number in \[0, n) and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_mod_inverse(BigInt *z, const BigInt *g, const BigInt *n)` – ModInverse sets z to the multiplicative inverse of g in the ring ℤ/nℤ and returns z.
- `Int big_jacobi(const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Jacobi returns the Jacobi symbol (x/y), either +1, -1, or 0.
- `BigInt *big_int_mod_sqrt(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *p)` – ModSqrt sets z to a square root of x mod p if such a square root exists, and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_lsh(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, Uint n)` – Lsh sets z = x \<\< n and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_rsh(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, Uint n)` – Rsh sets z = x \>\> n and returns z.
- `Uint big_int_bit(const BigInt *x, Int i)` – Bit returns the value of the i'th bit of x.
- `BigInt *big_int_set_bit(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, Int i, Uint b)` – SetBit sets z to x, with x's i'th bit set to b (0 or 1).
- `BigInt *big_int_and(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – And sets z = x & y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_and_not(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – AndNot sets z = x &^ y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_or(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Or sets z = x | y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_xor(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x, const BigInt *y)` – Xor sets z = x ^ y and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_not(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x)` – Not sets z = ^x and returns z.
- `BigInt *big_int_sqrt(BigInt *z, const BigInt *x)` – Sqrt sets z to ⌊√x⌋, the largest integer such that z² ≤ x, and returns z.
- `bool big_int_probably_prime(const BigInt *x, Int n)` – ProbablyPrime reports whether x is probably prime, applying the Miller-Rabin test with n pseudorandomly chosen bases as well as a Baillie-PSW test.
- `Str big_int_text(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Int base)` – Text returns the string representation of x in the given base.
- `Str big_int_string(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a)` – String returns the decimal representation of x as generated by x.Text(10).
- `Slice big_int_append(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Slice buf, Int base)` – Append appends the string representation of x, as generated by x.Text(base), to buf and returns the extended buffer.
- `void big_int_format(const BigInt *x, FmtState s, Rune ch)` – Format implements fmt.Formatter.
- `Error big_int_scan(BigInt *z, FmtScanState s, Rune ch)` – Scan is a support routine for fmt.Scanner; it sets z to the value of the scanned number.
- `Slice big_int_append_text(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements the encoding.TextAppender interface.
- `Slice big_int_marshal_text(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements the encoding.TextMarshaler interface.
- `Error big_int_unmarshal_text(BigInt *z, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements the encoding.TextUnmarshaler interface.
- `Slice big_int_marshal_json(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalJSON implements the \[encoding/json.Marshaler\] interface.
- `Error big_int_unmarshal_json(BigInt *z, Slice text)` – UnmarshalJSON implements the \[encoding/json.Unmarshaler\] interface.
- `Slice big_int_gob_encode(const BigInt *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GobEncode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobEncoder\] interface.
- `Error big_int_gob_decode(BigInt *z, Slice buf)` – GobDecode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobDecoder\] interface.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BIG_INT`
- `void big_rat_free(BigRat *x)` – Gives the words of both halves back and leaves x zero.
- `BigRat *big_new_rat(Alloc *a, int64_t num, int64_t den)` – NewRat creates a new Rat with numerator a and denominator b.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_float64(BigRat *z, double f)` – SetFloat64 sets z to exactly f and returns z.
- `float big_rat_float32(const BigRat *x, bool *exact)` – Float32 returns the nearest float32 value for x and a bool indicating whether f represents x exactly.
- `double big_rat_float64(const BigRat *x, bool *exact)` – Float64 returns the nearest float64 value for x and a bool indicating whether f represents x exactly.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_frac(BigRat *z, const BigInt *a, const BigInt *b)` – SetFrac sets z to a/b and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_frac64(BigRat *z, int64_t a, int64_t b)` – SetFrac64 sets z to a/b and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_int(BigRat *z, const BigInt *x)` – SetInt sets z to x (by making a copy of x) and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_int64(BigRat *z, int64_t x)` – SetInt64 sets z to x and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_uint64(BigRat *z, uint64_t x)` – SetUint64 sets z to x and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x)` – Set sets z to x (by making a copy of x) and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_abs(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x)` – Abs sets z to |x| (the absolute value of x) and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_neg(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x)` – Neg sets z to -x and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_inv(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x)` – Inv sets z to 1/x and returns z.
- `Int big_rat_sign(const BigRat *x)` – Sign returns: - -1 if x \< 0; - 0 if x == 0; - +1 if x \> 0.
- `bool big_rat_is_int(const BigRat *x)` – IsInt reports whether the denominator of x is 1.
- `BigInt *big_rat_num(BigRat *x)` – Num returns the numerator of x; it may be \<= 0.
- `BigInt *big_rat_denom(BigRat *x)` – Denom returns the denominator of x; it is always \> 0.
- `Int big_rat_cmp(const BigRat *x, const BigRat *y)` – Cmp compares x and y and returns: - -1 if x \< y; - 0 if x == y; - +1 if x \> y.
- `BigRat *big_rat_add(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x, const BigRat *y)` – Add sets z to the sum x+y and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_sub(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x, const BigRat *y)` – Sub sets z to the difference x-y and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_mul(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x, const BigRat *y)` – Mul sets z to the product x\*y and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_quo(BigRat *z, const BigRat *x, const BigRat *y)` – Quo sets z to the quotient x/y and returns z.
- `BigRat *big_rat_set_string(BigRat *z, Str s, bool *ok)` – SetString sets z to the value of s and returns z and a boolean indicating success.
- `Str big_rat_string(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a)` – String returns a string representation of x in the form "a/b" (even if b == 1).
- `Str big_rat_rat_string(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a)` – RatString returns a string representation of x in the form "a/b" if b != 1, and in the form "a" if b == 1.
- `Str big_rat_float_string(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a, Int prec)` – FloatString returns a string representation of x in decimal form with prec digits of precision after the radix point.
- `Int big_rat_float_prec(const BigRat *x, bool *exact)` – FloatPrec returns the number n of non-repeating digits immediately following the decimal point of the decimal representation of x.
- `Error big_rat_scan(BigRat *z, FmtScanState s, Rune ch)` – Scan is a support routine for fmt.Scanner.
- `Slice big_rat_append_text(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements the encoding.TextAppender interface.
- `Slice big_rat_marshal_text(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements the encoding.TextMarshaler interface.
- `Error big_rat_unmarshal_text(BigRat *z, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements the encoding.TextUnmarshaler interface.
- `Slice big_rat_gob_encode(const BigRat *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GobEncode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobEncoder\] interface.
- `Error big_rat_gob_decode(BigRat *z, Slice buf)` – GobDecode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobDecoder\] interface.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BIG_RAT`
- `Str big_err_nan_error(BigErrNaN err)` – ErrNaN.Error: the message.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BIG_ERR_NAN`
- `void big_float_free(BigFloat *x)` – Gives the mantissa back to the allocator and leaves x +0, with its precision and mode kept.
- `BigFloat *big_new_float(Alloc *a, double x)` – NewFloat allocates and returns a new Float set to x, with precision 53 and rounding mode ToNearestEven.
- `BigFloat *big_parse_float(Alloc *a, Str s, Int base, Uint prec, BigRoundingMode mode, Int *b, Error *err)` – ParseFloat is like f.Parse(s, base) with f set to the given precision and rounding mode.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_prec(BigFloat *z, Uint prec)` – SetPrec sets z's precision to prec and returns the (possibly) rounded value of z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_mode(BigFloat *z, BigRoundingMode mode)` – SetMode sets z's rounding mode to mode and returns an exact z.
- `Uint big_float_prec(const BigFloat *x)` – Prec returns the mantissa precision of x in bits.
- `Uint big_float_min_prec(const BigFloat *x)` – MinPrec returns the minimum precision required to represent x exactly (i.e., the smallest prec before x.SetPrec(prec) would start rounding x).
- `BigRoundingMode big_float_mode(const BigFloat *x)` – Mode returns the rounding mode of x.
- `BigAccuracy big_float_acc(const BigFloat *x)` – Acc returns the accuracy of x produced by the most recent operation, unless explicitly documented otherwise by that operation.
- `Int big_float_sign(const BigFloat *x)` – Sign returns: - -1 if x \< 0; - 0 if x is ±0; - +1 if x \> 0.
- `Int big_float_mant_exp(const BigFloat *x, BigFloat *mant)` – MantExp breaks x into its mantissa and exponent components and returns the exponent.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_mant_exp(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *mant, Int exp)` – SetMantExp sets z to mant × 2\*\*exp and returns z.
- `bool big_float_signbit(const BigFloat *x)` – Signbit reports whether x is negative or negative zero.
- `bool big_float_is_inf(const BigFloat *x)` – IsInf reports whether x is +Inf or -Inf.
- `bool big_float_is_int(const BigFloat *x)` – IsInt reports whether x is an integer.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_uint64(BigFloat *z, uint64_t x)` – SetUint64 sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_int64(BigFloat *z, int64_t x)` – SetInt64 sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_float64(BigFloat *z, double x)` – SetFloat64 sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_int(BigFloat *z, const BigInt *x)` – SetInt sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_rat(BigFloat *z, const BigRat *x)` – SetRat sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_inf(BigFloat *z, bool signbit)` – SetInf sets z to the infinite Float -Inf if signbit is set, or +Inf if signbit is not set, and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x)` – Set sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_copy(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x)` – Copy sets z to x, with the same precision, rounding mode, and accuracy as x.
- `uint64_t big_float_uint64(const BigFloat *x, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Uint64 returns the unsigned integer resulting from truncating x towards zero.
- `int64_t big_float_int64(const BigFloat *x, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Int64 returns the integer resulting from truncating x towards zero.
- `float big_float_float32(const BigFloat *x, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Float32 returns the float32 value nearest to x.
- `double big_float_float64(const BigFloat *x, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Float64 returns the float64 value nearest to x.
- `BigInt *big_float_int(const BigFloat *x, BigInt *z, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Int returns the result of truncating x towards zero; or nil if x is an infinity.
- `BigRat *big_float_rat(const BigFloat *x, BigRat *z, BigAccuracy *acc)` – Rat returns the rational number corresponding to x; or nil if x is an infinity.
- `BigFloat *big_float_abs(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x)` – Abs sets z to the (possibly rounded) value |x| (the absolute value of x) and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_neg(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x)` – Neg sets z to the (possibly rounded) value of x with its sign negated, and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_add(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x, const BigFloat *y)` – Add sets z to the rounded sum x+y and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_sub(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x, const BigFloat *y)` – Sub sets z to the rounded difference x-y and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_mul(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x, const BigFloat *y)` – Mul sets z to the rounded product x\*y and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_quo(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x, const BigFloat *y)` – Quo sets z to the rounded quotient x/y and returns z.
- `BigFloat *big_float_sqrt(BigFloat *z, const BigFloat *x)` – Sqrt sets z to the rounded square root of x, and returns it.
- `Int big_float_cmp(const BigFloat *x, const BigFloat *y)` – Cmp compares x and y and returns: - -1 if x \< y; - 0 if x == y (incl.
- `BigFloat *big_float_parse(BigFloat *z, Str s, Int base, Int *b, Error *err)` – Parse parses s which must contain a text representation of a floating- point number with a mantissa in the given conversion base (the exponent is always a decimal number), or a string representing an infinite value.
- `BigFloat *big_float_set_string(BigFloat *z, Str s, bool *ok)` – SetString sets z to the value of s and returns z and a boolean indicating success.
- `Str big_float_text(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a, Byte format, Int prec)` – Text converts the floating-point number x to a string according to the given format and precision prec.
- `Str big_float_string(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a)` – String formats x like x.Text('g', 10).
- `Slice big_float_append(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a, Slice buf, Byte format, Int prec)` – Append appends to buf the string form of the floating-point number x, as generated by x.Text, and returns the extended buffer.
- `void big_float_format(const BigFloat *x, FmtState s, Rune format)` – Format implements fmt.Formatter.
- `Error big_float_scan(BigFloat *z, FmtScanState s, Rune ch)` – Scan is a support routine for fmt.Scanner; it sets z to the value of the scanned number.
- `Slice big_float_gob_encode(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GobEncode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobEncoder\] interface.
- `Error big_float_gob_decode(BigFloat *z, Slice buf)` – GobDecode implements the \[encoding/gob.GobDecoder\] interface.
- `Slice big_float_append_text(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements the encoding.TextAppender interface.
- `Slice big_float_marshal_text(const BigFloat *x, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements the encoding.TextMarshaler interface.
- `Error big_float_unmarshal_text(BigFloat *z, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements the encoding.TextUnmarshaler interface.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_BIG_FLOAT`

## burrow/math/bits.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `math/bits` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Int bits_len64(uint64_t x)` – Len64 returns the minimum number of bits required to represent x; the result is 0 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_len32(uint32_t x)` – Len32 returns the minimum number of bits required to represent x; the result is 0 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_len16(uint16_t x)` – Len16 returns the minimum number of bits required to represent x; the result is 0 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_len8(uint8_t x)` – Len8 returns the minimum number of bits required to represent x; the result is 0 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_len(Uint x)` – Len returns the minimum number of bits required to represent x; the result is 0 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_leading_zeros64(uint64_t x)` – LeadingZeros64 returns the number of leading zero bits in x; the result is 64 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_leading_zeros32(uint32_t x)` – LeadingZeros32 returns the number of leading zero bits in x; the result is 32 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_leading_zeros16(uint16_t x)` – LeadingZeros16 returns the number of leading zero bits in x; the result is 16 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_leading_zeros8(uint8_t x)` – LeadingZeros8 returns the number of leading zero bits in x; the result is 8 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_leading_zeros(Uint x)` – LeadingZeros returns the number of leading zero bits in x; the result is UintSize for x == 0.
- `Int bits_trailing_zeros64(uint64_t x)` – TrailingZeros64 returns the number of trailing zero bits in x; the result is 64 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_trailing_zeros32(uint32_t x)` – TrailingZeros32 returns the number of trailing zero bits in x; the result is 32 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_trailing_zeros16(uint16_t x)` – TrailingZeros16 returns the number of trailing zero bits in x; the result is 16 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_trailing_zeros8(uint8_t x)` – TrailingZeros8 returns the number of trailing zero bits in x; the result is 8 for x == 0.
- `Int bits_trailing_zeros(Uint x)` – TrailingZeros returns the number of trailing zero bits in x; the result is UintSize for x == 0.
- `Int bits_ones_count64(uint64_t x)` – OnesCount64 returns the number of one bits ("population count") in x.
- `Int bits_ones_count32(uint32_t x)` – OnesCount32 returns the number of one bits ("population count") in x.
- `Int bits_ones_count16(uint16_t x)` – OnesCount16 returns the number of one bits ("population count") in x.
- `Int bits_ones_count8(uint8_t x)` – OnesCount8 returns the number of one bits ("population count") in x.
- `Int bits_ones_count(Uint x)` – OnesCount returns the number of one bits ("population count") in x.
- `uint64_t bits_rotate_left64(uint64_t x, Int k)` – RotateLeft64 returns the value of x rotated left by (k mod 64) bits.
- `uint32_t bits_rotate_left32(uint32_t x, Int k)` – RotateLeft32 returns the value of x rotated left by (k mod 32) bits.
- `uint16_t bits_rotate_left16(uint16_t x, Int k)` – RotateLeft16 returns the value of x rotated left by (k mod 16) bits.
- `uint8_t bits_rotate_left8(uint8_t x, Int k)` – RotateLeft8 returns the value of x rotated left by (k mod 8) bits.
- `Uint bits_rotate_left(Uint x, Int k)` – RotateLeft returns the value of x rotated left by (k mod UintSize) bits.
- `uint16_t bits_reverse_bytes16(uint16_t x)` – ReverseBytes16 returns the value of x with its bytes in reversed order.
- `uint32_t bits_reverse_bytes32(uint32_t x)` – ReverseBytes32 returns the value of x with its bytes in reversed order.
- `uint64_t bits_reverse_bytes64(uint64_t x)` – ReverseBytes64 returns the value of x with its bytes in reversed order.
- `Uint bits_reverse_bytes(Uint x)` – ReverseBytes returns the value of x with its bytes in reversed order.
- `uint64_t bits_reverse64(uint64_t x)` – Reverse64 returns the value of x with its bits in reversed order.
- `uint32_t bits_reverse32(uint32_t x)` – Reverse32 returns the value of x with its bits in reversed order.
- `uint16_t bits_reverse16(uint16_t x)` – Reverse16 returns the value of x with its bits in reversed order.
- `uint8_t bits_reverse8(uint8_t x)` – Reverse8 returns the value of x with its bits in reversed order.
- `Uint bits_reverse(Uint x)` – Reverse returns the value of x with its bits in reversed order.
- `uint64_t bits_add64(uint64_t x, uint64_t y, uint64_t carry, uint64_t *carry_out)` – Add64 returns the sum with carry of x, y and carry: sum = x + y + carry.
- `uint32_t bits_add32(uint32_t x, uint32_t y, uint32_t carry, uint32_t *carry_out)` – Add32 returns the sum with carry of x, y and carry: sum = x + y + carry.
- `Uint bits_add(Uint x, Uint y, Uint carry, Uint *carry_out)` – Add returns the sum with carry of x, y and carry: sum = x + y + carry.
- `uint64_t bits_sub64(uint64_t x, uint64_t y, uint64_t borrow, uint64_t *borrow_out)` – Sub64 returns the difference of x, y and borrow: diff = x - y - borrow.
- `uint32_t bits_sub32(uint32_t x, uint32_t y, uint32_t borrow, uint32_t *borrow_out)` – Sub32 returns the difference of x, y and borrow, diff = x - y - borrow.
- `Uint bits_sub(Uint x, Uint y, Uint borrow, Uint *borrow_out)` – Sub returns the difference of x, y and borrow: diff = x - y - borrow.
- `uint64_t bits_mul64(uint64_t x, uint64_t y, uint64_t *lo)` – Mul64 returns the 128-bit product of x and y: (hi, lo) = x \* y with the product bits' upper half returned in hi and the lower half returned in lo.
- `uint32_t bits_mul32(uint32_t x, uint32_t y, uint32_t *lo)` – Mul32 returns the 64-bit product of x and y: (hi, lo) = x \* y with the product bits' upper half returned in hi and the lower half returned in lo.
- `Uint bits_mul(Uint x, Uint y, Uint *lo)` – Mul returns the full-width product of x and y: (hi, lo) = x \* y with the product bits' upper half returned in hi and the lower half returned in lo.
- `uint64_t bits_div64(uint64_t hi, uint64_t lo, uint64_t y, uint64_t *rem)` – Div64 returns the quotient and remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y: quo = (hi, lo)/y, rem = (hi, lo)%y with the dividend bits' upper half in parameter hi and the lower half in parameter lo.
- `uint32_t bits_div32(uint32_t hi, uint32_t lo, uint32_t y, uint32_t *rem)` – Div32 returns the quotient and remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y: quo = (hi, lo)/y, rem = (hi, lo)%y with the dividend bits' upper half in parameter hi and the lower half in parameter lo.
- `Uint bits_div(Uint hi, Uint lo, Uint y, Uint *rem)` – Div returns the quotient and remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y: quo = (hi, lo)/y, rem = (hi, lo)%y with the dividend bits' upper half in parameter hi and the lower half in parameter lo.
- `uint64_t bits_rem64(uint64_t hi, uint64_t lo, uint64_t y)` – Rem64 returns the remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y.
- `uint32_t bits_rem32(uint32_t hi, uint32_t lo, uint32_t y)` – Rem32 returns the remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y.
- `Uint bits_rem(Uint hi, Uint lo, Uint y)` – Rem returns the remainder of (hi, lo) divided by y.

## burrow/math/cmplx.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `math/cmplx` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Complex128 cmplx_add(Complex128 x, Complex128 y)`
- `Complex128 cmplx_sub(Complex128 x, Complex128 y)`
- `Complex128 cmplx_neg(Complex128 x)`
- `bool cmplx_eq(Complex128 x, Complex128 y)` – Go's ==, which is true for +0 against -0 and false for NaN against anything.
- `Complex128 cmplx_mul(Complex128 x, Complex128 y)` – x\*y, as (a\*c - b\*d) + (a\*d + b\*c)i with each product rounded on its own.
- `Complex128 cmplx_div(Complex128 n, Complex128 m)` – n/m, which does not fail for a zero m: 1/0 is an infinity, as in Go.
- `double cmplx_abs(Complex128 x)` – Abs returns the absolute value (also called the modulus) of x.
- `double cmplx_phase(Complex128 x)` – Phase returns the phase (also called the argument) of x.
- `double cmplx_polar(Complex128 x, double *theta)` – Polar returns the absolute value r and phase θ of x, such that x = r \* e\*\*θi.
- `Complex128 cmplx_rect(double r, double theta)` – Rect returns the complex number x with polar coordinates r, θ.
- `Complex128 cmplx_conj(Complex128 x)` – Conj returns the complex conjugate of x.
- `bool cmplx_is_inf(Complex128 x)` – IsInf reports whether either real(x) or imag(x) is an infinity.
- `bool cmplx_is_nan(Complex128 x)` – IsNaN reports whether either real(x) or imag(x) is NaN and neither is an infinity.
- `Complex128 cmplx_inf(void)` – Inf returns a complex infinity, complex(+Inf, +Inf).
- `Complex128 cmplx_nan(void)` – NaN returns a complex “not-a-number” value.
- `Complex128 cmplx_sqrt(Complex128 x)` – Sqrt returns the square root of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_pow(Complex128 x, Complex128 y)` – Pow returns x\*\*y, the base-x exponential of y.
- `Complex128 cmplx_exp(Complex128 x)` – Exp returns e\*\*x, the base-e exponential of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_log(Complex128 x)` – Log returns the natural logarithm of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_log10(Complex128 x)` – Log10 returns the decimal logarithm of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_sin(Complex128 x)` – Sin returns the sine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_cos(Complex128 x)` – Cos returns the cosine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_tan(Complex128 x)` – Tan returns the tangent of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_cot(Complex128 x)` – Cot returns the cotangent of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_asin(Complex128 x)` – Asin returns the inverse sine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_acos(Complex128 x)` – Acos returns the inverse cosine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_atan(Complex128 x)` – Atan returns the inverse tangent of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_sinh(Complex128 x)` – Sinh returns the hyperbolic sine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_cosh(Complex128 x)` – Cosh returns the hyperbolic cosine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_tanh(Complex128 x)` – Tanh returns the hyperbolic tangent of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_asinh(Complex128 x)` – Asinh returns the inverse hyperbolic sine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_acosh(Complex128 x)` – Acosh returns the inverse hyperbolic cosine of x.
- `Complex128 cmplx_atanh(Complex128 x)` – Atanh returns the inverse hyperbolic tangent of x.

- `void math_rand_rand_seed(MathRandRand *r, int64_t seed)` – Seed uses the provided seed value to initialize the generator to a deterministic state.

- `Int math_rand_rand_int(MathRandRand *r)` – Int returns a non-negative pseudo-random int.

- `uint64_t math_rand_rand_uint64(MathRandRand *r)` – Uint64 returns a pseudo-random 64-bit value as a uint64.
- `int64_t math_rand_rand_int63n(MathRandRand *r, int64_t n)` – Int63n returns, as an int64, a non-negative pseudo-random number in the half-open interval \[0,n).

- `double math_rand_rand_exp_float64(MathRandRand *r)` – ExpFloat64 returns an exponentially distributed float64 in the range (0, +\[math.MaxFloat64\]\] with an exponential distribution whose rate parameter (lambda) is 1 and whose mean is 1/lambda (1).
- `Slice math_rand_rand_perm(MathRandRand *r, Alloc *a, Int n)` – Perm returns, as a slice of n ints, a pseudo-random permutation of the integers in the half-open interval \[0,n).
- `void math_rand_rand_shuffle(MathRandRand *r, Int n, SwapFunc swap)` – Shuffle pseudo-randomizes the order of elements.
- `Int math_rand_rand_read(MathRandRand *r, Slice p, Error *err)` – Read generates len(p) random bytes and writes them into p.
- `MathRandZipf *math_rand_new_zipf(Alloc *a, MathRandRand *r, double s, double v, uint64_t imax)` – NewZipf returns a Zipf variate generator.
- `uint64_t math_rand_zipf_uint64(MathRandZipf *z)` – Uint64 returns a value drawn from the Zipf distribution described by the Zipf object.
- `int64_t math_rand_int63(void)` – Int63 returns a non-negative pseudo-random 63-bit integer as an int64 from the default Source.
- `int32_t math_rand_int31(void)` – Int31 returns a non-negative pseudo-random 31-bit integer as an int32 from the default Source.
- `Int math_rand_int(void)` – Int returns a non-negative pseudo-random int from the default Source.
- `uint32_t math_rand_uint32(void)` – Uint32 returns a pseudo-random 32-bit value as a uint32 from the default Source.
- `uint64_t math_rand_uint64(void)` – Uint64 returns a pseudo-random 64-bit value as a uint64 from the default Source.
- `int64_t math_rand_int63n(int64_t n)` – Int63n returns, as an int64, a non-negative pseudo-random number in the half-open interval \[0,n) from the default Source.

- `Int math_rand_intn(Int n)` – Intn returns, as an int, a non-negative pseudo-random number in the half-open interval \[0,n) from the default Source.
- `double math_rand_float64(void)` – Float64 returns, as a float64, a pseudo-random number in the half-open interval \[0.0,1.0) from the default Source.

- `double math_rand_exp_float64(void)` – ExpFloat64 returns an exponentially distributed float64 in the range (0, +\[math.MaxFloat64\]\] with an exponential distribution whose rate parameter (lambda) is 1 and whose mean is 1/lambda (1) from the default Source.
- `Slice math_rand_perm(Alloc *a, Int n)` – Perm returns, as a slice of n ints, a pseudo-random permutation of the integers in the half-open interval \[0,n) from the default Source.
- `void math_rand_shuffle(Int n, SwapFunc swap)` – Shuffle pseudo-randomizes the order of elements using the default Source.

## burrow/mem.h

- `void *mem_alloc(Alloc *a, size_t size, size_t align)` – Zeroed, because Go's zero value rule is not a convention there, it is the language, and code ported from Go assumes it everywhere.
- `void *mem_alloc_nozero(Alloc *a, size_t size, size_t align)` – Not zeroed.
- `void *mem_alloc_array(Alloc *a, size_t n, size_t size, size_t align)` – n \* size with the multiplication checked, because an attacker controlled element count that wraps is the oldest heap overflow there is.
- `void *mem_realloc(Alloc *a, void *p, size_t old, size_t nsz, size_t align)`
- `void mem_free(Alloc *a, void *p, size_t size, size_t align)`
- `void mem_reset(Alloc *a)` – Gives everything back at once.
- `bool mem_can_reset(Alloc *a)`
- `AllocStats mem_stats(Alloc *a)`
- `void mem_set_oom(Alloc *a, OomFunc fn, void *ctx)` – Installs the out of memory handler for one allocator.

## burrow/mem/arena.h

- `void arena_init(Arena *ar, Alloc *parent, size_t chunk_size)` – parent is where chunks come from.
- `Alloc *arena_allocator(Arena *ar)` – The allocator to pass to everything else.
- `void arena_reset(Arena *ar)` – Everything allocated becomes invalid, and the chunks are kept for reuse, so the next round of work does no allocation from the parent at all.
- `ArenaMark arena_mark(Arena *ar)` – Where the arena is now.
- `void arena_release(Arena *ar, ArenaMark m)` – Everything allocated since the mark becomes invalid.
- `void arena_free(Arena *ar)` – Everything allocated becomes invalid and every chunk goes back to the parent.

## burrow/mem/fixed.h

- `void fixed_init(Fixed *fx, void *buf, size_t size)` – The buffer stays yours.
- `Alloc *fixed_allocator(Fixed *fx)`
- `void fixed_reset(Fixed *fx)` – Everything allocated becomes invalid and the whole buffer is available again.
- `size_t fixed_available(const Fixed *fx)` – How many bytes are left, which is the question people actually ask of this one.

## burrow/mem/gc.h

- `bool gc_available(void)` – Whether this build has the collector in it.
- `Alloc *gc_allocator(void)` – The collecting allocator, or NULL if gc_available is false.
- `void gc_collect(void)` – Collect now.

## burrow/mem/heap.h

- `Alloc *heap_allocator(void)` – The process allocator.

## burrow/mem/track.h

- `void track_init(Track *tr, Alloc *under)` – under is where the memory actually comes from and it has to outlive the Track.
- `Alloc *track_allocator(Track *tr)`
- `void track_set_meta(Track *tr, Alloc *meta)` – Where the bookkeeping is kept, which is not the allocator being tracked.
- `void track_set_quarantine(Track *tr, size_t bytes)` – How much freed memory to hold on to before releasing the oldest, in bytes.
- `void track_on_fault(Track *tr, TrackReporter fn, void *ctx)` – Called for every fault.
- `uint64_t track_check(Track *tr)` – Reports every block that is still live as a leak, then returns the total number of faults seen over the Track's whole life, leaks included.
- `uint64_t track_faults(const Track *tr)` – The count without the leak sweep, for a test that wants to look partway through.
- `size_t track_live(const Track *tr)` – How many blocks are live right now, which is the other thing a test asks.
- `void track_free(Track *tr)` – Releases the quarantine, checking the poison on the way out, and frees the bookkeeping.
- `Alloc *track_note(Alloc *a, const char *file, int line)`

## burrow/mime.h
