- `Slice net_interface_addrs_of(const NetInterface *ifi, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Addrs returns a list of unicast interface addresses for a specific interface.
- `Slice net_interface_multicast_addrs(const NetInterface *ifi, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MulticastAddrs returns a list of multicast, joined group addresses for a specific interface.
- `NetAddr net_ip_net_as_addr(const NetIPNet *n)` – n as a NetAddr, which points at n, and nil for a NULL n.
- `NetConn net_file_conn(Alloc *a, OsFile *f, Error *err)` – FileConn returns a copy of the network connection corresponding to the open file f.
- `NetListener net_file_listener(Alloc *a, OsFile *f, Error *err)` – FileListener returns a copy of the network listener corresponding to the open file f.
- `NetPacketConn net_file_packet_conn(Alloc *a, OsFile *f, Error *err)` – FilePacketConn returns a copy of the packet network connection corresponding to the open file f.

## burrow/net/http.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `net/http` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str http_status_text(Int code)` – StatusText returns a text for the HTTP status code.
- `HttpHeader http_header_make(Alloc *a)` – make(http.Header).
- `bool http_header_add(HttpHeader h, Str key, Str value)` – Add adds the key, value pair to the header.
- `bool http_header_set(HttpHeader h, Str key, Str value)` – Set sets the header entries associated with key to the single element value.
- `Str http_header_get(HttpHeader h, Str key)` – Get gets the first value associated with the given key.
- `Slice http_header_values(HttpHeader h, Str key)` – Values returns all values associated with the given key.
- `void http_header_del(HttpHeader h, Str key)` – Del deletes the values associated with key.
- `Error http_header_write(HttpHeader h, IoWriter w)` – Write writes a header in wire format.
- `Error http_header_write_subset(HttpHeader h, IoWriter w, Map *exclude)` – WriteSubset writes a header in wire format.
- `HttpHeader http_header_clone(Alloc *a, HttpHeader h)` – Clone returns a copy of h or nil if h is nil.
- `Str http_canonical_header_key(Alloc *a, Str s)` – CanonicalHeaderKey returns the canonical format of the header key s.
- `Time http_parse_time(Alloc *a, Str text, Error *err)` – ParseTime parses a time header (such as the Date: header), trying each of the three formats allowed by HTTP/1.1: TimeFormat, time.RFC850, and time.ANSIC.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_COOKIE`
- `Slice http_parse_cookie(Alloc *a, Str line, Error *err)` – ParseCookie parses a Cookie header value and returns all the cookies which were set in it.
- `HttpCookie http_parse_set_cookie(Alloc *a, Str line, Error *err)` – ParseSetCookie parses a Set-Cookie header value and returns a cookie.
- `void http_cookie_free(Alloc *a, HttpCookie *c)` – Gives back the unparsed Slice of a cookie from http_parse_set_cookie.
- `Str http_cookie_string(Alloc *a, const HttpCookie *c)` – String returns the serialization of the cookie for use in a Cookie header (if only Name and Value are set) or a Set-Cookie response header (if other fields are set).
- `Error http_cookie_valid(const HttpCookie *c)` – Valid reports whether the cookie is valid.
- `void http_cookie_jar_set_cookies(HttpCookieJar j, const Url *u, Slice cookies)` – SetCookies handles the receipt of the cookies in a reply for the given URL.
- `Slice http_cookie_jar_cookies(HttpCookieJar j, Alloc *a, const Url *u)` – Cookies returns the cookies to send in a request for the given URL.
- `extern const IoReadCloser http_no_body` – NoBody is an io.ReadCloser with no bytes.
- `extern const Error http_err_body_read_after_close` – ErrBodyReadAfterClose is returned when reading a Request or Response Body after the body has been closed.
- `HttpRequest *http_read_request(Alloc *a, BufioReader *b, Error *err)` – ReadRequest reads and parses an incoming request from b.
- `void http_request_free(HttpRequest *r)` – Gives back a request http_read_request made, and the memory of its body, so the body is not to be read after this.
- `bool http_request_proto_at_least(const HttpRequest *r, Int major, Int minor)` – ProtoAtLeast reports whether the HTTP protocol used in the request is at least major.minor.
- `Str http_request_user_agent(const HttpRequest *r)` – UserAgent returns the client's User-Agent, if sent in the request.
- `Str http_request_referer(const HttpRequest *r)` – Referer returns the referring URL, if sent in the request.
- `bool http_request_basic_auth(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str *username, Str *password)` – BasicAuth returns the username and password provided in the request's Authorization header, if the request uses HTTP Basic Authentication.
- `bool http_request_set_basic_auth(HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str username, Str password)` – SetBasicAuth sets the request's Authorization header to use HTTP Basic Authentication with the provided username and password.
- `extern const Error http_err_no_cookie` – ErrNoCookie is returned by Request's Cookie method when a cookie is not found.
- `Slice http_request_cookies(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a)` – Cookies parses and returns the HTTP cookies sent with the request.
- `Slice http_request_cookies_named(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str name)` – CookiesNamed parses and returns the named HTTP cookies sent with the request or an empty slice if none matched.
- `HttpCookie http_request_cookie(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Cookie returns the named cookie provided in the request or ErrNoCookie if not found.
- `bool http_request_add_cookie(HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, const HttpCookie *c)` – AddCookie adds a cookie to the request.
- `HttpRequest *http_new_request(Alloc *a, Str method, Str url, IoReader body, Error *err)` – NewRequest wraps NewRequestWithContext using context.Background.
- `HttpRequest *http_new_request_with_context(Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str method, Str url, IoReader body, Error *err)` – NewRequestWithContext returns a new Request given a method, URL, and optional body.
- `Context http_request_context(const HttpRequest *r)` – Context returns the request's context.
- `HttpRequest *http_request_with_context(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Context ctx)` – WithContext returns a shallow copy of r with its context changed to ctx.
- `Error http_request_write(HttpRequest *r, IoWriter w)` – Write writes an HTTP/1.1 request, which is the header and body, in wire format.
- `Error http_request_write_proxy(HttpRequest *r, IoWriter w)` – WriteProxy is like Request.Write but writes the request in the form expected by an HTTP proxy.
- `HttpRequest *http_request_clone(const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Context ctx)` – Clone returns a deep copy of r with its context changed to ctx.
- `extern const Error http_err_missing_file` – ErrMissingFile is returned by FormFile when the provided file field name is either not present in the request or not a file field.
- `Error http_request_parse_form(HttpRequest *r)` – ParseForm populates r.Form and r.PostForm.
- `Error http_request_parse_multipart_form(HttpRequest *r, int64_t max_memory)` – ParseMultipartForm parses a request body as multipart/form-data.
- `Str http_request_form_value(HttpRequest *r, Str key)` – FormValue returns the first value for the named component of the query.
- `Str http_request_post_form_value(HttpRequest *r, Str key)` – PostFormValue returns the first value for the named component of the POST, PUT, or PATCH request body.
- `MultipartFile *http_request_form_file(HttpRequest *r, Str key, MultipartFileHeader **fh, Error *err)` – FormFile returns the first file for the provided form key.
- `MultipartReader *http_request_multipart_reader(HttpRequest *r, Error *err)` – MultipartReader returns a MIME multipart reader if this is a multipart/form-data or a multipart/mixed POST request, else returns nil and an error.
- `Str http_request_path_value(const HttpRequest *r, Str name)` – PathValue returns the value for the named path wildcard in the ServeMux pattern that matched the request.
- `bool http_request_set_path_value(HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Str value)` – SetPathValue sets name to value, so that subsequent calls to r.PathValue(name) return value.
- `bool http_parse_http_version(Str vers, Int *major, Int *minor)` – ParseHTTPVersion parses an HTTP version string according to RFC 7230, section 2.6.
- `extern const Error http_err_no_location` – ErrNoLocation is returned by the Response.Location method when no Location header is present.
- `HttpResponse *http_read_response(Alloc *a, BufioReader *r, HttpRequest *req, Error *err)` – ReadResponse reads and returns an HTTP response from r.
- `void http_response_free(HttpResponse *r)` – Gives back a response http_read_response made, and its body, after running its on_free.
- `Error http_response_write(HttpResponse *r, IoWriter w)` – Write writes r to w in the HTTP/1.x server response format, including the status line, headers, body, and optional trailer.
- `bool http_response_body_writer(const HttpResponse *r, IoWriter *w)` – Whether r's body can be written to as well as read, which is Go's resp.Body.(io.ReadWriteCloser), as the body of a 101 Switching Protocols response from HttpTransport can.
- `bool http_response_proto_at_least(const HttpResponse *r, Int major, Int minor)` – ProtoAtLeast reports whether the HTTP protocol used in the response is at least major.minor.
- `Url *http_response_location(const HttpResponse *r, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Location returns the URL of the response's "Location" header, if present.
- `Slice http_response_cookies(const HttpResponse *r, Alloc *a)` – Cookies parses and returns the cookies set in the Set-Cookie headers.
- `IoWriter http_response_writer_as_io_writer(HttpResponseWriter w)` – The writer as the IoWriter it embeds.
- `HttpHeader http_response_writer_header(HttpResponseWriter w)` – Header returns the header map that will be sent by ResponseWriter.WriteHeader.
- `Int http_response_writer_write(HttpResponseWriter w, Slice p, Error *err)` – Write writes the data to the connection as part of an HTTP reply.
- `void http_response_writer_write_header(HttpResponseWriter w, Int status_code)` – WriteHeader sends an HTTP response header with the provided status code.
- `extern const Error http_err_body_not_allowed` – ErrBodyNotAllowed is returned by ResponseWriter.Write calls when the HTTP method or response code does not permit a body.
- `void http_handler_serve_http(HttpHandler h, HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r)` – Handler.ServeHTTP.
- `void http_handler_func_serve_http(HttpHandlerFunc f, HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r)` – ServeHTTP calls f(w, r).
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_HANDLER_FUNC`
- `HttpHandler http_handler_func_as_handler(HttpHandlerFunc *f)` – HandlerFunc(f) as a Handler, which calls f.
- `void http_error(HttpResponseWriter w, Str error, Int code)` – Error replies to the request with the specified error message and HTTP code.
- `void http_not_found(HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r)` – NotFound replies to the request with an HTTP 404 not found error.
- `HttpHandler http_not_found_handler(void)` – NotFoundHandler returns a simple request handler that replies to each request with a “404 page not found” reply.
- `void http_redirect(HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r, Str url, Int code)` – Redirect replies to the request with a redirect to url, which may be a path relative to the request path.
- `bool http_set_cookie(HttpResponseWriter w, const HttpCookie *c)` – SetCookie adds a Set-Cookie header to the provided ResponseWriter's headers.
- `HttpHandler http_redirect_handler(Alloc *a, Str url, Int code)` – RedirectHandler returns a request handler that redirects each request it receives to the given url using the given status code.
- `HttpHandler http_strip_prefix(Alloc *a, Str prefix, HttpHandler h)` – StripPrefix returns a handler that serves HTTP requests by removing the given prefix from the request URL's Path (and RawPath if set) and invoking the handler h.
- `HttpServeMux *http_new_serve_mux(Alloc *a)` – NewServeMux allocates and returns a new ServeMux.
- `void http_serve_mux_free(HttpServeMux *mux)` – Gives back what the mux holds, and the mux itself if http_new_serve_mux made it.
- `extern HttpServeMux *const http_default_serve_mux` – DefaultServeMux is the default ServeMux used by Serve.
- `#define http_serve_mux_handle(mux, pattern, h)` – Handle registers the handler for the given pattern.
- `#define http_serve_mux_handle_func(mux, pattern, f)` – HandleFunc registers the handler function for the given pattern.
- `#define http_handle(pattern, h)` – Handle registers the handler for the given pattern in DefaultServeMux.
- `#define http_handle_func(pattern, f)` – HandleFunc registers the handler function for the given pattern in DefaultServeMux.
- `HttpHandler http_serve_mux_handler(HttpServeMux *mux, const HttpRequest *r, Alloc *a, Str *pattern)` – Handler returns the handler to use for the given request, consulting r.Method, r.Host, and r.URL.Path.
- `void http_serve_mux_serve_http(HttpServeMux *mux, HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r)` – ServeHTTP dispatches the request to the handler whose pattern most closely matches the request URL.
- `HttpHandler http_serve_mux_as_handler(HttpServeMux *mux)` – The mux as a Handler, which calls http_serve_mux_serve_http.
- `HttpCrossOriginProtection *http_new_cross_origin_protection(Alloc *a)` – NewCrossOriginProtection returns a new CrossOriginProtection value.
- `void http_cross_origin_protection_free(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c)` – Not in Go.
- `Error http_cross_origin_protection_add_trusted_origin(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c, Str origin)` – AddTrustedOrigin allows all requests with an Origin header which exactly matches the given value.
- `void http_cross_origin_protection_add_insecure_bypass_pattern(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c, Str pattern)` – AddInsecureBypassPattern permits all requests that match the given pattern.
- `bool http_cross_origin_protection_set_deny_handler(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c, HttpHandler h)` – SetDenyHandler sets a handler to invoke when a request is rejected.
- `Error http_cross_origin_protection_check(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c, const HttpRequest *req)` – Check applies cross-origin checks to a request.
- `HttpHandler http_cross_origin_protection_handler(HttpCrossOriginProtection *c, HttpHandler h)` – Handler returns a handler that applies cross-origin checks before invoking the handler h.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_DIR`
- `HttpFile http_dir_open(HttpDir d, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open implements FileSystem using os.Open, opening files for reading rooted and relative to the directory d.
- `HttpFileSystem http_dir_as_file_system(const HttpDir *d)` – d as an HttpFileSystem, borrowing it.
- `HttpFileSystem http_fs(Alloc *a, Fs fsys)` – FS converts fsys to a FileSystem implementation, for use with FileServer and NewFileTransport.
- `HttpHandler http_file_server(Alloc *a, HttpFileSystem root)` – FileServer returns a handler that serves HTTP requests with the contents of the file system rooted at root.
- `HttpHandler http_file_server_fs(Alloc *a, Fs root)` – FileServerFS returns a handler that serves HTTP requests with the contents of the file system fsys.
- `void http_serve_content(HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r, Str name, Time modtime, IoReadSeeker content)` – ServeContent replies to the request using the content in the provided ReadSeeker.
- `void http_serve_file(HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r, Str name)` – ServeFile replies to the request with the contents of the named file or directory.
- `void http_serve_file_fs(HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r, Fs fsys, Str name)` – ServeFileFS replies to the request with the contents of the named file or directory from the file system fsys.
- `Str http_detect_content_type(Slice data)` – DetectContentType implements the algorithm described at https://mimesniff.spec.whatwg.org/ to determine the Content-Type of the given data.
- `bool http_protocols_http1(HttpProtocols p)` – HTTP1 reports whether p includes HTTP/1.
- `void http_protocols_set_http1(HttpProtocols *p, bool ok)` – SetHTTP1 adds or removes HTTP/1 from p.
- `bool http_protocols_http2(HttpProtocols p)` – HTTP2 reports whether p includes HTTP/2.
- `void http_protocols_set_http2(HttpProtocols *p, bool ok)` – SetHTTP2 adds or removes HTTP/2 from p.
- `bool http_protocols_unencrypted_http2(HttpProtocols p)` – UnencryptedHTTP2 reports whether p includes unencrypted HTTP/2.
- `void http_protocols_set_unencrypted_http2(HttpProtocols *p, bool ok)` – SetUnencryptedHTTP2 adds or removes unencrypted HTTP/2 from p.
- `Str http_protocols_string(HttpProtocols p)` – Protocols.String, such as "{HTTP1,HTTP2}".
- `extern const Error http_err_server_closed` – ErrServerClosed is returned by the Server.Serve, ServeTLS, ListenAndServe, and ListenAndServeTLS methods after a call to Server.Shutdown or Server.Close.
- `extern const Error http_err_hijacked` – ErrHijacked is returned by ResponseWriter.Write calls when the underlying connection has been hijacked using the Hijacker interface.
- `extern const Error http_err_content_length` – ErrContentLength is returned by ResponseWriter.Write calls when a Handler set a Content-Length response header with a declared size and then attempted to write more bytes than declared.
- `extern const Error http_err_abort_handler` – ErrAbortHandler is a sentinel panic value to abort a handler.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_PROTOCOL_ERROR`
- `Str http_protocol_error_error(const HttpProtocolError *pe)` – ProtocolError.Error, which is error_string.
- `bool http_protocol_error_is(const HttpProtocolError *pe, Error err)` – Is lets http.ErrNotSupported match errors.ErrUnsupported.
- `extern const Error http_err_not_supported` – ErrNotSupported indicates that a feature is not supported.
- `extern const Error http_err_missing_boundary` – ErrMissingBoundary is returned by Request.MultipartReader when the request's Content-Type does not include a "boundary" parameter.
- `extern const Error http_err_not_multipart` – ErrNotMultipart is returned by Request.MultipartReader when the request's Content-Type is not multipart/form-data.
- `extern const Error http_err_unexpected_trailer` – Deprecated: ErrUnexpectedTrailer is no longer returned by anything in the net/http package.
- `extern const Error http_err_header_too_long` – Deprecated: ErrHeaderTooLong is no longer returned by anything in the net/http package.
- `extern const Error http_err_short_body` – Deprecated: ErrShortBody is no longer returned by anything in the net/http package.
- `extern const Error http_err_missing_content_length` – Deprecated: ErrMissingContentLength is no longer returned by anything in the net/http package.
- `extern const Error http_err_write_after_flush` – Deprecated: ErrWriteAfterFlush is no longer returned by anything in the net/http package.
- `extern const Error http_err_line_too_long` – ErrLineTooLong is returned when reading request or response bodies with malformed chunked encoding.
- `Str http_conn_state_string(HttpConnState s)` – ConnState.String, such as "idle", and "" for a state that is not one.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_TLS_NEXT_PROTO_FUNC` – The type of an HttpTLSNextProtoFunc, for the values of a tls_next_proto map.
- `Error http_server_serve(HttpServer *s, NetListener l)` – Serve accepts incoming connections on the Listener l, creating a new service goroutine for each.
- `Error http_server_listen_and_serve(HttpServer *s)` – ListenAndServe listens on the TCP network address s.Addr and then calls Serve to handle requests on incoming connections.
- `Error http_server_serve_tls(HttpServer *s, NetListener l, Str cert_file, Str key_file)` – ServeTLS accepts incoming connections on the Listener l, creating a new service goroutine for each.
- `Error http_server_listen_and_serve_tls(HttpServer *s, Str cert_file, Str key_file)` – ListenAndServeTLS listens on the TCP network address s.Addr and then calls ServeTLS to handle requests on incoming TLS connections.
- `Error http_server_close(HttpServer *s)` – Close immediately closes all active net.Listeners and any connections in state StateNew, StateActive, or StateIdle.
- `Error http_server_shutdown(HttpServer *s, Context ctx)` – Shutdown gracefully shuts down the server without interrupting any active connections.
- `bool http_server_register_on_shutdown(HttpServer *s, Func f)` – RegisterOnShutdown registers a function to call on Server.Shutdown.
- `void http_server_set_keep_alives_enabled(HttpServer *s, bool v)` – SetKeepAlivesEnabled controls whether HTTP keep-alives are enabled.
- `void http_server_free(HttpServer *s)` – Waits for the goroutine of every connection the server has served to end, and for the functions http_server_shutdown started, and gives back what the server holds.
- `Error http_serve(NetListener l, HttpHandler handler)` – Serve accepts incoming HTTP connections on the listener l, creating a new service goroutine for each.
- `Error http_listen_and_serve(Str addr, HttpHandler handler)` – ListenAndServe listens on the TCP network address addr and then calls Serve with handler to handle requests on incoming connections.
- `Error http_serve_tls(NetListener l, HttpHandler handler, Str cert_file, Str key_file)` – ServeTLS accepts incoming HTTPS connections on the listener l, creating a new service goroutine for each.
- `Error http_listen_and_serve_tls(Str addr, Str cert_file, Str key_file, HttpHandler handler)` – ListenAndServeTLS acts identically to ListenAndServe, except that it expects HTTPS connections.
- `extern const Any http_server_context_key` – ServerContextKey is a context key.
- `extern const Any http_local_addr_context_key` – LocalAddrContextKey is a context key.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_SERVER`
- `HttpResponseController http_new_response_controller(HttpResponseWriter rw)` – NewResponseController creates a ResponseController for a request.
- `Error http_response_controller_flush(HttpResponseController *c)` – Flush flushes buffered data to the client.
- `NetConn http_response_controller_hijack(HttpResponseController *c, BufioReadWriter *buf, Error *err)` – Hijack lets the caller take over the connection.
- `Error http_response_controller_set_read_deadline(HttpResponseController *c, Time deadline)` – SetReadDeadline sets the deadline for reading the entire request, including the body.
- `Error http_response_controller_set_write_deadline(HttpResponseController *c, Time deadline)` – SetWriteDeadline sets the deadline for writing the response.
- `Error http_response_controller_enable_full_duplex(HttpResponseController *c)` – EnableFullDuplex indicates that the request handler will interleave reads from Request.Body with writes to the ResponseWriter.
- `HttpHandler http_timeout_handler(Alloc *a, HttpHandler h, Duration dt, Str msg)` – TimeoutHandler returns a Handler that runs h with the given time limit.
- `extern const Error http_err_handler_timeout` – ErrHandlerTimeout is returned on ResponseWriter Write calls in handlers which have timed out.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_MAX_BYTES_ERROR`
- `Str http_max_bytes_error_error(const HttpMaxBytesError *e)` – MaxBytesError.Error, "http: request body too large".
- `IoReadCloser http_max_bytes_reader(Alloc *a, HttpResponseWriter w, IoReadCloser r, int64_t n)` – MaxBytesReader is similar to io.LimitReader but is intended for limiting the size of incoming request bodies.
- `HttpHandler http_max_bytes_handler(Alloc *a, HttpHandler h, int64_t n)` – MaxBytesHandler returns a Handler that runs h with its ResponseWriter and Request.Body wrapped by a MaxBytesReader.
- `HttpHandler http_allow_query_semicolons(Alloc *a, HttpHandler h)` – AllowQuerySemicolons returns a handler that serves requests by converting any unescaped semicolons in the URL query to ampersands, and invoking the handler h.
- `HttpResponse *http_round_tripper_round_trip(HttpRoundTripper rt, HttpRequest *req, Error *err)` – RoundTrip executes a single HTTP transaction, returning a Response for the provided Request.
- `extern const Error http_err_skip_alt_protocol` – ErrSkipAltProtocol is a sentinel error value defined by Transport.RegisterProtocol.
- `HttpRoundTripper http_new_file_transport(Alloc *a, HttpFileSystem fs)` – NewFileTransport returns a new RoundTripper, serving the provided FileSystem.
- `HttpRoundTripper http_new_file_transport_fs(Alloc *a, Fs fsys)` – NewFileTransportFS returns a new RoundTripper, serving the provided file system fsys.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_HTTP_TRANSPORT_TLS_NEXT_PROTO_FUNC` – The type of an HttpTransportTLSNextProtoFunc, for the values of a tls_next_proto map.
- `HttpResponse *http_transport_round_trip(HttpTransport *t, HttpRequest *req, Error *err)` – RoundTrip implements the RoundTripper interface.
- `HttpRoundTripper http_transport_as_round_tripper(HttpTransport *t)` – The transport as an HttpRoundTripper.
- `void http_transport_close_idle_connections(HttpTransport *t)` – CloseIdleConnections closes any connections which were previously connected from previous requests but are now sitting idle in a "keep-alive" state.
- `void http_transport_cancel_request(HttpTransport *t, HttpRequest *req)` – CancelRequest cancels an in-flight request by closing its connection.
- `bool http_transport_register_protocol(HttpTransport *t, Str scheme, HttpRoundTripper rt)` – RegisterProtocol registers a new protocol with scheme.
- `HttpTransport http_transport_clone(HttpTransport *t, Alloc *a)` – Clone returns a deep copy of t's exported fields.
- `void http_transport_free(HttpTransport *t)` – Closes the idle connections and waits for every connection, dial and goroutine the transport has going to end.
- `extern HttpTransport *const http_default_transport` – DefaultTransport is the default implementation of Transport and is used by DefaultClient.
- `Url *http_proxy_from_environment(HttpRequest *req, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ProxyFromEnvironment returns the URL of the proxy to use for a given request, as indicated by the environment variables HTTP_PROXY, HTTPS_PROXY and NO_PROXY (or the lowercase versions thereof).
- `HttpProxyFunc http_proxy_from_environment_func(void)` – http_proxy_from_environment as the HttpProxyFunc a transport takes.
- `HttpProxyFunc http_proxy_url(const Url *u)` – ProxyURL returns a proxy function (for use in a Transport) that always returns the same URL.
- `extern const Error http_err_use_last_response` – ErrUseLastResponse can be returned by Client.CheckRedirect hooks to control how redirects are processed.
- `extern const Error http_err_scheme_mismatch` – ErrSchemeMismatch is returned when a server returns an HTTP response to an HTTPS client.
- `HttpResponse *http_client_do(HttpClient *c, HttpRequest *req, Error *err)` – Do sends an HTTP request and returns an HTTP response, following policy (such as redirects, cookies, auth) as configured on the client.
- `HttpResponse *http_client_get(HttpClient *c, Str url, Error *err)` – Get issues a GET to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_client_head(HttpClient *c, Str url, Error *err)` – Head issues a HEAD to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_client_post(HttpClient *c, Str url, Str content_type, IoReader body, Error *err)` – Post issues a POST to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_client_post_form(HttpClient *c, Str url, UrlValues data, Error *err)` – PostForm issues a POST to the specified URL, with data's keys and values URL-encoded as the request body.
- `void http_client_close_idle_connections(HttpClient *c)` – CloseIdleConnections closes any connections on its Transport which were previously connected from previous requests but are now sitting idle in a "keep-alive" state.
- `extern HttpClient *const http_default_client` – DefaultClient is the default Client and is used by Get, Head, and Post.
- `HttpResponse *http_get(Str url, Error *err)` – Get issues a GET to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_head(Str url, Error *err)` – Head issues a HEAD to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_post(Str url, Str content_type, IoReader body, Error *err)` – Post issues a POST to the specified URL.
- `HttpResponse *http_post_form(Str url, UrlValues data, Error *err)` – PostForm issues a POST to the specified URL, with data's keys and values URL-encoded as the request body.

## burrow/net/http/cgi.h

- `HttptraceClientTrace *httptrace_context_client_trace(Context ctx)` – ContextClientTrace returns the ClientTrace associated with the provided context.

- `void httptrace_client_trace_get_conn(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Str host_port)` – GetConn is called before a connection is created or retrieved from an idle pool.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_got_conn(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, HttptraceGotConnInfo info)` – GotConn is called after a successful connection is obtained.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_put_idle_conn(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Error err)` – PutIdleConn is called when the connection is returned to the idle pool.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_got_first_response_byte(const HttptraceClientTrace *t)` – GotFirstResponseByte is called when the first byte of the response headers is available.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_got100_continue(const HttptraceClientTrace *t)` – Got100Continue is called if the server replies with a "100 Continue" response.
- `Error httptrace_client_trace_got1xx_response(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Int code, TextprotoMIMEHeader header)` – Got1xxResponse is called for each 1xx informational response header returned before the final non-1xx response.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_dns_start(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, HttptraceDNSStartInfo info)` – DNSStart is called when a DNS lookup begins.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_dns_done(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, HttptraceDNSDoneInfo info)` – DNSDone is called when a DNS lookup ends.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_connect_start(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Str network, Str addr)` – ConnectStart is called when a new connection's Dial begins.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_connect_done(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Str network, Str addr, Error err)` – ConnectDone is called when a new connection's Dial completes.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_tls_handshake_start(const HttptraceClientTrace *t)` – TLSHandshakeStart is called when the TLS handshake is started.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_tls_handshake_done(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, TlsConnectionState state, Error err)` – TLSHandshakeDone is called after the TLS handshake with either the successful handshake's connection state, or a non-nil error on handshake failure.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_wrote_header_field(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, Str key, Slice values)` – WroteHeaderField is called after the Transport has written each request header.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_wrote_headers(const HttptraceClientTrace *t)` – WroteHeaders is called after the Transport has written all request headers.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_wait100_continue(const HttptraceClientTrace *t)` – Wait100Continue is called if the Request specified "Expect: 100-continue" and the Transport has written the request headers but is waiting for "100 Continue" from the server before writing the request body.
- `void httptrace_client_trace_wrote_request(const HttptraceClientTrace *t, HttptraceWroteRequestInfo info)` – WroteRequest is called with the result of writing the request and any body.

- `bool netip_prefix_is_single_ip(NetipPrefix p)` – IsSingleIP reports whether p contains exactly one IP.
- `bool netip_prefix_eq(NetipPrefix p, NetipPrefix p2)` – Go's p == p2.
- `NetipPrefix netip_parse_prefix(Str s, Error *err)` – ParsePrefix parses s as an IP address prefix.
- `NetipPrefix netip_must_parse_prefix(Str s)` – MustParsePrefix calls ParsePrefix(s) and panics on error.
- `Int netip_prefix_compare(NetipPrefix p, NetipPrefix p2)` – Compare returns an integer comparing two prefixes.
- `NetipPrefix netip_prefix_masked(NetipPrefix p)` – Masked returns p in its canonical form, with all but the high p.Bits() bits of p.Addr() masked off.
- `bool netip_prefix_contains(NetipPrefix p, NetipAddr ip)` – Contains reports whether the network p includes ip.
- `bool netip_prefix_overlaps(NetipPrefix p, NetipPrefix o)` – Overlaps reports whether p and o contain any IP addresses in common.
- `Str netip_prefix_string(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a)` – String returns the CIDR notation of p: "\<ip\>/\<bits\>".
- `Slice netip_prefix_append_to(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – AppendTo appends a text encoding of p, as generated by Prefix.MarshalText, to b and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice netip_prefix_append_text(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements the encoding.TextAppender interface.
- `Slice netip_prefix_marshal_text(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements the encoding.TextMarshaler interface.
- `Error netip_prefix_unmarshal_text(NetipPrefix *p, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements the encoding.TextUnmarshaler interface.
- `Slice netip_prefix_append_binary(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendBinary implements the encoding.BinaryAppender interface.
- `Slice netip_prefix_marshal_binary(NetipPrefix p, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalBinary implements the encoding.BinaryMarshaler interface.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_RPC_REQUEST` – The descriptors gob sends the two headers with, under Go's names, Request and Response, and Go's field names, so a Go client or server can talk to this one.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_RPC_SERVER_ERROR`

- `Error rpc_server_error_as_error(RpcServerError e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, made in a.
- `extern const Error rpc_err_shutdown` – rpc.ErrShutdown, from a call on a client that has been closed or whose connection has gone.
- `RpcServer *rpc_new_server(Alloc *a)` – NewServer returns a new Server.
- `void rpc_server_free(RpcServer *s)` – Gives back the server and what it holds, once nothing is using it.
- `extern RpcServer *const rpc_default_server` – DefaultServer is the default instance of \*Server.
- `Error rpc_server_register(RpcServer *s, Any rcvr)` – Register publishes in the server the set of methods of the receiver value that satisfy the following conditions: - exported method of exported type - two arguments, both of exported type - the second argument is a pointer - one return value, of type error
- `Error rpc_server_register_name(RpcServer *s, Str name, Any rcvr)` – RegisterName is like Register but uses the provided name for the type instead of the receiver's concrete type.
- `void rpc_server_serve_conn(RpcServer *s, IoReadWriteCloser conn)` – ServeConn runs the server on a single connection.
- `void rpc_server_serve_codec(RpcServer *s, RpcServerCodec codec)` – ServeCodec is like ServeConn but uses the specified codec to decode requests and encode responses.
- `Error rpc_server_serve_request(RpcServer *s, RpcServerCodec codec)` – ServeRequest is like ServeCodec but synchronously serves a single request.
- `void rpc_server_accept(RpcServer *s, NetListener lis)` – Accept accepts connections on the listener and serves requests for each incoming connection.
- `HttpHandler rpc_server_as_handler(RpcServer *s)` – Server.ServeHTTP, as a handler that takes a CONNECT request over and serves RPC on its connection.
- `void rpc_server_serve_http(RpcServer *s, HttpResponseWriter w, HttpRequest *r)` – ServeHTTP implements an http.Handler that answers RPC requests.
- `void rpc_server_handle_http(RpcServer *s, Str rpc_path, Str debug_path)` – HandleHTTP registers an HTTP handler for RPC messages on rpcPath, and a debugging handler on debugPath.
- `Error rpc_register(Any rcvr)` – Register publishes the receiver's methods in the DefaultServer.
- `Error rpc_register_name(Str name, Any rcvr)` – RegisterName is like Register but uses the provided name for the type instead of the receiver's concrete type.
- `void rpc_serve_conn(IoReadWriteCloser conn)` – ServeConn runs the DefaultServer on a single connection.
- `void rpc_serve_codec(RpcServerCodec codec)` – ServeCodec is like ServeConn but uses the specified codec to decode requests and encode responses.
- `Error rpc_serve_request(RpcServerCodec codec)` – ServeRequest is like ServeCodec but synchronously serves a single request.
- `void rpc_accept(NetListener lis)` – Accept accepts connections on the listener and serves requests to DefaultServer for each incoming connection.
- `void rpc_handle_http(void)` – HandleHTTP registers an HTTP handler for RPC messages to DefaultServer on DefaultRPCPath and a debugging handler on DefaultDebugPath.
- `void rpc_call_free(RpcCall *call)` – Gives back a call rpc_client_go made, once it is done, and the channel it made if it was not given one.
- `RpcClient *rpc_new_client(Alloc *a, IoReadWriteCloser conn)` – NewClient returns a new Client to handle requests to the set of services at the other end of the connection.
- `RpcClient *rpc_new_client_with_codec(Alloc *a, RpcClientCodec codec)` – NewClientWithCodec is like NewClient but uses the specified codec to encode requests and decode responses.
- `RpcClient *rpc_dial(Alloc *a, Str network, Str address, Error *err)` – Dial connects to an RPC server at the specified network address.
- `RpcClient *rpc_dial_http(Alloc *a, Str network, Str address, Error *err)` – DialHTTP connects to an HTTP RPC server at the specified network address listening on the default HTTP RPC path.
- `RpcClient *rpc_dial_http_path(Alloc *a, Str network, Str address, Str path, Error *err)` – DialHTTPPath connects to an HTTP RPC server at the specified network address and path.
- `Error rpc_client_close(RpcClient *c)` – Close calls the underlying codec's Close method.
- `void rpc_client_free(RpcClient *c)` – Closes the client if it is open, waits for the goroutine reading its responses to stop, and gives it back with its codec.
- `RpcCall *rpc_client_go(RpcClient *c, Alloc *a, Str service_method, Any args, Any reply, Chan *done)` – Go invokes the function asynchronously.
- `Error rpc_client_call(RpcClient *c, Alloc *a, Str service_method, Any args, Any reply)` – Call invokes the named function, waits for it to complete, and returns its error status.

## burrow/net/smtp.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `net/smtp` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `SmtpAuth smtp_plain_auth(Alloc *a, Str identity, Str username, Str password, Str host)` – PlainAuth returns an Auth that implements the PLAIN authentication mechanism as defined in RFC 4616.
- `SmtpAuth smtp_crammd5_auth(Alloc *a, Str username, Str secret)` – CRAMMD5Auth returns an Auth that implements the CRAM-MD5 authentication mechanism as defined in RFC 2195.
- `void smtp_auth_free(Alloc *a, SmtpAuth auth)` – Gives back an SmtpAuth from smtp_plain_auth or smtp_crammd5_auth to the a it came from.
- `SmtpClient *smtp_dial(Alloc *a, Str addr, Error *err)` – Dial returns a new Client connected to an SMTP server at addr.
- `SmtpClient *smtp_new_client(Alloc *a, NetConn conn, Str host, Error *err)` – NewClient returns a new Client using an existing connection and host as a server name to be used when authenticating.
- `Error smtp_client_close(SmtpClient *c)` – Close closes the connection.
- `void smtp_client_free(SmtpClient *c)` – Gives back c, the TLS connections smtp_client_start_tls made, and the connection too if smtp_dial made it, without closing anything else.
- `Error smtp_client_hello(SmtpClient *c, Str local_name)` – Hello sends a HELO or EHLO to the server as the given host name.
- `Error smtp_client_start_tls(SmtpClient *c, TlsConfig *config)` – StartTLS sends the STARTTLS command and encrypts all further communication.
- `TlsConnectionState smtp_client_tls_connection_state(SmtpClient *c, bool *ok)` – TLSConnectionState returns the client's TLS connection state.
- `Error smtp_client_verify(SmtpClient *c, Str addr)` – Verify checks the validity of an email address on the server.
- `Error smtp_client_auth(SmtpClient *c, SmtpAuth auth)` – Auth authenticates a client using the provided authentication mechanism.
- `Error smtp_client_mail(SmtpClient *c, Str from)` – Mail issues a MAIL command to the server using the provided email address.
- `Error smtp_client_rcpt(SmtpClient *c, Str to)` – Rcpt issues a RCPT command to the server using the provided email address.
- `IoWriteCloser smtp_client_data(SmtpClient *c, Error *err)` – Data issues a DATA command to the server and returns a writer that can be used to write the mail headers and body.
- `bool smtp_client_extension(SmtpClient *c, Str ext, Str *param)` – Extension reports whether an extension is support by the server.
- `Error smtp_client_reset(SmtpClient *c)` – Reset sends the RSET command to the server, aborting the current mail transaction.
- `Error smtp_client_noop(SmtpClient *c)` – Noop sends the NOOP command to the server.
- `Error smtp_client_quit(SmtpClient *c)` – Quit sends the QUIT command and closes the connection to the server.
- `Error smtp_send_mail(Str addr, SmtpAuth auth, Str from, Slice to, Slice msg)` – SendMail connects to the server at addr, switches to TLS if possible, authenticates with the optional mechanism a if possible, and then sends an email from address from, to addresses to, with message msg.

## burrow/os.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `os` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool os_is_path_separator(uint8_t c)` – IsPathSeparator reports whether c is a directory separator character.
- `extern const Error os_err_no_deadline` – "file type does not support deadline", from the deadline setters on a file that cannot have one, which is every file here for now.
- `extern const Error os_err_deadline_exceeded` – "i/o timeout", for a read or write that ran past its deadline.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_OS_LINK_ERROR`
- `Str os_link_error_error(const OsLinkError *e, Alloc *a)` – The text, built in a.
- `Error os_link_error_unwrap(const OsLinkError *e)` – e-\>err.
- `Error os_link_error_new(Alloc *a, Str op, Str old, Str new_, Error err)` – One you fill in with the names copied into a, the shape of fs_path_error_new.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_OS_SYSCALL_ERROR`
- `Str os_syscall_error_error(const OsSyscallError *e, Alloc *a)` – The text, built in a.
- `Error os_syscall_error_unwrap(const OsSyscallError *e)` – e-\>err.
- `bool os_syscall_error_timeout(const OsSyscallError *e)` – Timeout reports whether this error represents a timeout.
- `Error os_new_syscall_error(Alloc *a, Str syscall, Error err)` – NewSyscallError returns, as an error, a new SyscallError with the given system call name and error details.
- `bool os_is_exist(Error err)` – IsExist returns a boolean indicating whether its argument is known to report that a file or directory already exists.
- `bool os_is_not_exist(Error err)` – IsNotExist returns a boolean indicating whether its argument is known to report that a file or directory does not exist.
- `bool os_is_permission(Error err)` – IsPermission returns a boolean indicating whether its argument is known to report that permission is denied.
- `bool os_is_timeout(Error err)` – IsTimeout returns a boolean indicating whether its argument is known to report that a timeout occurred.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_OS_FILE`
- `OsFile *os_open(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file for reading.
- `OsFile *os_create(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Create creates or truncates the named file.
- `OsFile *os_open_file(Alloc *a, Str name, Int flag, OsFileMode perm, Error *err)` – OpenFile is the generalized open call; most users will use Open or Create instead.
- `OsFile *os_new_file(Alloc *a, Uintptr fd, Str name)` – NewFile returns a new File with the given file descriptor and name.
- `Str os_file_name(const OsFile *f)` – Name returns the name of the file as presented to Open.
- `Uintptr os_file_fd(OsFile *f)` – Fd returns the system file descriptor or handle referencing the open file.
- `Int os_file_read(OsFile *f, Slice p, Error *err)` – Read reads up to len(b) bytes from the File and stores them in b.
- `Int os_file_read_at(OsFile *f, Slice p, int64_t off, Error *err)` – ReadAt reads len(b) bytes from the File starting at byte offset off.
- `Int os_file_write(OsFile *f, Slice p, Error *err)` – Write writes len(b) bytes from b to the File.
- `Int os_file_write_string(OsFile *f, Str s, Error *err)` – WriteString is like Write, but writes the contents of string s rather than a slice of bytes.
- `Int os_file_write_at(OsFile *f, Slice p, int64_t off, Error *err)` – WriteAt writes len(b) bytes to the File starting at byte offset off.
- `int64_t os_file_seek(OsFile *f, int64_t offset, Int whence, Error *err)` – Seek sets the offset for the next Read or Write on file to offset, interpreted according to whence: 0 means relative to the origin of the file, 1 means relative to the current offset, and 2 means relative to the end.
- `OsFileInfo os_file_stat(OsFile *f, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Stat returns the FileInfo structure describing file.
- `Error os_file_sync(OsFile *f)` – Sync commits the current contents of the file to stable storage.
- `Error os_file_truncate(OsFile *f, int64_t size)` – Truncate changes the size of the file.
- `Error os_file_chmod(OsFile *f, OsFileMode mode)` – Chmod changes the mode of the file to mode.
- `Error os_file_chown(OsFile *f, Int uid, Int gid)` – Chown changes the numeric uid and gid of the named file.
- `Error os_file_chdir(OsFile *f)` – Chdir changes the current working directory to the file, which must be a directory.
- `OsFile *os_pipe(Alloc *a, OsFile **w, Error *err)` – Pipe returns a connected pair of Files; reads from r return bytes written to w.
- `Error os_file_set_deadline(OsFile *f, Time t)` – SetDeadline sets the read and write deadlines for a File.
- `Error os_file_set_read_deadline(OsFile *f, Time t)` – SetReadDeadline sets the deadline for future Read calls and any currently-blocked Read call.
- `Error os_file_set_write_deadline(OsFile *f, Time t)` – SetWriteDeadline sets the deadline for any future Write calls and any currently-blocked Write call.
- `Error os_file_close(OsFile *f)` – Close closes the File, rendering it unusable for I/O. On files that support SetDeadline, any pending I/O operations will be canceled and return immediately with an ErrClosed error.
- `void os_file_free(OsFile *f)` – Closes f if it is still open and gives its memory back.
- `IoReader os_file_as_io_reader(OsFile *f)` – f as the io interfaces, borrowing it.
- `IoWriter os_file_as_io_writer(OsFile *f)`
- `IoCloser os_file_as_io_closer(OsFile *f)`
- `IoReadCloser os_file_as_io_read_closer(OsFile *f)`
- `IoWriteCloser os_file_as_io_write_closer(OsFile *f)`
- `IoSeeker os_file_as_io_seeker(OsFile *f)`
- `IoReaderAt os_file_as_io_reader_at(OsFile *f)`
- `IoWriterAt os_file_as_io_writer_at(OsFile *f)`
- `IoStringWriter os_file_as_io_string_writer(OsFile *f)`
- `IoReadWriteSeeker os_file_as_io_read_write_seeker(OsFile *f)`
- `FsFile os_file_as_fs_file(OsFile *f)` – f as an fs.File, borrowing it, with os_file_read_dir as its read_dir.
- `int64_t os_file_read_from(OsFile *f, IoReader r, Error *err)` – ReadFrom implements io.ReaderFrom.
- `int64_t os_file_write_to(OsFile *f, IoWriter w, Error *err)` – WriteTo implements io.WriterTo.
- `SyscallRawConn os_file_syscall_conn(OsFile *f, Error *err)` – SyscallConn returns a raw file.
- `SyscallConn os_file_as_syscall_conn(OsFile *f)` – f as a SyscallConn, borrowing it, whose syscall_conn is os_file_syscall_conn.
- `OsFile *os_stdin_file(void)` – os.Stdin, os.Stdout and os.Stderr, opened the first time you ask.
- `OsFile *os_stdout_file(void)`
- `OsFile *os_stderr_file(void)`
- `OsFileInfo os_stat(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Stat returns a FileInfo describing the named file.
- `OsFileInfo os_lstat(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Lstat returns a FileInfo describing the named file.
- `bool os_same_file(OsFileInfo fi1, OsFileInfo fi2)` – SameFile reports whether fi1 and fi2 describe the same file.
- `Slice os_read_file(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – ReadFile reads the named file and returns the contents.
- `Error os_write_file(Str name, Slice data, OsFileMode perm)` – WriteFile writes data to the named file, creating it if necessary.
- `Error os_mkdir(Str name, OsFileMode perm)` – Mkdir creates a new directory with the specified name and permission bits (before umask).
- `Error os_remove(Str name)` – Remove removes the named file or directory.
- `Error os_rename(Str oldpath, Str newpath)` – Rename renames (moves) oldpath to newpath.
- `Error os_link(Str oldname, Str newname)` – Link creates newname as a hard link to the oldname file.
- `Error os_symlink(Str oldname, Str newname)` – Symlink creates newname as a symbolic link to oldname.
- `Str os_readlink(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Readlink returns the destination of the named symbolic link.
- `Error os_chmod(Str name, OsFileMode mode)` – Chmod changes the mode of the named file to mode.
- `Error os_chown(Str name, Int uid, Int gid)` – Chown changes the numeric uid and gid of the named file.
- `Error os_lchown(Str name, Int uid, Int gid)` – Lchown changes the numeric uid and gid of the named file.
- `Error os_chtimes(Str name, Time atime, Time mtime)` – Chtimes changes the access and modification times of the named file, similar to the Unix utime() or utimes() functions.
- `Error os_truncate(Str name, int64_t size)` – Truncate changes the size of the named file.
- `Slice os_file_read_dir(OsFile *f, Alloc *a, Int n, Error *err)` – ReadDir reads the contents of the directory associated with the file f and returns a slice of DirEntry values in directory order.
- `Slice os_file_readdir(OsFile *f, Alloc *a, Int n, Error *err)` – Readdir reads the contents of the directory associated with file and returns a slice of up to n FileInfo values, as would be returned by Lstat, in directory order.
- `Slice os_file_readdirnames(OsFile *f, Alloc *a, Int n, Error *err)` – Readdirnames reads the contents of the directory associated with file and returns a slice of up to n names of files in the directory, in directory order.
- `Slice os_read_dir(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – ReadDir reads the named directory, returning all its directory entries sorted by filename.
- `Error os_mkdir_all(Str path, OsFileMode perm)` – MkdirAll creates a directory named path, along with any necessary parents, and returns nil, or else returns an error.
- `Error os_remove_all(Str path)` – RemoveAll removes path and any children it contains.
- `Str os_temp_dir(Alloc *a)` – TempDir returns the default directory to use for temporary files.
- `OsFile *os_create_temp(Alloc *a, Str dir, Str pattern, Error *err)` – CreateTemp creates a new temporary file in the directory dir, opens the file for reading and writing, and returns the resulting file.
- `Str os_mkdir_temp(Alloc *a, Str dir, Str pattern, Error *err)` – MkdirTemp creates a new temporary directory in the directory dir and returns the pathname of the new directory.
- `Fs os_dir_fs(Alloc *a, Str dir)` – DirFS returns a file system (an fs.FS) for the tree of files rooted at the directory dir.
- `Error os_copy_fs(Str dir, Fs fsys)` – CopyFS copies the file system fsys into the directory dir, creating dir if necessary.
- `Str os_lookup_env(Alloc *a, Str key, bool *found)` – LookupEnv retrieves the value of the environment variable named by the key.
- `Str os_getenv(Alloc *a, Str key)` – Getenv retrieves the value of the environment variable named by the key.
- `Error os_setenv(Str key, Str value)` – Setenv sets the value of the environment variable named by the key.
- `Error os_unsetenv(Str key)` – Unsetenv unsets a single environment variable.
- `void os_clearenv(void)` – Clearenv deletes all environment variables.
- `Slice os_environ(Alloc *a)` – Environ returns a copy of strings representing the environment, in the form "key=value".
- `Str os_expand(Alloc *a, Str s, StrFunc mapping)` – Expand replaces ${var} or $var in the string based on the mapping function.
- `Str os_expand_env(Alloc *a, Str s)` – ExpandEnv replaces ${var} or $var in the string according to the values of the current environment variables.
- `Slice os_args(void)` – Args hold the command-line arguments, starting with the program name.
- `Int os_getuid(void)` – Getuid returns the numeric user id of the caller.
- `Int os_geteuid(void)` – Geteuid returns the numeric effective user id of the caller.
- `Int os_getgid(void)` – Getgid returns the numeric group id of the caller.
- `Int os_getegid(void)` – Getegid returns the numeric effective group id of the caller.
- `Slice os_getgroups(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Getgroups returns a list of the numeric ids of groups that the caller belongs to.
- `Int os_getpid(void)` – Getpid returns the process id of the caller.
- `Int os_getppid(void)` – Getppid returns the process id of the caller's parent.
- `Int os_getpagesize(void)` – Getpagesize returns the underlying system's memory page size.
- `void os_exit(Int code)` – Exit causes the current program to exit with the given status code.
- `Str os_hostname(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Hostname returns the host name reported by the kernel.
- `Str os_getwd(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Getwd returns an absolute path name corresponding to the current directory.
- `Error os_chdir(Str dir)` – Chdir changes the current working directory to the named directory.
- `Str os_user_home_dir(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – UserHomeDir returns the current user's home directory.
- `Str os_user_cache_dir(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – UserCacheDir returns the default root directory to use for user-specific cached data.
- `Str os_user_config_dir(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – UserConfigDir returns the default root directory to use for user-specific configuration data.
- `OsSignal os_signal_from_syscall(SyscallSignal s)` – s as an os.Signal, with nothing allocated.
- `SyscallSignal os_signal_to_syscall(OsSignal sig)` – The SyscallSignal inside sig, or -1 when sig is not one.
- `extern const OsSignal os_interrupt` – os.Interrupt and os.Kill: SIGINT and SIGKILL.
- `extern const OsSignal os_kill`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_OS_SIGNAL` – The descriptor for os.Signal, which is what a channel for os/signal is made with: chan_make(a, TYPE_OS_SIGNAL, 1).
- `extern const Error os_err_process_done` – ErrProcessDone indicates a Process has finished.
- `extern const Error os_err_no_handle` – ErrNoHandle indicates a Process does not have a handle.
- `OsProcess *os_start_process(Alloc *a, Str name, Slice argv, const OsProcAttr *attr, Error *err)` – StartProcess starts a new process with the program, arguments and attributes specified by name, argv and attr.
- `OsProcess *os_find_process(Alloc *a, Int pid, Error *err)` – FindProcess looks for a running process by its pid.
- `Error os_process_release(OsProcess *p)` – Release releases any resources associated with the Process p, rendering it unusable in the future.
- `Error os_process_kill(OsProcess *p)` – Kill causes the Process to exit immediately.
- `Error os_process_signal(OsProcess *p, OsSignal sig)` – Signal sends a signal to the Process.
- `OsProcessState *os_process_wait(OsProcess *p, Error *err)` – Wait waits for the Process to exit, and then returns a ProcessState describing its status and an error, if any.
- `Error os_process_with_handle(OsProcess *p, OsHandleFunc f)` – WithHandle calls a supplied function f with a valid process handle as an argument.
- `void os_process_free(OsProcess *p)` – Releases p if it has not been, and gives its memory back.
- `Int os_process_state_pid(const OsProcessState *ps)` – Pid returns the process id of the exited process.
- `bool os_process_state_exited(const OsProcessState *ps)` – Exited reports whether the program has exited.
- `bool os_process_state_success(const OsProcessState *ps)` – Success reports whether the program exited successfully, such as with exit status 0 on Unix.
- `Int os_process_state_exit_code(const OsProcessState *ps)` – ExitCode returns the exit code of the exited process, or -1 if the process hasn't exited or was terminated by a signal.
- `Str os_process_state_string(const OsProcessState *ps, Alloc *a)` – ProcessState.String: "exit status 1", "signal: killed", "signal: segmentation fault (core dumped)" and so on, as Go words them.
- `SyscallWaitStatus os_process_state_sys(const OsProcessState *ps)` – Sys returns system-dependent exit information about the process.
- `const SyscallRusage *os_process_state_sys_usage(const OsProcessState *ps)` – SysUsage returns system-dependent resource usage information about the exited process.
- `Duration os_process_state_user_time(const OsProcessState *ps)` – UserTime returns the user CPU time of the exited process and its children.
- `Duration os_process_state_system_time(const OsProcessState *ps)` – SystemTime returns the system CPU time of the exited process and its children.
- `void os_process_state_free(OsProcessState *ps)`
- `Str os_executable(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Executable returns the path name for the executable that started the current process.
- `OsRoot *os_open_root(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – OpenRoot opens the named directory.
- `OsFile *os_open_in_root(Alloc *a, Str dir, Str name, Error *err)` – OpenInRoot opens the file name in the directory dir.
- `Str os_root_name(const OsRoot *r)` – Name returns the name of the directory presented to OpenRoot.
- `Error os_root_close(OsRoot *r)` – Close closes the Root.
- `void os_root_free(OsRoot *r)` – The memory behind r, closed first if it is still open.
- `OsFile *os_root_open(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file in the root for reading.
- `OsFile *os_root_create(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Create creates or truncates the named file in the root.
- `OsFile *os_root_open_file(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Int flag, OsFileMode perm, Error *err)` – OpenFile opens the named file in the root.
- `OsRoot *os_root_open_root(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – OpenRoot opens the named directory in the root.
- `Error os_root_chmod(OsRoot *r, Str name, OsFileMode mode)` – Chmod changes the mode of the named file in the root to mode.
- `Error os_root_mkdir(OsRoot *r, Str name, OsFileMode perm)` – Mkdir creates a new directory in the root with the specified name and permission bits (before umask).
- `Error os_root_mkdir_all(OsRoot *r, Str name, OsFileMode perm)` – MkdirAll creates a new directory in the root, along with any necessary parents.
- `Error os_root_chown(OsRoot *r, Str name, Int uid, Int gid)` – Chown changes the numeric uid and gid of the named file in the root.
- `Error os_root_lchown(OsRoot *r, Str name, Int uid, Int gid)` – Lchown changes the numeric uid and gid of the named file in the root.
- `Error os_root_chtimes(OsRoot *r, Str name, Time atime, Time mtime)` – Chtimes changes the access and modification times of the named file in the root.
- `Error os_root_remove(OsRoot *r, Str name)` – Remove removes the named file or (empty) directory in the root.
- `Error os_root_remove_all(OsRoot *r, Str name)` – RemoveAll removes the named file or directory and any children that it contains.
- `Error os_root_rename(OsRoot *r, Str oldname, Str newname)` – Rename renames (moves) oldname to newname.
- `Error os_root_link(OsRoot *r, Str oldname, Str newname)` – Link creates newname as a hard link to the oldname file.
- `Error os_root_symlink(OsRoot *r, Str oldname, Str newname)` – Symlink creates newname as a symbolic link to oldname.
- `OsFileInfo os_root_stat(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Stat returns a FileInfo describing the named file in the root.
- `OsFileInfo os_root_lstat(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Lstat returns a FileInfo describing the named file in the root.
- `Str os_root_readlink(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Readlink returns the destination of the named symbolic link in the root.
- `Slice os_root_read_file(OsRoot *r, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – ReadFile reads the named file in the root and returns its contents.
- `Error os_root_write_file(OsRoot *r, Str name, Slice data, OsFileMode perm)` – WriteFile writes data to the named file in the root, creating it if necessary.
- `Fs os_root_fs(OsRoot *r)` – FS returns a file system (an fs.FS) for the tree of files in the root.

## burrow/os/exec.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `os/exec` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error exec_err_not_found` – ErrNotFound is the error resulting if a path search failed to find an executable file.
- `extern const Error exec_err_dot` – ErrDot indicates that a path lookup resolved to an executable in the current directory due to ‘.’ being in the path, either implicitly or explicitly.
- `extern const Error exec_err_wait_delay` – ErrWaitDelay is returned by Cmd.Wait if the process exits with a successful status code but its output pipes are not closed before the command's WaitDelay expires.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXEC_ERROR`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_EXEC_EXIT_ERROR`
- `ExecCmd *exec_command(Alloc *a, Str name, Slice args)` – Command returns the Cmd struct to execute the named program with the given arguments.
- `ExecCmd *exec_command_v(Alloc *a, Str name, int n, ...)` – exec_command without a Slice.
- `ExecCmd *exec_command_context(Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str name, Slice args)` – CommandContext is like Command but includes a context.
- `ExecCmd *exec_command_context_v(Alloc *a, Context ctx, Str name, int n, ...)`
- `void exec_cmd_free(ExecCmd *c)` – Gives back everything the Cmd holds and the Cmd itself.
- `Str exec_look_path(Alloc *a, Str file, Error *err)` – LookPath searches for an executable named file in the current path, following the conventions of the host operating system.
- `Str exec_cmd_string(const ExecCmd *c, Alloc *a)` – String returns a human-readable description of c.
- `Error exec_cmd_run(ExecCmd *c)` – Run starts the specified command and waits for it to complete.
- `Error exec_cmd_start(ExecCmd *c)` – Start starts the specified command but does not wait for it to complete.
- `Error exec_cmd_wait(ExecCmd *c)` – Wait waits for the command to exit and waits for any copying to stdin or copying from stdout or stderr to complete.
- `Slice exec_cmd_output(ExecCmd *c, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Output runs the command and returns its standard output.
- `Slice exec_cmd_combined_output(ExecCmd *c, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – CombinedOutput runs the command and returns its combined standard output and standard error.
- `IoWriteCloser exec_cmd_stdin_pipe(ExecCmd *c, Error *err)` – StdinPipe returns a pipe that will be connected to the command's standard input when the command starts.
- `IoReadCloser exec_cmd_stdout_pipe(ExecCmd *c, Error *err)` – StdoutPipe returns a pipe that will be connected to the command's standard output when the command starts.
- `IoReadCloser exec_cmd_stderr_pipe(ExecCmd *c, Error *err)` – StderrPipe returns a pipe that will be connected to the command's standard error when the command starts.
- `Slice exec_cmd_environ(const ExecCmd *c, Alloc *a)` – Environ returns a copy of the environment in which the command would be run as it is currently configured.
- `Str exec_error_error(const ExecError *e, Alloc *a)` – ExecError's and ExecExitError's text, built in a, and each as an Error.
- `Error exec_error_as_error(const ExecError *e, Alloc *a)`
- `Str exec_exit_error_error(const ExecExitError *e, Alloc *a)`
- `Error exec_error_unwrap(const ExecError *e)` – Error.Unwrap: e-\>err.
- `Int exec_exit_error_pid(const ExecExitError *e)` – The ProcessState methods Go's ExitError gets from its embedded \*os.ProcessState, each the os_process_state\_ function of the same name called on e-\>process_state.
- `bool exec_exit_error_exited(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `bool exec_exit_error_success(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `Int exec_exit_error_exit_code(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `Str exec_exit_error_string(const ExecExitError *e, Alloc *a)`
- `SyscallWaitStatus exec_exit_error_sys(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `const SyscallRusage *exec_exit_error_sys_usage(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `Duration exec_exit_error_user_time(const ExecExitError *e)`
- `Duration exec_exit_error_system_time(const ExecExitError *e)`

## burrow/os/signal.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `os/signal` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `void signal_notify(Chan *c, Slice sigs)` – Notify causes package signal to relay incoming signals to c.
- `void signal_notify_v(Chan *c, int n, ...)` – signal_notify without a Slice.
- `void signal_stop(Chan *c)` – Stop causes package signal to stop relaying incoming signals to c.
- `void signal_reset(Slice sigs)` – Reset undoes the effect of any prior calls to Notify for the provided signals.
- `void signal_reset_v(int n, ...)`
- `void signal_ignore(Slice sigs)` – Ignore causes the provided signals to be ignored.
- `void signal_ignore_v(int n, ...)`
- `bool signal_ignored(OsSignal sig)` – Ignored reports whether sig is currently ignored.
- `Context signal_notify_context(Alloc *a, Context parent, ContextCancelFunc *stop, Slice sigs)` – NotifyContext returns a copy of the parent context that is marked done (its Done channel is closed) when one of the listed signals arrives, when the returned stop function is called, or when the parent context's Done channel is closed, whichever happens first.
- `Context signal_notify_context_v(Alloc *a, Context parent, ContextCancelFunc *stop, int n, ...)`

## burrow/os/user.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `os/user` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `User *user_current(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Current returns the current user.
- `User *user_lookup(Alloc *a, Str username, Error *err)` – Lookup looks up a user by username.
- `User *user_lookup_id(Alloc *a, Str uid, Error *err)` – LookupId looks up a user by userid.
- `UserGroup *user_lookup_group(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – LookupGroup looks up a group by name.
- `UserGroup *user_lookup_group_id(Alloc *a, Str gid, Error *err)` – LookupGroupId looks up a group by groupid.
- `Slice user_group_ids(const User *u, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GroupIds returns the list of group IDs that the user is a member of.
- `void user_free(Alloc *a, User *u)` – Give back a User or UserGroup from this package.
- `void user_group_free(Alloc *a, UserGroup *g)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_USER_UNKNOWN_USER_ID_ERROR`
- `Str user_unknown_user_id_error_error(UserUnknownUserIdError e, Alloc *a)`
- `Error user_unknown_user_id_error_as_error(UserUnknownUserIdError e, Alloc *a)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_USER_UNKNOWN_USER_ERROR`
- `Str user_unknown_user_error_error(UserUnknownUserError e, Alloc *a)`
- `Error user_unknown_user_error_as_error(UserUnknownUserError e, Alloc *a)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_USER_UNKNOWN_GROUP_ID_ERROR`
- `Str user_unknown_group_id_error_error(UserUnknownGroupIdError e, Alloc *a)`
- `Error user_unknown_group_id_error_as_error(UserUnknownGroupIdError e, Alloc *a)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_USER_UNKNOWN_GROUP_ERROR`
- `Str user_unknown_group_error_error(UserUnknownGroupError e, Alloc *a)`
- `Error user_unknown_group_error_as_error(UserUnknownGroupError e, Alloc *a)`

## burrow/pal.h

- `const char *pal_errno_string(PalErrno e)` – The message for a code, as a NUL terminated ASCII string that lives forever.
- `int64_t pal_errno_native(PalErrno e)` – The platform's own code behind e: the errno on POSIX, and on Windows the ERROR\_ or WSA code the system gave.
- `int64_t pal_errno_message(int64_t native, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – Windows' message for a native code, from FormatMessageW, in US English when the system has one and in the user's language otherwise, without the line break at the end.
- `int64_t pal_clock_monotonic(void)` – Nanoseconds since some arbitrary point that does not change while the process runs.
- `int64_t pal_clock_high_precision(void)` – A high-resolution timestamp for measuring time differences on the order of tens to hundreds of nanoseconds, which is what the FIPS 140-3 entropy source times its samples with.
- `int64_t pal_clock_realtime(void)` – Nanoseconds since 1970-01-01 UTC, leap seconds smeared or ignored the way the platform does it.
- `void pal_nanosleep(int64_t ns)` – Sleep for at least ns, or return early if a signal arrives.
- `int64_t pal_tz_load(const char *name, void *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – Read a zoneinfo database entry into buf and return its length, or -1.
- `void *pal_vm_reserve(int64_t bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Reserve address space without backing it with anything.
- `bool pal_vm_commit(void *addr, int64_t bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Back a range inside a reservation with memory and make it readable and writable.
- `bool pal_vm_decommit(void *addr, int64_t bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Give the memory behind a range back to the system, keeping the reservation so that nothing else can take the addresses.
- `bool pal_vm_release(void *addr, int64_t bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Drop a reservation entirely.
- `bool pal_vm_guard(void *addr, int64_t bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Turn a committed range into a guard: any access to it faults.
- `void *pal_mmap(int64_t fd, int64_t off, int64_t len, uint32_t prot, PalErrno *err)` – Map a file, or part of one, into memory.
- `bool pal_munmap(void *addr, int64_t len, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_page_size(void)` – The page size, which every address and length in the memory group has to be a multiple of.
- `int64_t pal_cpu_count(void)` – How many hardware threads the process may run on, which is what Go's NumCPU answers and what the scheduler sizes itself from.
- `int64_t pal_cpu_name(char *buf, int64_t cap)` – The processor's name, the one a benchmark run prints on its cpu: line, into buf as a NUL terminated string cut to fit.
- `uint32_t pal_cpu_features(void)` – The PAL_CPU\_ bits this processor has, found on the first call and kept.
- `int64_t pal_hostname(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The machine's name into buf, NUL terminated, returning its length or -1.
- `int64_t pal_hosts_path(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – Where the hosts file lives, into buf, NUL terminated, returning its length or -1.
- `bool pal_random_bytes(void *buf, int64_t n, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_registry_content_types(PalContentTypeFn fn, void *env, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_text_mappings(PalMappingFn fn, void *env)`
- `bool pal_tz_info(PalTzInfo *out, PalErrno *err)` – GetTimeZoneInformation.
- `bool pal_tz_key_names(const char *key, int64_t key_len, char *std, int64_t *std_len, char *dst, int64_t *dst_len, PalErrno *err)` – The standard and daylight names of the zone called key under HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE\\SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Windows NT\\CurrentVersion\\Time Zones, which is Go's MUI_Std and MUI_Dlt, or Std and Dlt when either of those fails.
- `bool pal_tz_english_name(const char *std, int64_t std_len, const char *dst, int64_t dst_len, char *out, int64_t *out_len, PalErrno *err)` – Go's toEnglishName: the name of the first key under Time Zones whose names are std and dst, where a dst equal to std matches any daylight name.
- `bool pal_cert_verify(const PalCertVerifyOpts *opts, PalCertChainFn fn, void *env, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_trust_evaluate(const PalTrustOpts *opts, PalTrustFn fn, PalTrustCertFn cert_fn, void *env, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_thread_create(void (*fn)(void *), void *arg, int64_t stack_bytes, PalErrno *err)` – Start a thread running fn(arg) on a stack of at least stack_bytes, or 0 for the platform's default.
- `bool pal_thread_join(int64_t thread, PalErrno *err)` – Wait for a thread to finish and release its handle.
- `bool pal_thread_detach(int64_t thread, PalErrno *err)` – Say the thread will never be joined, so the system can release what it holds as soon as the thread returns.
- `bool pal_thread_cancel_io(int64_t thread, PalErrno *err)` – CancelSynchronousIo: makes a ReadFile or WriteFile the thread is blocked in right now give up, with PAL_ECANCELED.
- `int64_t pal_thread_self(void)` – An identifier for the calling thread that is unique among the threads running right now.
- `void pal_thread_yield(void)` – Give up the rest of this thread's time slice.
- `bool pal_thread_stack_bounds(void **lo, void **hi)` – Where the calling thread's own stack begins and ends: lo is the lowest address on it and hi is one past the highest.
- `bool pal_thread_stack_limit(void **lo)` – The lowest address the calling thread's stack can grow down to, for code that wants to know how deep it can still go.
- `bool pal_thread_on_exit(PalThreadExit *node)`
- `bool pal_futex_wait(uint32_t *addr, uint32_t expect, int64_t timeout_ns, PalErrno *err)` – Sleep until the 32 bit word at addr stops being expect, or until somebody wakes it, or until timeout_ns passes.
- `int64_t pal_futex_wake(uint32_t *addr, int64_t n, PalErrno *err)` – Wake at most n waiters on addr, returning how many were woken, or -1.
- `bool pal_stdout_capture_begin(PalStdoutCapture *c, void (*sink)(void *env, const void *p, int64_t n), void *env, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_stdout_capture_end(PalStdoutCapture *c, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_open(const char *path, uint32_t flags, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_close(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_read(int64_t fd, void *buf, int64_t n, PalErrno *err)` – Short reads and writes are normal and are not errors.
- `int64_t pal_pread(int64_t fd, void *buf, int64_t n, int64_t off, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_write(int64_t fd, const void *buf, int64_t n, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_pwrite(int64_t fd, const void *buf, int64_t n, int64_t off, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_seek(int64_t fd, int64_t off, int32_t whence, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_fsync(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_ftruncate(int64_t fd, int64_t size, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_truncate(const char *path, int64_t size, PalErrno *err)` – ftruncate by name.
- `bool pal_stat(const char *path, PalStat *out, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_lstat(const char *path, PalStat *out, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_fstat(int64_t fd, PalStat *out, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_unlink(const char *path, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_rename(const char *from, const char *to, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_rename_replace(const char *from, const char *to, PalErrno *err)` – rename the way Go's Root.Rename does it on Windows: through a handle on from that leaves a link alone, with POSIX semantics, so that whatever is at to goes, a link to a directory included, where MoveFileEx says access denied.
- `bool pal_mkdir(const char *path, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_rmdir(const char *path, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_readdir(PalDir *d, PalDirEntry *out, PalErrno *err)` – One entry per call.
- `bool pal_link(const char *from, const char *to, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_symlink(const char *target, const char *path, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_readlink(const char *path, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The link's target into buf.
- `bool pal_chmod(const char *path, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_eaccess(const char *path, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_chown(const char *path, int64_t uid, int64_t gid, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_lchown(const char *path, int64_t uid, int64_t gid, PalErrno *err)` – chown on a symbolic link itself rather than what it points at.
- `bool pal_fchmod(int64_t fd, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)` – chmod and chown on an open file.
- `bool pal_fchown(int64_t fd, int64_t uid, int64_t gid, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_utimes(const char *path, int64_t atime_ns, int64_t mtime_ns, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_openat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, uint32_t flags, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)` – The same calls on a name relative to an open directory, which is what os.Root walks a path with.
- `bool pal_lstatat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, PalStat *out, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_mkdirat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_unlinkat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, bool dir, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_readlinkat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_renameat(int64_t olddirfd, const char *from, int64_t newdirfd, const char *to, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_linkat(int64_t olddirfd, const char *from, int64_t newdirfd, const char *to, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_symlinkat(const char *target, int64_t dirfd, const char *path, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_fchmodat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_fchownat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, int64_t uid, int64_t gid, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_utimesat(int64_t dirfd, const char *path, int64_t atime_ns, int64_t mtime_ns, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_dup(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_nonblock(int64_t fd, bool *on, PalErrno *err)` – Whether fd is in non blocking mode, and putting it in or out of it, which are Go's unix.IsNonblock and syscall.SetNonblock.
- `bool pal_set_nonblock(int64_t fd, bool on, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_pipe(int64_t out[2], uint32_t flags, PalErrno *err)` – out\[0\] is the read end and out\[1\] is the write end.
- `int pal_fd_wait(int64_t fd, bool write, int64_t cancel, PalErrno *err)` – Waits until fd can be read without blocking, or written when write is true, or until cancel can be read, whichever comes first.
- `int64_t pal_spawn(const PalSpawn *req, PalErrno *err)` – Start the process and return its id, or -1.
- `bool pal_set_ids(PalSetID which, uint32_t a, uint32_t b, uint32_t c, PalErrno *err)` – setuid, setgid and the rest, which, with the ids it takes in order and the ones after them ignored.
- `bool pal_setgroups(const uint32_t *gids, int64_t n, PalErrno *err)` – setgroups, from the C library for the same reason.
- `int64_t pal_wait(int64_t pid, int32_t *status, uint32_t flags, PalErrno *err)` – Wait for a child.
- `bool pal_kill(int64_t pid, int32_t sig, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_getpid(void)`
- `bool pal_kill_native(int64_t pid, int32_t sig, PalErrno *err)` – kill(2) with the system's own signal number, which is what syscall.Kill takes, and with pid passed through as it is, so that 0 and the negative numbers reach a process group the way they do in C. On Windows pid is a process handle, 9 terminates it with exit code 1 as Go's Process.Kill does, 0 asks whether it is still running, and anything else is PAL_ENOTSUP.
- `int64_t pal_wait4(int64_t pid, uint32_t *status, int32_t options, PalRusage *ru, PalErrno *err)` – Wait for a child and report what the system says, unchanged, which is what syscall.Wait4 and os.Process.Wait need and pal_wait does not keep.
- `bool pal_wait_ready(int64_t pid, PalErrno *err)` – Block until the child pid can be waited for, without reaping it, so that a caller can stop signalling it before the id is free to be reused.
- `int64_t pal_process_id(int64_t pid)` – Turn a pid from pal_spawn into the number the system knows the process by.
- `int64_t pal_process_open(int64_t id, PalErrno *err)` – The other way: a pid that pal_wait4, pal_kill and pal_process_close take, for the process with the system's id.
- `void pal_process_close(int64_t pid)` – Let go of a pid from pal_spawn or pal_process_open that nobody is going to pal_wait for.
- `int64_t pal_getppid(void)` – The parent's process id.
- `void pal_ids(PalIds *out)`
- `int64_t pal_getgroups(uint32_t *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The supplementary group ids into buf, returning how many there are.
