- `PalErrno pal_getpwnam(const char *name, PalPasswd *pw, char *buf, int64_t cap, bool *found)` – getpwnam_r, getpwuid_r, getgrnam_r and getgrgid_r, which is the libc user database and so everything NSS knows about, not only /etc/passwd.
- `PalErrno pal_getpwuid(uint32_t uid, PalPasswd *pw, char *buf, int64_t cap, bool *found)`
- `PalErrno pal_getgrnam(const char *name, PalGroup *gr, char *buf, int64_t cap, bool *found)`
- `PalErrno pal_getgrgid(uint32_t gid, PalGroup *gr, char *buf, int64_t cap, bool *found)`
- `int64_t pal_user_buf_size(bool group)` – sysconf(\_SC_GETPW_R_SIZE_MAX), or \_SC_GETGR_R_SIZE_MAX for group, the size the system suggests for the buffers above.
- `int pal_getgrouplist(const char *name, uint32_t gid, uint32_t *gids, int *n)` – getgrouplist: the groups name is in, with gid among them, into gids, which has room for \*n.
- `PalErrno pal_win_current_user(char *buf, int64_t cap, int *stage)` – The process owner from its token: the user SID, the primary group SID, the profile directory, the NameSamCompatible name and the NameDisplay name, in that order.
- `PalErrno pal_win_current_groups(char *buf, int64_t cap, int *n, int *stage)` – The SIDs of every group in the process token, \*n of them.
- `PalErrno pal_win_lookup_name(const char *name, char *buf, int64_t cap, uint32_t *type, bool *service)` – LookupAccountName: name's SID as a string into buf, with its account type and whether it is one of the service accounts LocalSystem, LocalService and NetworkService.
- `PalErrno pal_win_lookup_sid(const char *sid, char *buf, int64_t cap, uint32_t *type, bool *service)` – LookupAccountSid on a SID in string form: the account name, the domain and the SID in the form ConvertSidToStringSid gives, with the type and service flag as above.
- `PalErrno pal_win_domain_joined(bool *joined)` – NetGetJoinInformation: whether the machine is in a domain.
- `PalErrno pal_win_display_name(const char *account, char *buf, int64_t cap)` – TranslateName from NameSamCompatible, "DOMAIN\\user", to NameDisplay.
- `PalErrno pal_win_user_full_name(const char *server, const char *user, char *buf, int64_t cap)` – NetUserGetInfo at level 10 and 4: the full name, and the RID of the primary group.
- `PalErrno pal_win_user_primary_group(const char *server, const char *user, uint32_t *rid)`
- `PalErrno pal_win_user_local_groups(const char *user, char *buf, int64_t cap, int *n)` – NetUserGetLocalGroups with LG_INCLUDE_INDIRECT: the names of the local groups user is in, \*n of them.
- `PalErrno pal_win_profile_path(const char *sid, char *buf, int64_t cap)` – The ProfileImagePath value of the ProfileList key for sid, under HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Windows NT\\CurrentVersion, which is where a user's home is once they have logged in, and GetProfilesDirectory, the directory the homes are made in.
- `PalErrno pal_win_profiles_dir(char *buf, int64_t cap)`
- `int64_t pal_std_handle(int i)` – The handle behind standard input, output or error, for i 0, 1 or 2, which is what a PalSpawn fds list wants when a child is to share ours.
- `void pal_exit(int32_t code)` – Ends the process now.
- `bool pal_exit_started(void)` – Whether pal_exit has been called.
- `const char *const *pal_environ(void)` – The environment as a NULL terminated array of "KEY=VALUE".
- `int64_t pal_getenv(const char *key, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The environment as it is now rather than as it started, for the os package, which on Windows reads it from the system every time the way Go does.
- `bool pal_setenv(const char *key, const char *value, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_environ_read(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_args(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The process's command line, which is what Go's os.Args starts from: every argument, the program name first, each followed by a NUL, one after another in buf.
- `bool pal_chdir(const char *path, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_fchdir(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)` – chdir to the directory open as fd.
- `int64_t pal_getcwd(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The working directory into buf, NUL terminated.
- `int64_t pal_temp_dir(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The directory for temporary files into buf, NUL terminated, as Go's os.TempDir finds it: $TMPDIR or /tmp on Unix, and GetTempPath2W, or GetTempPathW before Windows 11, on Windows, without the trailing backslash.
- `bool pal_is_dos_device_name(const char *name, int64_t n)` – Whether Windows reads name, n bytes of UTF-8 with no NUL in it, as a DOS device such as NUL or COM1, by asking RtlIsDosDeviceName_U the way Go's filepath does for a reserved name with an extension.
- `int64_t pal_full_path(const char *path, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – path made absolute the way Windows does it, by GetFullPathNameW, which is what Go's filepath.Abs asks there: against the working directory, or the one Windows keeps for the drive when path is "C:x".
- `int64_t pal_find_name(const char *path, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The last element of path as the file system stores it, which on Windows can differ in case from the one asked for.
- `int64_t pal_exec_lookup(const char *name, char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – Resolve a program name against the search path, writing the full path to buf.
- `int64_t pal_executable(char *buf, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – The path of the running program into buf, NUL terminated, returning its length, or -1 with PAL_ERANGE when it does not fit.
- `bool pal_signal_install(int32_t sig, PalSignalHandler handler, PalErrno *err)` – Install a handler.
- `bool pal_signal_mask(int32_t sig, bool block, PalErrno *err)` – Block or unblock a signal for the calling thread only.
- `bool pal_signal_stack_install(PalErrno *err)` – Gives the calling thread somewhere for a handler to run that is not the stack it is already on.
- `void pal_signal_stack_remove(void)` – Gives back what the call above took.
- `const void *pal_signal_fault_addr(const void *info)` – The address a bad memory access was trying to reach, out of the info pointer a PAL_SIGFAULT handler was called with.
- `bool pal_signal_context_pc_fp(const void *ctx, uintptr_t *pc, uintptr_t *fp)` – The program counter and the frame pointer of the code a signal interrupted, out of the ctx pointer its handler was called with.
- `bool pal_profile_timer(int32_t hz, PalErrno *err)` – Starts the timer that sends PAL_SIGPROF hz times a second of CPU time the process uses, on whichever of its threads is running when it fires, or stops it when hz is zero.
- `bool pal_signal_relay_init(PalSignalRelay send, PalErrno *err)` – Once, before the first pal_signal_relay.
- `bool pal_signal_relay(int32_t native, int32_t how, PalErrno *err)` – Changes what one signal does.
- `bool pal_signal_ignored(int32_t native)` – Whether the signal is ignored right now, which is how os/signal finds out that it was started under nohup.
- `void pal_signal_note_wake(void)` – A one place gate between the handler and the thread that reads what it caught.
- `void pal_signal_note_sleep(void)`
- `uintptr_t pal_syscall(uintptr_t trap, const uintptr_t *args, int32_t n, uintptr_t *errnum)` – The system call trap with the first n of args, n at most 9, the way the system's syscall(2) makes it, which on FreeBSD is \_\_syscall so that a 64-bit result comes back whole.
- `uintptr_t pal_call(void *fn, const uintptr_t *args, int32_t n, int32_t nfixed, uintptr_t *errnum)` – Calls the C function at fn with the first n of args, n at most 18, or 42 on Windows as in Go's SyscallN, each an integer or a pointer passed as a whole register, and returns what came back in the return register.
- `void *pal_libc_symbol(const char *name)` – The address of the C library function name, as the dynamic linker finds it from the program, or NULL if there is no such function.
- `uintptr_t pal_load_library(const uint16_t *name, bool system, uintptr_t *errnum)` – LoadLibraryExW of name, a UTF-16 string with a NUL on the end, looking only in the system directory when system is true, which is what Go does for the DLLs its syscall package names, so that one dropped next to the program is not picked up instead.
- `void *pal_proc_address(uintptr_t module, const char *name, uintptr_t *errnum)` – GetProcAddress: the function name in module, or NULL with GetLastError's code in \*errnum.
- `bool pal_dl_realpath(const char *path, char *out, size_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – path with every symbolic link, ".", ".." and extra separator resolved, as realpath(3) gives it, into out, which holds cap bytes including the NUL that ends it.
- `int64_t pal_dl_open(const char *path, char *msg, size_t cap)` – dlopen of path with RTLD_NOW | RTLD_GLOBAL, which is what Go's plugin package asks for, or LoadLibraryExW. It returns the handle, or 0 with the reason in msg, which holds cap bytes and always ends with a NUL.
- `void *pal_dl_sym(int64_t handle, const char *name, PalErrno *err)` – dlsym, or GetProcAddress: the address of the symbol name in the library handle, which stays good until the library is closed.
- `bool pal_dl_close(int64_t handle, PalErrno *err)` – dlclose, or FreeLibrary.
- `bool pal_user_lookup(int64_t uid, PalUser *out, PalErrno *err)` – uid below zero means the user this process is running as.
- `int64_t pal_socket(int32_t family, int32_t type, int32_t protocol, PalErrno *err)` – A socket, always non blocking and always close on exec, because every descriptor in burrow goes to the poller and a blocking one would park an OS thread instead of a goroutine.
- `bool pal_socket_close(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)` – Closes a socket.
- `int64_t pal_socket_dup(int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)` – A second descriptor for the socket fd, close on exec, which is what Go's File methods hand out.
- `bool pal_bind(int64_t fd, const PalSockAddr *addr, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_listen(int64_t fd, int32_t backlog, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_accept(int64_t fd, PalSockAddr *peer, PalErrno *err)` – Returns the new descriptor, or -1 with PAL_EAGAIN when there is nothing waiting, which is the normal answer on a non blocking listener and is what sends the goroutine to the poller.
- `bool pal_getsockname(int64_t fd, PalSockAddr *out, PalErrno *err)` – The address a socket is bound to and the one it is connected to, Go's Getsockname and Getpeername.
- `bool pal_getpeername(int64_t fd, PalSockAddr *out, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_connect(int64_t fd, const PalSockAddr *addr, PalErrno *err)` – PAL_EINPROGRESS is the normal answer, not a failure: the caller waits for writability and then reads PAL_SO_ERROR to find out how it went.
- `int64_t pal_sendto(int64_t fd, const void *buf, int64_t n, const PalSockAddr *addr, PalErrno *err)` – addr NULL means a connected socket, which makes these send and recv.
- `int64_t pal_recvfrom(int64_t fd, void *buf, int64_t n, PalSockAddr *from, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_recvmsg(int64_t fd, void *buf, int64_t n, void *oob, int64_t oobcap, int64_t *oobn, int32_t *flags, PalSockAddr *from, PalErrno *err)` – recvmsg and sendmsg, with one buffer of data, and oob for the control messages as the system lays them out, which is what Go's ReadMsgUDP and the rest hand their callers.
- `int64_t pal_sendmsg(int64_t fd, const void *buf, int64_t n, const void *oob, int64_t oobn, const PalSockAddr *to, PalErrno *err)`
- `int64_t pal_writev(int64_t fd, const PalIovec *v, int32_t count, PalErrno *err)` – writev on a connected socket: sends the count buffers in v as one write and answers how much of them went.
- `bool pal_getsockopt(int64_t fd, int32_t opt, int64_t *value, PalErrno *err)` – A value is 1 or 0 for the options that are on or off, a count for the buffer sizes, the hop limits and TCP_KEEPCNT, and seconds for the other two TCP ones.
- `bool pal_setsockopt(int64_t fd, int32_t opt, int64_t value, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_setsockopt_mreq(int64_t fd, int32_t opt, const PalMreq *m, PalErrno *err)`
- `bool pal_shutdown(int64_t fd, int32_t how, PalErrno *err)`
- `int32_t pal_listen_backlog_max(void)` – The longest queue of connections a listener can ask the system for, which is what Go's net passes to listen: /proc/sys/net/core/somaxconn on Linux and a sysctl on macOS, FreeBSD and OpenBSD.
- `bool pal_kernel_version_ge(int32_t major, int32_t minor)` – Linux's unix.KernelVersionGE: whether the running kernel is major.minor or newer, by the release uname gives, such as "6.8.0-45-generic".
- `bool pal_mptcp_in_use(int64_t fd, bool sol_mptcp)` – Whether the TCP socket fd is speaking Multipath TCP, which is the second half of Go's isUsingMultipathTCP.
- `int64_t pal_getaddrinfo(const char *host, const char *service, int32_t family, int32_t socktype, int32_t protocol, int32_t flags, PalAddrInfo *out, int64_t cap, PalLookupError *err)` – Resolve host and service, either of which may be NULL, into out, and answer how many results there were, which may be more than cap.
- `bool pal_getnameinfo(const PalSockAddr *addr, char *host, int64_t cap, PalLookupError *err)` – The name the system has for addr, from getnameinfo with NI_NAMEREQD, into host with its NUL.
- `int64_t pal_res_search(const char *name, int32_t rclass, int32_t rtype, uint8_t *ans, int64_t cap, PalErrno *err)` – res_nsearch, or res_search on Linux and OpenBSD as in Go: a DNS query for name of type rtype and class rclass, through the system's stub resolver, with the reply into ans.
- `int64_t pal_if_enumerate(int32_t index, PalInterface *out, int64_t cap, PalIfError *err)` – The interface with this index, or all of them in the order the system lists them for 0.
- `int64_t pal_if_addrs(int32_t index, PalIfAddr *out, int64_t cap, PalIfError *err)` – The unicast addresses of the interface with this index, or of all of them for 0, and on Windows the anycast ones after each interface's unicast ones.
- `int64_t pal_if_multicast_addrs(int32_t index, PalIfAddr *out, int64_t cap, PalIfError *err)` – The multicast groups the interface with this index has joined, as PAL_IFA_ADDR records.
- `int64_t pal_poll_create(PalErrno *err)` – Makes the poller and whatever it wakes up with, which is one object as far as anything above here is concerned.
- `bool pal_poll_add(int64_t poll, int64_t fd, void *user, PalErrno *err)` – Register for reading and writing together, edge triggered, once for the life of the descriptor.
- `bool pal_poll_del(int64_t poll, int64_t fd, PalErrno *err)` – Undo it.
- `bool pal_poll_arm(int64_t poll, int64_t fd, uint32_t mode, PalErrno *err)` – Only where the backend is poll(2), which is level triggered and has to be told what to look for.
- `bool pal_poll_break(int64_t poll, PalErrno *err)` – Ends a blocking pal_poll_wait early, from any thread, including one that is not in the poller.
- `int64_t pal_poll_wait(int64_t poll, PalPollEvent *out, int64_t cap, int64_t timeout_ns, PalErrno *err)` – Wait for up to cap events.

## burrow/panic.h

- `void panic(Any v)` – Go's panic.
- `void panic_str(Str s)` – panic with a string, which is what most panics are.
- `Any panic_value(void)` – What is unwinding on this goroutine right now, or nil when nothing is.
- `Str panic_text(Any v)` – The text of a panic value, for printing one.

## burrow/path.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `path` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error path_err_bad_pattern` – ErrBadPattern indicates a pattern was malformed.
- `Str path_clean(Alloc *a, Str path)` – Clean returns the shortest path name equivalent to path by purely lexical processing.
- `Str path_split(Str path, Str *file)` – Split splits path immediately following the final slash, separating it into a directory and file name component.
- `Str path_join(Alloc *a, Slice elem)` – Join joins any number of path elements into a single path, separating them with slashes.
- `Str path_join_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)` – path_join without building a Slice first:
- `Str path_ext(Str path)` – Ext returns the file name extension used by path.
- `Str path_base(Str path)` – Base returns the last element of path.
- `bool path_is_abs(Str path)` – IsAbs reports whether the path is absolute.
- `Str path_dir(Alloc *a, Str path)` – Dir returns all but the last element of path, typically the path's directory.
- `bool path_match(Str pattern, Str name, Error *err)` – Match reports whether name matches the shell pattern.

## burrow/path/filepath.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `path/filepath` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error filepath_err_bad_pattern` – ErrBadPattern indicates a pattern was malformed.
- `bool filepath_is_path_separator(Byte c)` – os.IsPathSeparator: c == '/' everywhere, and c == '\\\\' as well on Windows.
- `Str filepath_clean(Alloc *a, Str path)` – Clean returns the shortest path name equivalent to path by purely lexical processing.
- `bool filepath_is_local(Str path)` – IsLocal reports whether path, using lexical analysis only, has all of these properties:
- `Str filepath_localize(Alloc *a, Str path, Error *err)` – Localize converts a slash-separated path into an operating system path.
- `Str filepath_to_slash(Alloc *a, Str path)` – ToSlash returns the result of replacing each separator character in path with a slash ('/') character.
- `Str filepath_from_slash(Alloc *a, Str path)` – FromSlash returns the result of replacing each slash ('/') character in path with a separator character.
- `Slice filepath_split_list(Alloc *a, Str path)` – SplitList splits a list of paths joined by the OS-specific ListSeparator, usually found in PATH or GOPATH environment variables.
- `Str filepath_split(Str path, Str *file)` – Split splits path immediately following the final Separator, separating it into a directory and file name component.
- `Str filepath_join(Alloc *a, Slice elem)` – Join joins any number of path elements into a single path, separating them with an OS specific Separator.
- `Str filepath_join_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)` – filepath_join without a Slice.
- `Str filepath_ext(Str path)` – Ext returns the file name extension used by path.
- `Str filepath_base(Str path)` – Base returns the last element of path.
- `Str filepath_dir(Alloc *a, Str path)` – Dir returns all but the last element of path, typically the path's directory.
- `bool filepath_is_abs(Str path)` – IsAbs reports whether the path is absolute.
- `Str filepath_volume_name(Alloc *a, Str path)` – VolumeName returns leading volume name.
- `Str filepath_rel(Alloc *a, Str base, Str targ, Error *err)` – Rel returns a relative path that is lexically equivalent to targPath when joined to basePath with an intervening separator.
- `bool filepath_match(Str pattern, Str name, Error *err)` – Match reports whether name matches the shell file name pattern.
- `bool filepath_has_prefix(Str p, Str prefix)` – HasPrefix exists for historical compatibility and should not be used.
- `Str filepath_abs(Alloc *a, Str path, Error *err)` – Abs returns an absolute representation of path.
- `Str filepath_eval_symlinks(Alloc *a, Str path, Error *err)` – EvalSymlinks returns the path name after the evaluation of any symbolic links.
- `Slice filepath_glob(Alloc *a, Str pattern, Error *err)` – Glob returns the names of all files matching pattern or nil if there is no matching file.
- `Error filepath_walk(Alloc *a, Str root, FilepathWalkFunc fn)` – Walk walks the file tree rooted at root, calling fn for each file or directory in the tree, including root.
- `Error filepath_walk_dir(Alloc *a, Str root, FsWalkDirFunc fn)` – WalkDir walks the file tree rooted at root, calling fn for each file or directory in the tree, including root.

## burrow/plugin.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `plugin` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Plugin *plugin_open(Str path, Error *err)` – Open opens a Go plugin.
- `PluginSymbol plugin_lookup(Plugin *p, Str sym_name, Error *err)` – Lookup searches for a symbol named symName in plugin p.

## burrow/proc.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `runtime` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool go(Func fn)` – Starts fn as a new goroutine and answers whether it started.
- `bool go_stack(Func fn, size_t stack_bytes)` – The same, with a stack size in bytes.
- `void runtime_main(Func fn)` – Starts the scheduler, runs fn as the main goroutine, and returns when fn returns.
- `void runtime_gosched(void)` – Gosched yields the processor, allowing other goroutines to run.
- `void runtime_preempt_point(void)` – Gives up the processor, but only if the scheduler has asked for it.
- `void runtime_goexit(void)` – Goexit terminates the goroutine that calls it.
- `int runtime_gomaxprocs(int n)` – GOMAXPROCS sets the maximum number of CPUs that can be executing simultaneously and returns the previous setting.
- `int runtime_numcpu(void)` – How many processors the machine has.
- `int runtime_numgoroutine(void)` – How many goroutines exist right now, running, runnable or parked.
- `Goroutine *sched_current(void)` – The goroutine this call is running on.
- `void sched_park(SchedUnlockFn unlockf, void *lock)` – Stops the calling goroutine and hands its thread to the scheduler.
- `void sched_ready(Goroutine *g)` – Makes a parked goroutine runnable again.

## burrow/reflect.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `reflect` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str reflect_kind_string(ReflectKind k)` – String returns the name of k.
- `Str reflect_chan_dir_string(ReflectChanDir d)` – ChanDir.String: "\<-chan", "chan\<-", "chan", or "ChanDir" and the number.
- `Str reflect_struct_tag_get(ReflectStructTag tag, Alloc *a, Str key)` – Get returns the value associated with key in the tag string.
- `Str reflect_struct_tag_lookup(ReflectStructTag tag, Alloc *a, Str key, bool *ok)` – Lookup returns the value associated with key in the tag string.
- `bool reflect_struct_field_is_exported(ReflectStructField f)` – IsExported reports whether the field is exported.
- `Int reflect_type_align(ReflectType t)` – Align returns the alignment in bytes of a value of this type when allocated in memory.
- `Int reflect_type_field_align(ReflectType t)` – FieldAlign returns the alignment in bytes of a value of this type when used as a field in a struct.
- `Uintptr reflect_type_size(ReflectType t)` – Size returns the number of bytes needed to store a value of the given type; it is analogous to unsafe.Sizeof.
- `ReflectKind reflect_type_kind(ReflectType t)` – Kind returns the specific kind of this type.
- `Str reflect_type_name(ReflectType t)` – Name returns the type's name within its package for a defined type.
- `Str reflect_type_pkg_path(ReflectType t)` – PkgPath returns a defined type's package path, that is, the import path that uniquely identifies the package, such as "encoding/base64".
- `Str reflect_type_string(ReflectType t)` – String returns a string representation of the type.
- `Int reflect_type_bits(ReflectType t)` – Bits returns the size of the type in bits.
- `bool reflect_type_comparable(ReflectType t)` – Comparable reports whether values of this type are comparable.
- `bool reflect_type_assignable_to(ReflectType t, ReflectType u)` – AssignableTo reports whether a value of the type is assignable to type u.
- `bool reflect_type_convertible_to(ReflectType t, ReflectType u)` – ConvertibleTo reports whether a value of the type is convertible to type u.
- `bool reflect_type_implements(ReflectType t, ReflectType u)` – Implements reports whether the type implements the interface type u.
- `ReflectChanDir reflect_type_chan_dir(ReflectType t)` – ChanDir returns a channel type's direction.
- `ReflectType reflect_type_elem(ReflectType t)` – Elem returns a type's element type.
- `ReflectType reflect_type_key(ReflectType t)` – Key returns a map type's key type.
- `Int reflect_type_len(ReflectType t)` – Len returns an array type's length.
- `Int reflect_type_num_field(ReflectType t)` – NumField returns a struct type's field count.
- `ReflectStructField reflect_type_field(ReflectType t, Int i)` – Field returns a struct type's i'th field.
- `ReflectStructField reflect_type_field_by_index(ReflectType t, Slice index)` – FieldByIndex returns the nested field corresponding to the index sequence.
- `bool reflect_type_field_by_name(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, Str name, ReflectStructField *f)` – FieldByName returns the struct field with the given name and a boolean indicating if the field was found.
- `bool reflect_type_field_by_name_func(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, ReflectNameMatch match, ReflectStructField *f)` – FieldByNameFunc returns the struct field with a name that satisfies the match function and a boolean indicating if the field was found.
- `Slice reflect_visible_fields(Alloc *a, ReflectType t)` – VisibleFields returns all the visible fields in t, which must be a struct type.
- `Int reflect_type_num_in(ReflectType t)` – NumIn returns a function type's input parameter count.
- `Int reflect_type_num_out(ReflectType t)` – NumOut returns a function type's output parameter count.
- `ReflectType reflect_type_in(ReflectType t, Int i)` – In returns the type of a function type's i'th input parameter.
- `ReflectType reflect_type_out(ReflectType t, Int i)` – Out returns the type of a function type's i'th output parameter.
- `bool reflect_type_is_variadic(ReflectType t)` – IsVariadic reports whether a function type's final input parameter is a "..." parameter.
- `Int reflect_type_num_method(ReflectType t)` – NumMethod returns the number of methods accessible using Method.
- `IterSeq reflect_type_fields(ReflectType t)` – Fields returns an iterator over each struct field for struct type t.
- `IterSeq reflect_type_methods(ReflectType t)` – Methods returns an iterator over each method in the type's method set.
- `IterSeq reflect_type_ins(ReflectType t)` – Ins returns an iterator over each input parameter of function type t.
- `IterSeq reflect_type_outs(ReflectType t)` – Outs returns an iterator over each output parameter of function type t.
- `bool reflect_type_overflow_int(ReflectType t, int64_t x)` – OverflowInt reports whether the int64 x cannot be represented by type t.
- `bool reflect_type_overflow_uint(ReflectType t, uint64_t x)` – OverflowUint reports whether the uint64 x cannot be represented by type t.
- `bool reflect_type_overflow_float(ReflectType t, double x)` – OverflowFloat reports whether the float64 x cannot be represented by type t.
- `bool reflect_type_overflow_complex(ReflectType t, Complex128 x)` – OverflowComplex reports whether the complex128 x cannot be represented by type t.
- `bool reflect_type_can_seq(ReflectType t)` – CanSeq reports whether a Value with this type can be iterated over using Value.Seq.
- `bool reflect_type_can_seq2(ReflectType t)` – CanSeq2 reports whether a Value with this type can be iterated over using Value.Seq2.
- `ReflectType reflect_type_of(Any i)` – TypeOf returns the reflection Type that represents the dynamic type of i.
- `ReflectType reflect_pointer_to(ReflectType t)` – PointerTo returns the pointer type with element t.
- `ReflectType reflect_slice_of(ReflectType t)` – SliceOf returns the slice type with element type t.
- `ReflectType reflect_map_of(ReflectType key, ReflectType elem)` – MapOf returns the map type with the given key and element types.
- `ReflectType reflect_array_of(Int length, ReflectType elem)` – ArrayOf returns the array type with the given length and element type.
- `ReflectType reflect_chan_of(ReflectChanDir dir, ReflectType t)` – ChanOf returns the channel type with the given direction and element type.
- `ReflectType reflect_ptr_to(ReflectType t)` – PtrTo returns the pointer type with element t.
- `#define reflect_type_for(T)` – TypeFor returns the Type that represents the type argument T.
- `ReflectType reflect_func_of(Slice in, Slice out, bool variadic)` – FuncOf returns the function type with the given argument and result types.
- `ReflectType reflect_struct_of(Slice fields)` – StructOf returns the struct type containing fields.
- `bool reflect_method_is_exported(ReflectMethod m)` – IsExported reports whether the method is exported.
- `ReflectMethod reflect_type_method(ReflectType t, Int i)` – Method returns the i'th method in the type's method set.
- `bool reflect_type_method_by_name(ReflectType t, Str name, ReflectMethod *m)` – MethodByName returns the method with that name in the type's method set and a boolean indicating if the method was found.
- `Str reflect_value_error_error(ReflectValueError *e, Alloc *a)` – ValueError.Error: "reflect: call of reflect.Value.Int on string Value".
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_of(Any i)` – ValueOf returns a new Value initialized to the concrete value stored in the interface i.
- `ReflectValue reflect_indirect(ReflectValue v)` – Indirect returns the value that v points to.
- `ReflectKind reflect_value_kind(ReflectValue v)` – Kind returns v's Kind.
- `ReflectType reflect_value_type(ReflectValue v)` – Type returns v's type.
- `bool reflect_value_is_valid(ReflectValue v)` – IsValid reports whether v represents a value.
- `bool reflect_value_is_nil(ReflectValue v)` – IsNil reports whether its argument v is nil.
- `bool reflect_value_is_zero(ReflectValue v)` – IsZero reports whether v is the zero value for its type.
- `bool reflect_value_can_addr(ReflectValue v)` – CanAddr reports whether the value's address can be obtained with Value.Addr.
- `bool reflect_value_can_set(ReflectValue v)` – CanSet reports whether the value of v can be changed.
- `bool reflect_value_can_interface(ReflectValue v)` – CanInterface reports whether Value.Interface can be used without panicking.
- `Any reflect_value_interface(ReflectValue v)` – Interface returns v's current value as an interface{}.
- `bool reflect_type_assert(ReflectValue v, ReflectType t, void *out)` – TypeAssert is semantically equivalent to:
- `ReflectInterfaceData reflect_value_interface_data(ReflectValue v)` – InterfaceData returns a pair of unspecified uintptr values.
- `bool reflect_value_can_int(ReflectValue v)` – CanInt reports whether Int can be used without panicking.
- `bool reflect_value_can_uint(ReflectValue v)` – CanUint reports whether Value.Uint can be used without panicking.
- `bool reflect_value_can_float(ReflectValue v)` – CanFloat reports whether Value.Float can be used without panicking.
- `bool reflect_value_can_complex(ReflectValue v)` – CanComplex reports whether Value.Complex can be used without panicking.
- `bool reflect_value_bool(ReflectValue v)` – Bool returns v's underlying value.
- `int64_t reflect_value_int(ReflectValue v)` – Int returns v's underlying value, as an int64.
- `uint64_t reflect_value_uint(ReflectValue v)` – Uint returns v's underlying value, as a uint64.
- `double reflect_value_float(ReflectValue v)` – Float returns v's underlying value, as a float64.
- `Complex128 reflect_value_complex(ReflectValue v)` – Complex returns v's underlying value, as a complex128.
- `Str reflect_value_string(ReflectValue v)` – String returns the string v's underlying value, as a string.
- `Slice reflect_value_bytes(ReflectValue v)` – Bytes returns v's underlying value.
- `bool reflect_value_overflow_int(ReflectValue v, int64_t x)` – OverflowInt reports whether the int64 x cannot be represented by v's type.
- `bool reflect_value_overflow_uint(ReflectValue v, uint64_t x)` – OverflowUint reports whether the uint64 x cannot be represented by v's type.
- `bool reflect_value_overflow_float(ReflectValue v, double x)` – OverflowFloat reports whether the float64 x cannot be represented by v's type.
- `bool reflect_value_overflow_complex(ReflectValue v, Complex128 x)` – OverflowComplex reports whether the complex128 x cannot be represented by v's type.
- `Uintptr reflect_value_pointer(ReflectValue v)` – Pointer returns v's value as a uintptr.
- `void *reflect_value_unsafe_pointer(ReflectValue v)` – UnsafePointer returns v's value as a unsafe.Pointer.
- `Uintptr reflect_value_unsafe_addr(ReflectValue v)` – UnsafeAddr returns a pointer to v's data, as a uintptr.
- `Int reflect_value_len(ReflectValue v)` – Len returns v's length.
- `Int reflect_value_cap(ReflectValue v)` – Cap returns v's capacity.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_index(ReflectValue v, Int i)` – Index returns v's i'th element.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_elem(ReflectValue v)` – Elem returns the value that the interface v contains or that the pointer v points to.
- `Int reflect_value_num_field(ReflectValue v)` – NumField returns the number of fields in the struct v.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_field(ReflectValue v, Int i)` – Field returns the i'th field of the struct v.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_field_by_index(ReflectValue v, Slice index)` – FieldByIndex returns the nested field corresponding to index.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_field_by_index_err(ReflectValue v, Slice index, Error *err)` – FieldByIndexErr returns the nested field corresponding to index.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_field_by_name(ReflectValue v, Str name)` – FieldByName returns the struct field with the given name.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_field_by_name_func(ReflectValue v, ReflectNameMatch match)` – FieldByNameFunc returns the struct field with a name that satisfies the match function.
- `Int reflect_value_num_method(ReflectValue v)` – NumMethod returns the number of methods in the value's method set.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_method(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Int i)` – Method returns a function value corresponding to v's i'th method.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_method_by_name(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Str name)` – MethodByName returns a function value corresponding to the method of v with the given name.
- `Slice reflect_value_call(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Slice in)` – Call calls the function v with the input arguments in.
- `Slice reflect_value_call_slice(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Slice in)` – CallSlice calls the variadic function v with the input arguments in, assigning the slice in\[len(in)-1\] to v's final variadic argument.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_map_index(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue key)` – MapIndex returns the value associated with key in the map v.
- `Slice reflect_value_map_keys(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – MapKeys returns a slice containing all the keys present in the map, in unspecified order.
- `ReflectMapIter *reflect_value_map_range(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – MapRange returns a range iterator for a map.
- `bool reflect_map_iter_next(ReflectMapIter *it)` – Next advances the map iterator and reports whether there is another entry.
- `ReflectValue reflect_map_iter_key(ReflectMapIter *it)` – Key returns the key of iter's current map entry.
- `ReflectValue reflect_map_iter_value(ReflectMapIter *it)` – Value returns the value of iter's current map entry.
- `void reflect_map_iter_reset(ReflectMapIter *it, ReflectValue v)` – Reset modifies iter to iterate over v.
- `IterSeq reflect_value_seq(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – Seq returns an iter.Seq\[Value\] that loops over the elements of v.
- `IterSeq2 reflect_value_seq2(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – Seq2 returns an iter.Seq2\[Value, Value\] that loops over the elements of v.
- `IterSeq2 reflect_value_fields(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – Fields returns an iterator over each StructField of v along with its Value.
- `IterSeq2 reflect_value_methods(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – Methods returns an iterator over each Method of v along with the corresponding method Value; this is a function with v bound as the receiver.
- `bool reflect_value_comparable(ReflectValue v)` – Comparable reports whether the value v is comparable.
- `bool reflect_value_equal(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue u)` – Equal reports true if v is equal to u.
- `bool reflect_deep_equal(Any x, Any y)` – DeepEqual reports whether x and y are “deeply equal,” defined as follows.
- `SwapFunc reflect_swapper(Any s)` – Swapper returns a function that swaps the elements in the provided slice.
- `ReflectValue reflect_zero(ReflectType t)` – Zero returns a Value representing the zero value for the specified type.
- `ReflectValue reflect_new(Alloc *a, ReflectType t)` – New returns a Value representing a pointer to a new zero value for the specified type.
- `ReflectValue reflect_new_at(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, void *p)` – NewAt returns a Value representing a pointer to a value of the specified type, using p as that pointer.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_addr(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a)` – Addr returns a pointer value representing the address of v.
- `void reflect_value_set(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue x)` – Set assigns x to the value v.
- `void reflect_value_set_bool(ReflectValue v, bool x)` – SetBool sets v's underlying value.
- `void reflect_value_set_int(ReflectValue v, int64_t x)` – SetInt sets v's underlying value to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_uint(ReflectValue v, uint64_t x)` – SetUint sets v's underlying value to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_float(ReflectValue v, double x)` – SetFloat sets v's underlying value to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_complex(ReflectValue v, Complex128 x)` – SetComplex sets v's underlying value to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_string(ReflectValue v, Str x)` – SetString sets v's underlying value to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_bytes(ReflectValue v, Slice x)` – SetBytes sets v's underlying value.
- `void reflect_value_set_pointer(ReflectValue v, void *x)` – SetPointer sets the unsafe.Pointer value v to x.
- `void reflect_value_set_len(ReflectValue v, Int n)` – SetLen sets v's length to n.
- `void reflect_value_set_cap(ReflectValue v, Int n)` – SetCap sets v's capacity to n.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_convert(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, ReflectType t)` – Convert returns the value v converted to type t.
- `bool reflect_value_can_convert(ReflectValue v, ReflectType t)` – CanConvert reports whether the value v can be converted to type t.
- `void reflect_value_set_zero(ReflectValue v)` – SetZero sets v to be the zero value of v's type.
- `void reflect_value_set_map_index(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue key, ReflectValue elem)` – SetMapIndex sets the element associated with key in the map v to elem.
- `void reflect_value_set_iter_key(ReflectValue v, ReflectMapIter *it)` – SetIterKey assigns to v the key of iter's current map entry.
- `void reflect_value_set_iter_value(ReflectValue v, ReflectMapIter *it)` – SetIterValue assigns to v the value of iter's current map entry.
- `void reflect_value_grow(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Int n)` – Grow increases the slice's capacity, if necessary, to guarantee space for another n elements.
- `void reflect_value_clear(ReflectValue v)` – Clear clears the contents of a map or zeros the contents of a slice.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_slice(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Int i, Int j)` – Slice returns v\[i:j\].
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_slice3(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, Int i, Int j, Int k)` – Slice3 is the 3-index form of the slice operation: it returns v\[i:j:k\].
- `ReflectValue reflect_make_slice(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, Int len, Int cap)` – MakeSlice creates a new zero-initialized slice value for the specified slice type, length, and capacity.
- `ReflectValue reflect_make_map(Alloc *a, ReflectType t)` – MakeMap creates a new map with the specified type.
- `ReflectValue reflect_make_map_with_size(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, Int n)` – MakeMapWithSize creates a new map with the specified type and initial space for approximately n elements.
- `ReflectValue reflect_make_chan(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, Int buffer)` – MakeChan creates a new channel with the specified type and buffer size.
- `ReflectValue reflect_slice_at(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, void *p, Int n)` – SliceAt returns a Value representing a slice whose underlying data starts at p, with length and capacity equal to n.
- `ReflectValue reflect_make_func(Alloc *a, ReflectType t, ReflectMakeFuncImpl fn)` – MakeFunc returns a new function of the given Type that wraps the function fn.
- `ReflectValue reflect_append(Alloc *a, ReflectValue s, Slice x)` – Append appends the values x to a slice s and returns the resulting slice.
- `ReflectValue reflect_append_slice(Alloc *a, ReflectValue s, ReflectValue t)` – AppendSlice appends a slice t to a slice s and returns the resulting slice.
- `Int reflect_copy(ReflectValue dst, ReflectValue src)` – Copy copies the contents of src into dst until either dst has been filled or src has been exhausted.
- `void reflect_value_send(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue x)` – Send sends x on the channel v.
- `bool reflect_value_try_send(ReflectValue v, ReflectValue x)` – TrySend attempts to send x on the channel v but will not block.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_recv(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, bool *ok)` – Recv receives and returns a value from the channel v.
- `ReflectValue reflect_value_try_recv(ReflectValue v, Alloc *a, bool *ok)` – TryRecv attempts to receive a value from the channel v but will not block.
- `void reflect_value_close(ReflectValue v)` – Close closes the channel v.
- `Int reflect_select(Alloc *a, Slice cases, ReflectValue *recv, bool *recv_ok)` – Select executes a select operation described by the list of cases.

## burrow/regexp.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `regexp` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Regexp *regexp_compile(Alloc *a, Str expr, Error *err)` – Compile parses a regular expression and returns, if successful, a Regexp object that can be used to match against text.
- `Regexp *regexp_compile_posix(Alloc *a, Str expr, Error *err)` – CompilePOSIX is like Compile but restricts the regular expression to POSIX ERE (egrep) syntax and changes the match semantics to leftmost-longest.
- `Regexp *regexp_must_compile(Alloc *a, Str str)` – MustCompile is like Compile but panics if the expression cannot be parsed.
- `Regexp *regexp_must_compile_posix(Alloc *a, Str str)` – MustCompilePOSIX is like CompilePOSIX but panics if the expression cannot be parsed.
- `void regexp_free(Regexp *re)` – Gives back everything re holds.
- `Regexp *regexp_copy(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a)` – Copy returns a new Regexp object copied from re.
- `void regexp_longest(Regexp *re)` – Longest makes future searches prefer the leftmost-longest match.
- `Str regexp_string(const Regexp *re)` – String returns the source text used to compile the regular expression.
- `Int regexp_num_subexp(const Regexp *re)` – NumSubexp returns the number of parenthesized subexpressions in this Regexp.
- `Slice regexp_subexp_names(const Regexp *re)` – SubexpNames returns the names of the parenthesized subexpressions in this Regexp.
- `Int regexp_subexp_index(const Regexp *re, Str name)` – SubexpIndex returns the index of the first subexpression with the given name, or -1 if there is no subexpression with that name.
- `Str regexp_literal_prefix(const Regexp *re, bool *complete)` – LiteralPrefix returns a literal string that must begin any match of the regular expression re.
- `Str regexp_quote_meta(Alloc *a, Str s)` – QuoteMeta returns a string that escapes all regular expression metacharacters inside the argument text; the returned string is a regular expression matching the literal text.
- `bool regexp_match(const Regexp *re, Slice b)` – Match reports whether the byte slice b contains any match of the regular expression re.
- `bool regexp_match_string(const Regexp *re, Str s)` – MatchString reports whether the string s contains any match of the regular expression re.
- `bool regexp_match_reader(const Regexp *re, IoRuneReader r)` – MatchReader reports whether the text returned by the io.RuneReader contains any match of the regular expression re.
- `bool regexp_match_pattern(Alloc *a, Str pattern, Slice b, Error *err)` – Match reports whether the byte slice b contains any match of the regular expression pattern.
- `bool regexp_match_string_pattern(Alloc *a, Str pattern, Str s, Error *err)` – MatchString reports whether the string s contains any match of the regular expression pattern.
- `bool regexp_match_reader_pattern(Alloc *a, Str pattern, IoRuneReader r, Error *err)` – MatchReader reports whether the text returned by the io.RuneReader contains any match of the regular expression pattern.
- `Slice regexp_find(const Regexp *re, Slice b)` – Find returns the text of the leftmost match for re in b.
- `Str regexp_find_string(const Regexp *re, Str s)` – FindString returns the text of the leftmost match for re in s.
- `Slice regexp_find_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – FindIndex returns the location of the leftmost match for re in b.
- `Slice regexp_find_string_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s)` – FindStringIndex returns the location of the leftmost match for re in s.
- `Slice regexp_find_reader_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, IoRuneReader r)` – FindReaderIndex returns the location of the leftmost match for re in r.
- `Slice regexp_find_submatch(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – FindSubmatch returns the first match for re in b, including submatches.
- `Slice regexp_find_string_submatch(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s)` – FindStringSubmatch returns the first match for re in s, including submatches.
- `Slice regexp_find_submatch_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b)` – FindSubmatchIndex returns the first match for re in b, including submatches.
- `Slice regexp_find_string_submatch_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s)` – FindStringSubmatchIndex returns the first match for re in s, including submatches.
- `Slice regexp_find_reader_submatch_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, IoRuneReader r)` – FindReaderSubmatchIndex returns the first match for re in r, including submatches.
- `Slice regexp_find_all(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b, Int n)` – FindAll returns all the matches for re in b.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_string(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s, Int n)` – FindAllString returns all the matches for re in s.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b, Int n)` – FindAllIndex returns the locations of all matches for re in b.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_string_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s, Int n)` – FindAllStringIndex returns the locations of all matches for re in s.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_submatch(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b, Int n)` – FindAllSubmatch returns the locations of all matches for re in b, including submatch locations.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_string_submatch(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s, Int n)` – FindAllStringSubmatch returns the locations of all matches for re in s, including submatch locations.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_submatch_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b, Int n)` – FindAllSubmatchIndex returns the locations of all matches for re in b, including submatch locations.
- `Slice regexp_find_all_string_submatch_index(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s, Int n)` – FindAllStringSubmatchIndex returns the locations of all matches for re in s, including submatch locations.
- `Slice regexp_split(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str s, Int n)` – Split slices s into substrings separated by the expression and returns a slice of the substrings between those expression matches.
- `Slice regexp_replace_all(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice src, Slice repl)` – ReplaceAll returns a copy of src, replacing matches of the Regexp with the replacement text repl.
- `Str regexp_replace_all_string(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str src, Str repl)` – ReplaceAllString returns a copy of src, replacing matches of the Regexp with the replacement string repl.
- `Slice regexp_replace_all_literal(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice src, Slice repl)` – ReplaceAllLiteral returns a copy of src, replacing matches of the Regexp with the replacement bytes repl.
- `Str regexp_replace_all_literal_string(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str src, Str repl)` – ReplaceAllLiteralString returns a copy of src, replacing matches of the Regexp with the replacement string repl.
- `Slice regexp_replace_all_func(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice src, BytesFunc repl)` – ReplaceAllFunc returns a copy of src in which all matches of the Regexp have been replaced by the return value of function repl applied to the matched byte slice.
- `Str regexp_replace_all_string_func(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Str src, StrFunc repl)` – ReplaceAllStringFunc returns a copy of src in which all matches of the Regexp have been replaced by the return value of function repl applied to the matched substring.
- `Slice regexp_expand(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice dst, Slice template_, Slice src, Slice match)` – Expand appends template to dst and returns the result; during the append, Expand replaces variables in the template with corresponding matches drawn from src.
- `Slice regexp_expand_string(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str template_, Str src, Slice match)` – ExpandString is like Regexp.Expand but the template and source are strings.
- `Slice regexp_append_text(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – AppendText implements encoding.TextAppender.
- `Slice regexp_marshal_text(const Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – MarshalText implements encoding.TextMarshaler.
- `Error regexp_unmarshal_text(Regexp *re, Alloc *a, Slice text)` – UnmarshalText implements encoding.TextUnmarshaler by calling Compile on the encoded value.

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `regexp/syntax` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Str syntax_error_code_string(SyntaxErrorCode e)` – ErrorCode.String: the code itself.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNTAX_ERROR`

- `Error syntax_error_as_error(const SyntaxError *e, Alloc *a)` – An Error for e, built in a with its own copy of the text.
- `Str syntax_op_string(SyntaxOp op)` – Op.String: "Literal", "Concat" and so on, "Op(42)" for a number with no name.
- `SyntaxRegexp *syntax_parse(Alloc *a, Str s, SyntaxFlags flags, Error *err)` – Parse parses a regular expression string s, controlled by the specified Flags, and returns a regular expression parse tree.
- `void syntax_regexp_free(SyntaxRegexp *re)` – Gives a tree from syntax_parse or syntax_regexp_simplify back to its allocator, all of it.
- `bool syntax_regexp_equal(const SyntaxRegexp *x, const SyntaxRegexp *y)` – Equal reports whether x and y have identical structure.
- `Str syntax_regexp_string(const SyntaxRegexp *re, Alloc *a)` – Regexp.String: the pattern written back out, in a form that parses to the same tree, built in a.
- `Int syntax_regexp_max_cap(const SyntaxRegexp *re)` – MaxCap walks the regexp to find the maximum capture index.
- `Slice syntax_regexp_cap_names(const SyntaxRegexp *re, Alloc *a)` – CapNames walks the regexp to find the names of capturing groups.
- `SyntaxRegexp *syntax_regexp_simplify(const SyntaxRegexp *re, Alloc *a)` – Simplify returns a regexp equivalent to re but without counted repetitions and with various other simplifications, such as rewriting /(?:a+)+/ to /a+/.
- `Str syntax_inst_op_string(SyntaxInstOp op)` – InstOp.String: "InstAlt" and so on, "" for a number with no name.
- `SyntaxEmptyOp syntax_empty_op_context(Rune r1, Rune r2)` – EmptyOpContext returns the zero-width assertions satisfied at the position between the runes r1 and r2.
- `bool syntax_is_word_char(Rune r)` – IsWordChar reports whether r is considered a “word character” during the evaluation of the \\b and \\B zero-width assertions.
- `bool syntax_inst_match_rune(const SyntaxInst *i, Rune r)` – MatchRune reports whether the instruction matches (and consumes) r.
- `Int syntax_inst_match_rune_pos(const SyntaxInst *i, Rune r)` – MatchRunePos checks whether the instruction matches (and consumes) r.
- `bool syntax_inst_match_empty_width(const SyntaxInst *i, Rune before, Rune after)` – MatchEmptyWidth reports whether the instruction matches an empty string between the runes before and after.
- `Str syntax_inst_string(const SyntaxInst *i, Alloc *a)` – Inst.String: the instruction as the program listing shows it, in a.
- `SyntaxProg *syntax_compile(Alloc *a, const SyntaxRegexp *re, Error *err)` – Compile compiles the regexp into a program to be executed.
- `void syntax_prog_free(SyntaxProg *p)` – Gives a program from syntax_compile back to its allocator.
- `Str syntax_prog_string(const SyntaxProg *p, Alloc *a)` – Prog.String: the listing, one instruction a line, in a.
- `Str syntax_prog_prefix(const SyntaxProg *p, Alloc *a, bool *complete)` – Prefix returns a literal string that all matches for the regexp must start with.
- `SyntaxEmptyOp syntax_prog_start_cond(const SyntaxProg *p)` – StartCond returns the leading empty-width conditions that must be true in any match.

## burrow/runtime.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `runtime` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_RUNTIME_ERROR` – The descriptor, which is what errors_as matches on.
- `const RuntimeError *runtime_error_from(Any v)` – Go's recover().(runtime.Error), which is the question "did the runtime stop this, or did the program".
- `void runtime_throw(Str msg)` – Stop the program, the way Go's runtime.throw does: print
- `void runtime_panic(Str msg)` – Panic with a RuntimeError carrying msg, which is what every check below does and what Go's runtime does for the conditions it lets you recover from.
- `void runtime_index_out_of_range(Int i, Int len)` – runtime error: index out of range \[i\] with length len
- `void runtime_slice_bounds_out_of_range(Int lo, Int hi, Int cap)` – runtime error: slice bounds out of range \[lo:hi\] with capacity cap
- `void runtime_integer_divide_by_zero(void)` – runtime error: integer divide by zero
- `void runtime_negative_shift(void)` – runtime error: negative shift amount
- `void runtime_integer_overflow(void)` – runtime error: integer overflow
- `void runtime_set_fatal_handler(RuntimeFatalFunc fn)`
- `Int runtime_callers(Int skip, Slice pcs)` – Callers fills the slice pc with the return program counters of function invocations on the calling goroutine's stack.
- `bool runtime_caller(Int skip, Uintptr *pc, Str *file, Int *line)` – Caller reports file and line number information about function invocations on the calling goroutine's stack.
- `bool runtime_func_for_pc(Uintptr pc, RuntimeFunc *out)` – FuncForPC returns a \*\[Func\] describing the function that contains the given program counter address, or else nil.
- `RuntimeFrames runtime_callers_frames(Slice pcs)` – CallersFrames takes a slice of PC values returned by Callers and prepares to return function/file/line information.
- `bool runtime_frames_next(RuntimeFrames *it, RuntimeFrame *out)` – Next returns a Frame representing the next call frame in the slice of PC values.
- `Int runtime_stack(Slice buf, bool all)` – Stack formats a stack trace of the calling goroutine into buf and returns the number of bytes written to buf.
- `void runtime_set_cpu_profile_rate(Int hz)` – SetCPUProfileRate sets the CPU profiling rate to hz samples per second.
- `Error runtime_start_trace(void)` – StartTrace enables tracing for the current process.
- `void runtime_stop_trace(void)` – StopTrace stops tracing, if it was previously enabled.
- `Bytes runtime_read_trace(void)` – ReadTrace returns the next chunk of binary tracing data, blocking until data is available.
- `uint64_t runtime_rand64(void)`
- `uint64_t runtime_memhash(const void *p, size_t n, uint64_t seed)` – The hash the map uses for n bytes at p, mixed with seed.
- `void runtime_panic_unhashable(const Type *t)` – Panic the way Go does when something asks for the hash of a value whose type has none: "runtime error: hash of unhashable type \[\]uint8".
