## burrow/runtime/pprof.h

- `PprofLabelSet pprof_labels(Alloc *a, Slice args)` – Labels takes an even number of strings representing key-value pairs and makes a LabelSet containing them.
- `PprofLabelSet pprof_labels_v(Alloc *a, int n, ...)` – The same, with the n strings as arguments.
- `Context pprof_with_labels(Alloc *a, Context ctx, PprofLabelSet labels)` – WithLabels returns a new context.Context with the given labels added.
- `bool pprof_label(Context ctx, Str key, Str *value)` – Label returns the value of the label with the given key on ctx, and a boolean indicating whether that label exists.
- `void pprof_for_labels(Context ctx, PprofLabelFunc f)` – ForLabels invokes f with each label set on the context.
- `void pprof_set_goroutine_labels(Context ctx)` – SetGoroutineLabels sets the current goroutine's labels to match ctx.
- `void pprof_do(Alloc *a, Context ctx, PprofLabelSet labels, PprofDoFunc f)` – Do calls f with a copy of the parent context with the given labels added to the parent's label map.
- `PprofProfile *pprof_new_profile(Str name)` – NewProfile creates a new profile with the given name.
- `PprofProfile *pprof_lookup(Str name)` – Lookup returns the profile with the given name, or NULL if no such profile exists.
- `Slice pprof_profiles(Alloc *a)` – Profiles returns a slice of all the known profiles, sorted by name.
- `Str pprof_profile_name(const PprofProfile *p)` – Name returns this profile's name, which can be passed to pprof_lookup to reobtain the profile.
- `Int pprof_profile_count(PprofProfile *p)` – Count returns the number of execution stacks currently in the profile.
- `void pprof_profile_add(PprofProfile *p, Any value, Int skip)` – Add adds the current execution stack to the profile, associated with value.
- `void pprof_profile_remove(PprofProfile *p, Any value)` – Remove removes the execution stack associated with value from the profile.
- `Error pprof_profile_write_to(PprofProfile *p, IoWriter w, Int debug)` – WriteTo writes a pprof-formatted snapshot of the profile to w.
- `Error pprof_write_heap_profile(IoWriter w)` – WriteHeapProfile is shorthand for Lookup("heap").WriteTo(w, 0).
- `Error pprof_start_cpu_profile(IoWriter w)` – StartCPUProfile enables CPU profiling for the current process.
- `void pprof_stop_cpu_profile(void)` – StopCPUProfile stops the current CPU profile, if any.

## burrow/runtime/trace.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `runtime/trace` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Error trace_start(IoWriter w)` – Start enables tracing for the current program.
- `void trace_stop(void)` – Stop stops the current tracing, if any.
- `bool trace_is_enabled(void)` – IsEnabled reports whether tracing is enabled.
- `Context trace_new_task(Alloc *a, Context pctx, Str task_type, TraceTask **task)` – NewTask creates a task instance with the type taskType and returns it along with a Context that carries the task.
- `void trace_task_end(TraceTask *t)` – End marks the end of the operation represented by the Task.
- `void trace_log(Context ctx, Str category, Str message)` – Log emits a one-off event with the given category and message.
- `void trace_logf(Context ctx, Str category, Str format, Slice args)` – Logf is like Log, but the value is formatted using the specified format spec.
- `#define trace_logf_v(ctx, category, ...)` – trace_logf with its arguments written out, as fmt_sprintf_v takes them.
- `void trace_with_region(Context ctx, Str region_type, Func fn)` – WithRegion starts a region associated with its calling goroutine, runs fn, and then ends the region.
- `TraceRegion trace_start_region(Context ctx, Str region_type)` – StartRegion starts a region and returns it.
- `void trace_region_end(TraceRegion r)` – End marks the end of the traced code region.
- `TraceFlightRecorder *trace_new_flight_recorder(Alloc *a, TraceFlightRecorderConfig cfg)` – NewFlightRecorder creates a new flight recorder from the provided configuration.
- `void trace_flight_recorder_free(TraceFlightRecorder *fr)` – Not in Go: stops fr if it is recording and gives back everything it holds.
- `Error trace_flight_recorder_start(TraceFlightRecorder *fr)` – Start activates the flight recorder and begins recording trace data.
- `void trace_flight_recorder_stop(TraceFlightRecorder *fr)` – Stop ends recording of trace data.
- `bool trace_flight_recorder_enabled(const TraceFlightRecorder *fr)` – Enabled returns true if the flight recorder is active.
- `int64_t trace_flight_recorder_write_to(TraceFlightRecorder *fr, IoWriter w, Error *err)` – WriteTo snapshots the moving window tracked by the flight recorder.

## burrow/slice.h

- `Slice slice_make(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, Int len, Int cap)` – make(\[\]T, len, cap), zeroed, because Go's zero value rule is the language and not a convention.
- `Slice slice_nil(const Type *elem)` – The nil slice of a given element type.
- `bool slice_is_nil(Slice s)`
- `Slice slice_from(void *p, Int len, Int cap, const Type *elem)` – A slice over memory you already have, which does not copy and does not take ownership.
- `void *slice_at(Slice s, Int i)` – Go's s\[i\], bounds checked against len and not against cap, returning a pointer to the element rather than the element, because C cannot return a value whose type is only known at runtime.
- `Slice slice_sub3(Slice s, Int lo, Int hi, Int max)` – s\[lo:hi\] and s\[lo:hi:max\].
- `Slice slice_sub(Slice s, Int lo, Int hi)`
- `Slice slice_append(Alloc *a, Slice s, const void *elems, Int n)` – append(s, elems...), with Go's semantics including the part people trip on.
- `Slice slice_append_slice(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Slice src)` – append(dst, src...).
- `Int slice_copy(Slice dst, Slice src)` – copy(dst, src), returning the number of elements copied, which is the smaller of the two lengths.
- `Int slice_copy_str(Slice dst, Str src)` – copy(dst, src) where src is a string, which Go allows for a \[\]byte destination and which turns up in every buffer implementation there is.
- `Slice slice_from_str(Alloc *a, Str s)` – \[\]byte(s) and string(b), both of which copy in Go and both of which copy here.
- `Str str_from_slice(Alloc *a, Slice s)`
- `void *slice_at_fast(Slice s, Int i, size_t elem_size)` – The same two operations again, told the element size by the caller.
- `Slice slice_append_fast(Alloc *a, Slice s, const void *elem, size_t elem_size)`

## burrow/slices.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `slices` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool slices_equal(Slice s1, Slice s2)` – Equal reports whether two slices are equal: the same length and all elements equal.
- `bool slices_equal_func(Slice s1, Slice s2, SlicesEqualFunc eq)` – EqualFunc reports whether two slices are equal using an equality function on each pair of elements.
- `int slices_compare(Slice s1, Slice s2)` – Compare compares the elements of s1 and s2, using cmp.Compare on each pair of elements.
- `int slices_compare_func(Slice s1, Slice s2, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – CompareFunc is like Compare but uses a custom comparison function on each pair of elements.
- `Int slices_index(Slice s, const void *v)` – Index returns the index of the first occurrence of v in s, or -1 if not present.
- `Int slices_index_func(Slice s, SlicesPredFunc f)` – IndexFunc returns the first index i satisfying f(s\[i\]), or -1 if none do.
- `bool slices_contains(Slice s, const void *v)` – Contains reports whether v is present in s.
- `bool slices_contains_func(Slice s, SlicesPredFunc f)` – ContainsFunc reports whether at least one element e of s satisfies f(e).
- `Slice slices_insert(Alloc *a, Slice s, Int i, const void *v, Int m)` – Insert modifies s in place by inserting the values v...
- `Slice slices_delete(Slice s, Int i, Int j)` – Delete modifies s in place by removing the elements s\[i:j\], and returns the modified slice.
- `Slice slices_delete_func(Slice s, SlicesPredFunc del)` – DeleteFunc modifies s in place by removing any elements for which del returns true, and returns the modified slice.
- `Slice slices_replace(Alloc *a, Slice s, Int i, Int j, const void *v, Int m)` – Replace modifies s in place by replacing the elements s\[i:j\] with the given v, and returns the modified slice.
- `Slice slices_clone(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – Clone returns a copy of the slice.
- `Slice slices_compact(Slice s)` – Compact replaces consecutive runs of equal elements with a single copy.
- `Slice slices_compact_func(Slice s, SlicesEqualFunc eq)` – CompactFunc is like Compact but uses an equality function to compare elements.
- `Slice slices_grow(Alloc *a, Slice s, Int n)` – Grow increases the slice's capacity, if necessary, to guarantee space for another n elements.
- `Slice slices_clip(Slice s)` – Clip removes unused capacity from the slice, returning s\[:len(s):len(s)\].
- `void slices_reverse(Slice s)` – Reverse reverses the elements of the slice in place.
- `Slice slices_concat(Alloc *a, const Slice *slices, Int n)` – Concat returns a new slice concatenating the passed in slices.
- `Slice slices_repeat(Alloc *a, Slice x, Int count)` – Repeat returns a new slice that repeats the provided slice the given number of times.
- `void slices_sort(Slice x)` – Sort sorts a slice of any ordered type in ascending order.
- `void slices_sort_func(Slice x, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – SortFunc sorts the slice x in ascending order as determined by the cmp function.
- `void slices_sort_stable_func(Slice x, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – SortStableFunc sorts the slice x while keeping the original order of equal elements, using cmp to compare elements in the same way as SortFunc.
- `bool slices_is_sorted(Slice x)` – IsSorted reports whether x is sorted in ascending order.
- `bool slices_is_sorted_func(Slice x, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – IsSortedFunc reports whether x is sorted in ascending order, with cmp as the comparison function as defined by SortFunc.
- `const void *slices_min(Slice x)` – Min returns the minimal value in x.
- `const void *slices_max(Slice x)` – Max returns the maximal value in x.
- `const void *slices_min_func(Slice x, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – MinFunc returns the minimal value in x, using cmp to compare elements.
- `const void *slices_max_func(Slice x, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – MaxFunc returns the maximal value in x, using cmp to compare elements.
- `Int slices_binary_search(Slice x, const void *target, bool *found)` – BinarySearch searches for target in a sorted slice and returns the earliest position where target is found, or the position where target would appear in the sort order; it also returns a bool saying whether the target is really found in the slice.
- `Int slices_binary_search_func(Slice x, const void *target, SlicesCmpFunc cmp, bool *found)` – BinarySearchFunc works like BinarySearch, but uses a custom comparison function.
- `IterSeq2 slices_all(const Slice *s)` – All returns an iterator over index-value pairs in the slice in the usual order.
- `IterSeq2 slices_backward(const Slice *s)` – Backward returns an iterator over index-value pairs in the slice, traversing it backward with descending indices.
- `IterSeq slices_values(const Slice *s)` – Values returns an iterator that yields the slice elements in order.
- `Slice slices_append_seq(Alloc *a, Slice s, IterSeq seq)` – AppendSeq appends the values from seq to the slice and returns the extended slice.
- `Slice slices_collect(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, IterSeq seq)` – Collect collects values from seq into a new slice and returns it.
- `Slice slices_sorted(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, IterSeq seq)` – Sorted collects values from seq into a new slice, sorts the slice, and returns it.
- `Slice slices_sorted_func(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, IterSeq seq, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – SortedFunc collects values from seq into a new slice, sorts the slice using the comparison function, and returns it.
- `Slice slices_sorted_stable_func(Alloc *a, const Type *elem, IterSeq seq, SlicesCmpFunc cmp)` – SortedStableFunc collects values from seq into a new slice.
- `IterSeq slices_chunk(Alloc *a, Slice s, Int n)` – Chunk returns an iterator over consecutive sub-slices of up to n elements of s.
- `void slices_seq_free(Alloc *a, IterSeq seq)` – Frees what slices_chunk took from a.

## burrow/sort.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `sort` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `void sort_sort(SortInterface data)` – Sort sorts data in ascending order as determined by the Less method.
- `void sort_stable(SortInterface data)` – Stable sorts data in ascending order as determined by the Less method, while keeping the original order of equal elements.
- `bool sort_is_sorted(SortInterface data)` – IsSorted reports whether data is sorted.
- `SortReverse sort_reverse(SortInterface data)` – Reverse returns the reverse order for data.
- `SortInterface sort_reverse_as_sort_interface(SortReverse *r)`
- `void sort_slice(Slice x, SortLessFunc less)` – Slice sorts the slice x given the provided less function.
- `void sort_slice_stable(Slice x, SortLessFunc less)` – SliceStable sorts the slice x using the provided less function, keeping equal elements in their original order.
- `bool sort_slice_is_sorted(Slice x, SortLessFunc less)` – SliceIsSorted reports whether the slice x is sorted according to the provided less function.
- `Int sort_search(Int n, SortSearchFunc f)` – Search uses binary search to find and return the smallest index i in \[0, n) at which f(i) is true, assuming that on the range \[0, n), f(i) == true implies f(i+1) == true.
- `Int sort_find(Int n, SortFindFunc cmp, bool *found)` – Find uses binary search to find and return the smallest index i in \[0, n) at which cmp(i) \<= 0.
- `void sort_ints(Slice x)` – Ints sorts a slice of ints in increasing order.
- `void sort_float64s(Slice x)` – Float64s sorts a slice of float64s in increasing order.
- `void sort_strings(Slice x)` – Strings sorts a slice of strings in increasing order.
- `bool sort_ints_are_sorted(Slice x)` – IntsAreSorted reports whether the slice x is sorted in increasing order.
- `bool sort_float64s_are_sorted(Slice x)` – Float64sAreSorted reports whether the slice x is sorted in increasing order, with not-a-number (NaN) values before any other values.
- `bool sort_strings_are_sorted(Slice x)` – StringsAreSorted reports whether the slice x is sorted in increasing order.
- `Int sort_search_ints(Slice a, Int x)` – SearchInts searches for x in a sorted slice of ints and returns the index as specified by Search.
- `Int sort_search_float64s(Slice a, double x)` – SearchFloat64s searches for x in a sorted slice of float64s and returns the index as specified by Search.
- `Int sort_search_strings(Slice a, Str x)` – SearchStrings searches for x in a sorted slice of strings and returns the index as specified by Search.
- `Int sort_int_slice_len(SortIntSlice x)`
- `bool sort_int_slice_less(SortIntSlice x, Int i, Int j)`
- `void sort_int_slice_swap(SortIntSlice x, Int i, Int j)`
- `void sort_int_slice_sort(SortIntSlice x)` – Sort is a convenience method: x.Sort() calls Sort(x).
- `Int sort_int_slice_search(SortIntSlice p, Int x)` – Search returns the result of applying SearchInts to the receiver and x.
- `SortInterface sort_int_slice_as_sort_interface(SortIntSlice *x)`
- `Int sort_float64_slice_len(SortFloat64Slice x)` – Less puts a NaN before every other value, which is what makes it a strict weak ordering where a plain \< is not.
- `bool sort_float64_slice_less(SortFloat64Slice x, Int i, Int j)` – Less reports whether x\[i\] should be ordered before x\[j\], as required by the sort Interface.
- `void sort_float64_slice_swap(SortFloat64Slice x, Int i, Int j)`
- `void sort_float64_slice_sort(SortFloat64Slice x)` – Sort is a convenience method: x.Sort() calls Sort(x).
- `Int sort_float64_slice_search(SortFloat64Slice p, double x)` – Search returns the result of applying SearchFloat64s to the receiver and x.
- `SortInterface sort_float64_slice_as_sort_interface(SortFloat64Slice *x)`
- `Int sort_string_slice_len(SortStringSlice x)`
- `bool sort_string_slice_less(SortStringSlice x, Int i, Int j)`
- `void sort_string_slice_swap(SortStringSlice x, Int i, Int j)`
- `void sort_string_slice_sort(SortStringSlice x)` – Sort is a convenience method: x.Sort() calls Sort(x).
- `Int sort_string_slice_search(SortStringSlice p, Str x)` – Search returns the result of applying SearchStrings to the receiver and x.
- `SortInterface sort_string_slice_as_sort_interface(SortStringSlice *x)`

## burrow/strconv.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `strconv` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Error strconv_err_range` – ErrRange indicates that a value is out of range for the target type.
- `extern const Error strconv_err_syntax` – ErrSyntax indicates that a value does not have the right syntax for the target type.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_STRCONV_NUM_ERROR`
- `Str strconv_num_error_error(Alloc *a, const StrconvNumError *e)` – The text Go's Error method gives, such as
- `Error strconv_num_error_unwrap(const StrconvNumError *e)` – e-\>err, which is what errors_unwrap gives for one inside an Error.
- `Error strconv_num_error_as_error(Alloc *a, const StrconvNumError *e)` – An Error for a StrconvNumError you filled in yourself, the way Go code writes &strconv.NumError{...}.
- `int64_t strconv_parse_int(Str s, Int base, Int bit_size, Error *err)` – ParseInt interprets a string s in the given base (0, 2 to 36) and bit size (0 to 64) and returns the corresponding value i.
- `uint64_t strconv_parse_uint(Str s, Int base, Int bit_size, Error *err)` – ParseUint is like ParseInt but for unsigned numbers.
- `Int strconv_atoi(Str s, Error *err)` – Atoi is equivalent to ParseInt(s, 10, 0), converted to type int.
- `Str strconv_format_int(Alloc *a, int64_t i, Int base)` – FormatInt returns the string representation of i in the given base, for 2 \<= base \<= 36.
- `Str strconv_format_uint(Alloc *a, uint64_t i, Int base)` – FormatUint returns the string representation of i in the given base, for 2 \<= base \<= 36.
- `Str strconv_itoa(Alloc *a, Int i)` – Itoa is equivalent to FormatInt(int64(i), 10).
- `Slice strconv_append_int(Alloc *a, Slice dst, int64_t i, Int base)` – AppendInt appends the string form of the integer i, as generated by FormatInt, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_uint(Alloc *a, Slice dst, uint64_t i, Int base)` – AppendUint appends the string form of the unsigned integer i, as generated by FormatUint, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `bool strconv_parse_bool(Str s, Error *err)` – ParseBool returns the boolean value represented by the string.
- `Str strconv_format_bool(bool b)` – FormatBool returns "true" or "false" according to the value of b.
- `Slice strconv_append_bool(Alloc *a, Slice dst, bool b)` – AppendBool appends "true" or "false", according to the value of b, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `double strconv_parse_float(Str s, Int bit_size, Error *err)` – ParseFloat converts the string s to a floating-point number with the precision specified by bitSize: 32 for float32, or 64 for float64.
- `Str strconv_format_float(Alloc *a, double f, Byte fmt, Int prec, Int bit_size)` – FormatFloat converts the floating-point number f to a string, according to the format fmt and precision prec.
- `Slice strconv_append_float(Alloc *a, Slice dst, double f, Byte fmt, Int prec, Int bit_size)` – AppendFloat appends the string form of the floating-point number f, as generated by FormatFloat, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Complex128 strconv_parse_complex(Str s, Int bit_size, Error *err)` – ParseComplex converts the string s to a complex number with the precision specified by bitSize: 64 for complex64, or 128 for complex128.
- `Str strconv_format_complex(Alloc *a, Complex128 c, Byte fmt, Int prec, Int bit_size)` – FormatComplex converts the complex number c to a string of the form (a+bi) where a and b are the real and imaginary parts, formatted according to the format fmt and precision prec.
- `Str strconv_quote(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Quote returns a double-quoted Go string literal representing s.
- `Str strconv_quote_to_ascii(Alloc *a, Str s)` – QuoteToASCII returns a double-quoted Go string literal representing s.
- `Str strconv_quote_to_graphic(Alloc *a, Str s)` – QuoteToGraphic returns a double-quoted Go string literal representing s.
- `Str strconv_quote_rune(Alloc *a, Rune r)` – QuoteRune returns a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune.
- `Str strconv_quote_rune_to_ascii(Alloc *a, Rune r)` – QuoteRuneToASCII returns a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune.
- `Str strconv_quote_rune_to_graphic(Alloc *a, Rune r)` – QuoteRuneToGraphic returns a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str s)` – AppendQuote appends a double-quoted Go string literal representing s, as generated by Quote, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote_to_ascii(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str s)` – AppendQuoteToASCII appends a double-quoted Go string literal representing s, as generated by QuoteToASCII, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote_to_graphic(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Str s)` – AppendQuoteToGraphic appends a double-quoted Go string literal representing s, as generated by QuoteToGraphic, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote_rune(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Rune r)` – AppendQuoteRune appends a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune, as generated by QuoteRune, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote_rune_to_ascii(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Rune r)` – AppendQuoteRuneToASCII appends a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune, as generated by QuoteRuneToASCII, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `Slice strconv_append_quote_rune_to_graphic(Alloc *a, Slice dst, Rune r)` – AppendQuoteRuneToGraphic appends a single-quoted Go character literal representing the rune, as generated by QuoteRuneToGraphic, to dst and returns the extended buffer.
- `bool strconv_can_backquote(Str s)` – CanBackquote reports whether the string s can be represented unchanged as a single-line backquoted string without control characters other than tab.
- `Str strconv_unquote(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – Unquote interprets s as a single-quoted, double-quoted, or backquoted Go string literal, returning the string value that s quotes.
- `Str strconv_quoted_prefix(Str s, Error *err)` – QuotedPrefix returns the quoted string (as understood by Unquote) at the prefix of s.
- `Rune strconv_unquote_char(Str s, Byte quote, bool *multibyte, Str *tail, Error *err)` – UnquoteChar decodes the first character or byte in the escaped string or character literal represented by the string s.
- `bool strconv_is_print(Rune r)` – IsPrint reports whether the rune is defined as printable by Go, with the same definition as unicode.IsPrint: letters, numbers, punctuation, symbols and ASCII space.
- `bool strconv_is_graphic(Rune r)` – IsGraphic reports whether the rune is defined as a Graphic by Unicode.

## burrow/strings.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `strings` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `Int strings_compare(Str a, Str b)` – Compare returns an integer comparing two strings lexicographically.
- `bool strings_equal_fold(Str s, Str t)` – EqualFold reports whether s and t, interpreted as UTF-8 strings, are equal under simple Unicode case-folding, which is a more general form of case-insensitivity.
- `bool strings_has_prefix(Str s, Str prefix)` – HasPrefix reports whether the string s begins with prefix.
- `bool strings_has_suffix(Str s, Str suffix)` – HasSuffix reports whether the string s ends with suffix.
- `bool strings_contains(Str s, Str substr)` – Contains reports whether substr is within s.
- `bool strings_contains_any(Str s, Str chars)` – ContainsAny reports whether any Unicode code points in chars are within s.
- `bool strings_contains_rune(Str s, Rune r)` – ContainsRune reports whether the Unicode code point r is within s.
- `bool strings_contains_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – ContainsFunc reports whether any Unicode code points r within s satisfy f(r).
- `Int strings_count(Str s, Str substr)` – Count counts the number of non-overlapping instances of substr in s.
- `Int strings_index(Str s, Str substr)` – Index returns the index of the first instance of substr in s, or -1 if substr is not present in s.
- `Int strings_index_any(Str s, Str chars)` – IndexAny returns the index of the first instance of any Unicode code point from chars in s, or -1 if no Unicode code point from chars is present in s.
- `Int strings_index_byte(Str s, Byte c)` – IndexByte returns the index of the first instance of c in s, or -1 if c is not present in s.
- `Int strings_index_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – IndexFunc returns the index into s of the first Unicode code point satisfying f(c), or -1 if none do.
- `Int strings_index_rune(Str s, Rune r)` – IndexRune returns the index of the first instance of the Unicode code point r, or -1 if rune is not present in s.
- `Int strings_last_index(Str s, Str substr)` – LastIndex returns the index of the last instance of substr in s, or -1 if substr is not present in s.
- `Int strings_last_index_any(Str s, Str chars)` – LastIndexAny returns the index of the last instance of any Unicode code point from chars in s, or -1 if no Unicode code point from chars is present in s.
- `Int strings_last_index_byte(Str s, Byte c)` – LastIndexByte returns the index of the last instance of c in s, or -1 if c is not present in s.
- `Int strings_last_index_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – LastIndexFunc returns the index into s of the last Unicode code point satisfying f(c), or -1 if none do.
- `Str strings_cut(Str s, Str sep, Str *after, bool *found)` – Cut slices s around the first instance of sep, returning the text before and after sep.
- `Str strings_cut_last(Str s, Str sep, Str *after, bool *found)` – CutLast slices s around the last instance of sep, returning the text before and after sep.
- `Str strings_cut_prefix(Str s, Str prefix, bool *found)` – CutPrefix returns s without the provided leading prefix string and reports whether it found the prefix.
- `Str strings_cut_suffix(Str s, Str suffix, bool *found)` – CutSuffix returns s without the provided ending suffix string and reports whether it found the suffix.
- `Str strings_trim(Str s, Str cutset)` – Trim returns a slice of the string s with all leading and trailing Unicode code points contained in cutset removed.
- `Str strings_trim_left(Str s, Str cutset)` – TrimLeft returns a slice of the string s with all leading Unicode code points contained in cutset removed.
- `Str strings_trim_right(Str s, Str cutset)` – TrimRight returns a slice of the string s, with all trailing Unicode code points contained in cutset removed.
- `Str strings_trim_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimFunc returns a slice of the string s with all leading and trailing Unicode code points c satisfying f(c) removed.
- `Str strings_trim_left_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimLeftFunc returns a slice of the string s with all leading Unicode code points c satisfying f(c) removed.
- `Str strings_trim_right_func(Str s, RuneFunc f)` – TrimRightFunc returns a slice of the string s with all trailing Unicode code points c satisfying f(c) removed.
- `Str strings_trim_space(Str s)` – TrimSpace returns a slice (substring) of the string s, with all leading and trailing white space removed, as defined by Unicode.
- `Str strings_trim_prefix(Str s, Str prefix)` – TrimPrefix returns s without the provided leading prefix string.
- `Str strings_trim_suffix(Str s, Str suffix)` – TrimSuffix returns s without the provided trailing suffix string.
- `Slice strings_split(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep)` – Split slices s into all substrings separated by sep and returns a slice of the substrings between those separators.
- `Slice strings_split_n(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep, Int n)` – SplitN slices s into substrings separated by sep and returns a slice of the substrings between those separators.
- `Slice strings_split_after(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep)` – SplitAfter slices s into all substrings after each instance of sep and returns a slice of those substrings.
- `Slice strings_split_after_n(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep, Int n)` – SplitAfterN slices s into substrings after each instance of sep and returns a slice of those substrings.
- `Slice strings_fields(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Fields splits the string s around each instance of one or more consecutive white space characters, as defined by unicode.IsSpace, returning a slice of substrings of s or an empty slice if s contains only white space.
- `Slice strings_fields_func(Alloc *a, Str s, RuneFunc f)` – FieldsFunc splits the string s at each run of Unicode code points c satisfying f(c) and returns an array of slices of s.
- `IterSeq strings_lines(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Lines returns an iterator over the newline-terminated lines in the string s.
- `IterSeq strings_split_seq(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep)` – SplitSeq returns an iterator over all substrings of s separated by sep.
- `IterSeq strings_split_after_seq(Alloc *a, Str s, Str sep)` – SplitAfterSeq returns an iterator over substrings of s split after each instance of sep.
- `IterSeq strings_fields_seq(Alloc *a, Str s)` – FieldsSeq returns an iterator over substrings of s split around runs of whitespace characters, as defined by unicode.IsSpace.
- `IterSeq strings_fields_func_seq(Alloc *a, Str s, RuneFunc f)` – FieldsFuncSeq returns an iterator over substrings of s split around runs of Unicode code points satisfying f(c).
- `void strings_seq_free(Alloc *a, IterSeq seq)` – Frees the state of a sequence from one of the functions above, which must have come from a.
- `Str strings_clone(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Clone returns a fresh copy of s.
- `Str strings_join(Alloc *a, Slice elems, Str sep)` – Join concatenates the elements of its first argument to create a single string.
- `Str strings_repeat(Alloc *a, Str s, Int count)` – Repeat returns a new string consisting of count copies of the string s.
- `Str strings_replace(Alloc *a, Str s, Str old, Str repl, Int n)` – Replace returns a copy of the string s with the first n non-overlapping instances of old replaced by new.
- `Str strings_replace_all(Alloc *a, Str s, Str old, Str repl)` – ReplaceAll returns a copy of the string s with all non-overlapping instances of old replaced by new.
- `Str strings_map(Alloc *a, RuneMapFunc mapping, Str s)` – Map returns a copy of the string s with all its characters modified according to the mapping function.
- `Str strings_to_upper(Alloc *a, Str s)` – ToUpper returns s with all Unicode letters mapped to their upper case.
- `Str strings_to_lower(Alloc *a, Str s)` – ToLower returns s with all Unicode letters mapped to their lower case.
- `Str strings_to_title(Alloc *a, Str s)` – ToTitle returns a copy of the string s with all Unicode letters mapped to their Unicode title case.
- `Str strings_to_upper_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Str s)` – ToUpperSpecial returns a copy of the string s with all Unicode letters mapped to their upper case using the case mapping specified by c.
- `Str strings_to_lower_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Str s)` – ToLowerSpecial returns a copy of the string s with all Unicode letters mapped to their lower case using the case mapping specified by c.
- `Str strings_to_title_special(Alloc *a, UnicodeSpecialCase c, Str s)` – ToTitleSpecial returns a copy of the string s with all Unicode letters mapped to their Unicode title case, giving priority to the special casing rules.
- `Str strings_to_valid_utf8(Alloc *a, Str s, Str replacement)` – ToValidUTF8 returns a copy of the string s with each run of invalid UTF-8 byte sequences replaced by the replacement string, which may be empty.
- `Str strings_title(Alloc *a, Str s)` – Title returns a copy of the string s with all Unicode letters that begin words mapped to their Unicode title case.
- `Str strings_builder_string(StringsBuilder *b)` – String returns the accumulated string.
- `Int strings_builder_len(StringsBuilder *b)` – Len returns the number of accumulated bytes; b.Len() == len(b.String()).
- `Int strings_builder_cap(StringsBuilder *b)` – Cap returns the capacity of the builder's underlying byte slice.
- `void strings_builder_reset(StringsBuilder *b)` – Reset resets the Builder to be empty.
- `bool strings_builder_grow(StringsBuilder *b, Int n)` – Grow grows b's capacity, if necessary, to guarantee space for another n bytes.
- `Int strings_builder_write(StringsBuilder *b, Slice p, Error *err)` – Write appends the contents of p to b's buffer.
- `Error strings_builder_write_byte(StringsBuilder *b, Byte c)` – WriteByte appends the byte c to b's buffer.
- `Int strings_builder_write_rune(StringsBuilder *b, Rune r, Error *err)` – WriteRune appends the UTF-8 encoding of Unicode code point r to b's buffer.
- `Int strings_builder_write_string(StringsBuilder *b, Str s, Error *err)` – WriteString appends the contents of s to b's buffer.
- `IoWriter strings_builder_as_io_writer(StringsBuilder *b)` – The builder as an io.Writer, for fmt_fprintf and io_copy.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_STRINGS_BUILDER`
- `StringsReader *strings_new_reader(Alloc *a, Str s)` – NewReader returns a new Reader reading from s.
- `void strings_reader_reset(StringsReader *r, Str s)` – Reset resets the Reader to be reading from s.
- `Int strings_reader_len(StringsReader *r)` – Len returns the number of bytes of the unread portion of the string.
- `int64_t strings_reader_size(StringsReader *r)` – Size returns the original length of the underlying string.
- `Int strings_reader_read(StringsReader *r, Slice b, Error *err)` – Read implements the io.Reader interface.
- `Int strings_reader_read_at(StringsReader *r, Slice b, int64_t off, Error *err)` – ReadAt implements the io.ReaderAt interface.
- `Byte strings_reader_read_byte(StringsReader *r, Error *err)` – ReadByte implements the io.ByteReader interface.
- `Error strings_reader_unread_byte(StringsReader *r)` – UnreadByte implements the io.ByteScanner interface.
- `Rune strings_reader_read_rune(StringsReader *r, Int *size, Error *err)` – ReadRune implements the io.RuneReader interface.

- `int64_t strings_reader_seek(StringsReader *r, int64_t offset, Int whence, Error *err)` – Seek implements the io.Seeker interface.

## burrow/sync.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `sync` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_MUTEX` – The descriptor, so that a Mutex can be the receiver behind a SyncLocker and so that an Any holding one can be asserted back.
- `void sync_mutex_lock(SyncMutex *m)` – Lock locks m.
- `bool sync_mutex_try_lock(SyncMutex *m)` – TryLock tries to lock m and reports whether it succeeded.
- `void sync_mutex_unlock(SyncMutex *m)` – Unlock unlocks m.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_RW_MUTEX`
- `void sync_rw_mutex_lock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – Lock locks rw for writing.
- `bool sync_rw_mutex_try_lock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – TryLock tries to lock rw for writing and reports whether it succeeded.
- `void sync_rw_mutex_unlock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – Unlock unlocks rw for writing.
- `void sync_rw_mutex_r_lock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – RLock locks rw for reading.
- `bool sync_rw_mutex_try_r_lock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – TryRLock tries to lock rw for reading and reports whether it succeeded.
- `void sync_rw_mutex_r_unlock(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – RUnlock undoes a single RWMutex.RLock call; it does not affect other simultaneous readers.
- `SyncLocker sync_mutex_locker(SyncMutex *m)` – A Mutex as a Locker.
- `SyncLocker sync_rw_mutex_locker(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – An RWMutex's write lock as a Locker, for the same reason.
- `SyncLocker sync_rw_mutex_r_locker(SyncRWMutex *rw)` – RLocker returns a Locker interface that implements the Locker.Lock and Locker.Unlock methods by calling rw.RLock and rw.RUnlock.
- `void sync_locker_lock(SyncLocker l)` – Takes the lock, whichever lock it is.
- `void sync_locker_unlock(SyncLocker l)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_WAIT_GROUP`
- `void sync_wait_group_add(SyncWaitGroup *wg, Int delta)` – Add adds delta, which may be negative, to the WaitGroup task counter.
- `void sync_wait_group_done(SyncWaitGroup *wg)` – Done decrements the WaitGroup task counter by one.
- `void sync_wait_group_wait(SyncWaitGroup *wg)` – Wait blocks until the WaitGroup task counter is zero.
- `bool sync_wait_group_go(SyncWaitGroup *wg, Func f)` – Go calls f in a new goroutine and adds that task to the WaitGroup.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_ONCE`
- `void sync_once_do(SyncOnce *o, Func f)` – Do calls the function f if and only if Do is being called for the first time for this instance of Once.
- `void sync_once_func_call(SyncOnceFunc *of)` – Runs f once, and re-raises its panic on this and every later call if it panicked.
- `Func sync_once_func_fn(SyncOnceFunc *of)` – The same thing as a Func, for handing to something that takes one.
- `Any sync_once_value_get(SyncOnceValue *ov)` – Runs f once and gives back what it returned, on this and every later call.
- `void sync_once_values_get(SyncOnceValues *ov, Any *a, Any *b)` – Runs f once and writes both of its results through a and b, on this and every later call.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_COND` – The descriptor, so that an Any holding a Cond can be asserted back.
- `void sync_cond_wait(SyncCond *c)` – Wait atomically unlocks c.L and suspends execution of the calling goroutine.
- `void sync_cond_signal(SyncCond *c)` – Signal wakes one goroutine waiting on c, if there is any.
- `void sync_cond_broadcast(SyncCond *c)` – Broadcast wakes all goroutines waiting on c.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYNC_MAP` – The descriptor, so that an Any holding a Map can be asserted back.
- `bool sync_map_load(SyncMap *m, const void *key, void *out_val)` – Load returns the value stored in the map for a key, or nil if no value is present.
- `bool sync_map_store(SyncMap *m, const void *key, const void *val)` – Store sets the value for a key.
- `bool sync_map_swap(SyncMap *m, const void *key, const void *val, void *out_prev, bool *out_loaded)` – Swap swaps the value for a key and returns the previous value if any.
- `bool sync_map_load_or_store(SyncMap *m, const void *key, const void *val, void *out_actual, bool *out_loaded)` – LoadOrStore returns the existing value for the key if present.
- `bool sync_map_compare_and_swap(SyncMap *m, const void *key, const void *old, const void *val, bool *out_swapped)` – CompareAndSwap swaps the old and new values for key if the value stored in the map is equal to old.
- `bool sync_map_load_and_delete(SyncMap *m, const void *key, void *out_val)` – LoadAndDelete deletes the value for a key, returning the previous value if any.
- `void sync_map_delete(SyncMap *m, const void *key)` – Delete deletes the value for a key.
- `bool sync_map_compare_and_delete(SyncMap *m, const void *key, const void *old)` – CompareAndDelete deletes the entry for key if its value is equal to old.
- `bool sync_map_clear(SyncMap *m)` – Clear deletes all the entries, resulting in an empty Map.
- `void sync_map_range(SyncMap *m, SyncMapRangeFunc f, void *arg)` – Range calls f sequentially for each key and value present in the map.
- `void sync_map_free(SyncMap *m)` – Hands every node back to the allocator, and leaves the map empty and still usable, the way a freshly initialised one is.
- `Any sync_pool_get(SyncPool *p)` – Get selects an arbitrary item from the Pool, removes it from the Pool, and returns it to the caller.
- `void sync_pool_put(SyncPool *p, Any v)` – Put adds x to the pool.
- `void sync_pool_free(SyncPool *p)` – Hands everything in the pool to free_fn, gives the per P arrays back to the allocator, and leaves the pool empty and still usable.

## burrow/synctest.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `testing/synctest` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `bool synctest_run(Func f)` – Runs f in a new bubble and waits for every goroutine in it to finish.
- `void synctest_test(TestingT *t, TestingTFunc f)` – Test executes f in a new bubble.
- `void synctest_wait(void)` – Wait blocks until every goroutine within the current bubble, other than the current goroutine, is durably blocked.
- `void synctest_sleep(Duration d)` – Sleep blocks until the current bubble's clock has advanced by the duration of d and every goroutine within the current bubble, other than the current goroutine, is durably blocked.

## burrow/syscall.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `syscall` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_ERRNO` – The descriptor of Errno, for errors_as.
- `Str syscall_errno_error(SyscallErrno e, Alloc *a)` – Errno.Error: the text for e, built in a.
- `Error syscall_errno_as_error(SyscallErrno e, Alloc *a)` – e as an Error, in one allocation from a.
- `bool syscall_errno_is(SyscallErrno e, Error target)` – Errno.Is: whether e counts as target, which is one of fs_err_permission, fs_err_exist, fs_err_not_exist or errors_err_unsupported.
- `bool syscall_errno_timeout(SyscallErrno e)` – Errno.Timeout: EAGAIN, EWOULDBLOCK and ETIMEDOUT.
- `bool syscall_errno_temporary(SyscallErrno e)` – Errno.Temporary: EINTR, EMFILE, ENFILE everywhere but Windows, and the timeouts.
- `SyscallErrno syscall_errno_from_pal(PalErrno e)` – The Errno for a PAL failure.
- `Str syscall_signal_string(SyscallSignal s, Alloc *a)` – Signal.String: Go's name for s on this system, such as "interrupt" or "killed", and "signal " and the number for one it has no name for, built in a.
- `void syscall_signal_signal(SyscallSignal s)` – Signal.Signal, which does nothing.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SIGNAL` – The descriptor of Signal, which os_signal_from_syscall's OsSignal has as its self_type.
- `Error syscall_kill(Int pid, SyscallSignal sig)` – kill(2): send sig to pid, which reaches a process group when it is 0 or negative, as it does in C. The error is the Errno, from error_allocator.
- `extern SyncRWMutex syscall_fork_lock` – ForkLock is not used on plan9.
- `Int syscall_start_process(Str argv0, Slice argv, const SyscallProcAttr *attr, Uintptr *handle, Error *err)` – StartProcess wraps ForkExec for package os.
- `Error syscall_exec(Str argv0, Slice argv, Slice envv)` – Ordinary exec.
- `Str syscall_escape_arg(Alloc *a, Str s)` – EscapeArg rewrites command line argument s as prescribed in https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ms880421.
- `Str syscall_full_path(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – FullPath retrieves the full path of the specified file.
- `void syscall_close_on_exec(SyscallHandle fd)` – syscall.CloseOnExec: fd stops being inherited.
- `Error syscall_set_nonblock(SyscallHandle fd, bool nonblocking)`
- `Int syscall_fork_exec(Str argv0, Slice argv, const SyscallProcAttr *attr, Error *err)` – Combination of fork and exec, careful to be thread safe.
- `Error syscall_setgroups(Slice gids)` – syscall.Setgroups: the supplementary groups, a slice of Int.
- `Error syscall_setuid(Int uid)` – syscall.Setuid and the rest of the family.
- `Error syscall_setgid(Int gid)`
- `Error syscall_seteuid(Int euid)`
- `Error syscall_setegid(Int egid)`
- `Error syscall_setreuid(Int ruid, Int euid)`
- `Error syscall_setregid(Int rgid, Int egid)`
- `Error syscall_setresuid(Int ruid, Int euid, Int suid)`
- `Error syscall_setresgid(Int rgid, Int egid, Int sgid)`
- `Uintptr syscall_all_threads_syscall(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – AllThreadsSyscall performs a syscall on each OS thread of the Go runtime.
- `Uintptr syscall_all_threads_syscall6(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr a4, Uintptr a5, Uintptr a6, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – AllThreadsSyscall6 is like AllThreadsSyscall, but extended to six arguments.
- `bool syscall_wait_status_exited(SyscallWaitStatus w)` – The WaitStatus methods.
- `Int syscall_wait_status_exit_status(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `bool syscall_wait_status_signaled(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `SyscallSignal syscall_wait_status_signal(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `bool syscall_wait_status_core_dump(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `bool syscall_wait_status_stopped(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `bool syscall_wait_status_continued(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `SyscallSignal syscall_wait_status_stop_signal(SyscallWaitStatus w)`
- `Int syscall_wait_status_trap_cause(SyscallWaitStatus w)` – The ptrace event of a process stopped at a SIGTRAP, on Linux.
- `int64_t syscall_timespec_unix(const SyscallTimespec *ts, int64_t *nsec)` – Unix returns the time stored in ts as seconds plus nanoseconds.
- `int64_t syscall_timeval_unix(const SyscallTimeval *tv, int64_t *nsec)` – Unix returns the time stored in tv as seconds plus nanoseconds.
- `int64_t syscall_timespec_nano(const SyscallTimespec *ts)` – Nano returns the time stored in ts as nanoseconds.
- `int64_t syscall_timeval_nano(const SyscallTimeval *tv)` – Nano returns the time stored in tv as nanoseconds.
- `int64_t syscall_timespec_to_nsec(SyscallTimespec ts)` – TimespecToNsec returns the time stored in ts as nanoseconds.
- `int64_t syscall_timeval_nanoseconds(const SyscallTimeval *tv)` – Timeval.Nanoseconds, which is Nano under the name Windows has.
- `int64_t syscall_timeval_to_nsec(SyscallTimeval tv)` – TimevalToNsec returns the time stored in tv as nanoseconds.
- `SyscallTimespec syscall_nsec_to_timespec(int64_t nsec)` – NsecToTimespec converts a number of nanoseconds into a Timespec.
- `SyscallTimeval syscall_nsec_to_timeval(int64_t nsec)` – NsecToTimeval converts a number of nanoseconds into a Timeval.
- `Str syscall_getenv(Alloc *a, Str key, bool *found)` – syscall.Getenv: the value of key, from a, and in \*found whether it is set at all.
- `Error syscall_setenv(Str key, Str value)` – syscall.Setenv: set key to value.
- `Error syscall_unsetenv(Str key)` – syscall.Unsetenv, which is not an error when key is not set.
- `void syscall_clearenv(void)` – syscall.Clearenv: unset everything.
- `Slice syscall_environ(Alloc *a)` – syscall.Environ: a Slice of Str, "key=value" each, all from a.
- `int64_t syscall_filetime_nanoseconds(const SyscallFiletime *ft)` – Nanoseconds returns Filetime ft in nanoseconds since Epoch (00:00:00 UTC, January 1, 1970).
- `SyscallFiletime syscall_nsec_to_filetime(int64_t nsec)` – NsecToFiletime: the other way.
- `Int syscall_wait4(Int pid, SyscallWaitStatus *wstatus, Int options, SyscallRusage *rusage, Error *err)` – wait4(2): wait for pid and give back its id.
- `SyscallRusage syscall_rusage_from_pal(const PalRusage *ru)` – The Rusage for what pal_wait4 filled in, as syscall_errno_from_pal is the Errno for a PalErrno.
- `uint8_t *syscall_byte_ptr_from_string(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – BytePtrFromString returns a pointer to a NUL-terminated array of bytes containing the text of s.
- `Slice syscall_byte_slice_from_string(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – ByteSliceFromString returns a NUL-terminated slice of bytes containing the text of s.
- `Slice syscall_string_byte_slice(Alloc *a, Str s)` – StringByteSlice converts a string to a NUL-terminated \[\]byte, If s contains a NUL byte this function panics instead of returning an error.
- `uint8_t *syscall_string_byte_ptr(Alloc *a, Str s)` – StringBytePtr returns a pointer to a NUL-terminated array of bytes.
- `Int syscall_getpagesize(void)` – syscall.Getpagesize: the size of a memory page.
- `void syscall_exit(Int code)` – syscall.Exit: end the process now with code, running nothing on the way out, as Go's does.
- `Uintptr syscall_syscall(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – Deprecated: Use SyscallN instead.
- `Uintptr syscall_syscall6(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr a4, Uintptr a5, Uintptr a6, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – Deprecated: Use SyscallN instead.
- `Uintptr syscall_raw_syscall(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)`
- `Uintptr syscall_raw_syscall6(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr a4, Uintptr a5, Uintptr a6, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)`
- `Uintptr syscall_syscall9(Uintptr trap, Uintptr a1, Uintptr a2, Uintptr a3, Uintptr a4, Uintptr a5, Uintptr a6, Uintptr a7, Uintptr a8, Uintptr a9, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – Deprecated: Use SyscallN instead.
- `extern Int syscall_stdin` – syscall.Stdin, Stdout and Stderr: 0, 1 and 2, as variables, since Go has them as variables.
- `extern Int syscall_stdout`
- `extern Int syscall_stderr`
- `Int syscall_read(Int fd, Slice p, Error *err)` – read(2), write(2), pread(2) and pwrite(2) on p's bytes.
- `Int syscall_write(Int fd, Slice p, Error *err)`
- `Int syscall_pread(Int fd, Slice p, int64_t offset, Error *err)`
- `Int syscall_pwrite(Int fd, Slice p, int64_t offset, Error *err)`
- `Slice syscall_slice_ptr_from_strings(Alloc *a, Slice ss, Error *err)` – SlicePtrFromStrings converts a slice of strings to a slice of pointers to NUL-terminated byte arrays.
- `Slice syscall_string_slice_ptr(Alloc *a, Slice ss)` – StringSlicePtr converts a slice of strings to a slice of pointers to NUL-terminated byte arrays.
- `void syscall_slice_ptr_free(Alloc *a, Slice bb)` – Gives back what syscall_slice_ptr_from_strings or syscall_string_slice_ptr made from a.
- `Slice syscall_mmap(Int fd, int64_t offset, Int length, Int prot, Int flags, Error *err)` – syscall.Mmap: map length bytes of fd from offset, with the system's PROT\_ and MAP\_ flags, and give them back as a Slice of bytes.
- `Error syscall_munmap(Slice b)`
- `void syscall_iovec_set_len(SyscallIovec *iov, Int length)` – Iovec.SetLen, Msghdr.SetControllen and Cmsghdr.SetLen, which set a length whose width depends on the system.
- `void syscall_msghdr_set_controllen(SyscallMsghdr *msghdr, Int length)`
- `void syscall_cmsghdr_set_len(SyscallCmsghdr *cmsg, Int length)`
- `extern bool syscall_socket_disable_ipv6` – For testing: clients can set this flag to force creation of IPv6 sockets to return EAFNOSUPPORT.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_INET4` – The descriptors of the Sockaddr types, the self_type of the vtables.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_INET6`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_UNIX`
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_inet4_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrInet4 *sa)` – sa as a Sockaddr.
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_inet6_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrInet6 *sa)`
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_unix_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrUnix *sa)`
- `void syscall_sockaddr_free(Alloc *a, SyscallSockaddr sa)` – Gives back to a a Sockaddr that syscall_accept, syscall_getsockname, syscall_getpeername, syscall_recvfrom, syscall_recvmsg or, on Windows, syscall_raw_sockaddr_any_sockaddr made from a.
- `Int syscall_socket(Int domain, Int typ, Int proto, Error *err)` – Socket: socket(2).
- `SyscallSocketpairRet syscall_socketpair(Int domain, Int typ, Int proto, Error *err)`
- `Error syscall_bind(Int fd, SyscallSockaddr sa)` – Bind and Connect: bind(2) and connect(2) to sa.
- `Error syscall_connect(Int fd, SyscallSockaddr sa)`
- `Int syscall_accept(Alloc *a, Int fd, SyscallSockaddr *sa, Error *err)` – Accept: accept(2), with the peer's address made from a in \*sa.
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_getsockname(Alloc *a, Int fd, Error *err)` – Getsockname and Getpeername: the socket's own address and its peer's, made from a.
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_getpeername(Alloc *a, Int fd, Error *err)`
- `Int syscall_recvfrom(Alloc *a, Int fd, Slice p, Int flags, SyscallSockaddr *from, Error *err)` – Recvfrom: recvfrom(2) into p.
- `Int syscall_recvmsg(Alloc *a, Int fd, Slice p, Slice oob, Int flags, Int *oobn, Int *recvflags, SyscallSockaddr *from, Error *err)` – Recvmsg: recvmsg(2) into p, with the control messages in oob.
- `Error syscall_sendto(Int fd, Slice p, Int flags, SyscallSockaddr to)` – Sendto: sendto(2) of p to to, or with no address when to is the zero Sockaddr.
- `Int syscall_sendmsg_n(Int fd, Slice p, Slice oob, SyscallSockaddr to, Int flags, Error *err)` – SendmsgN: sendmsg(2) of p with the control messages in oob, to to, or with no address when to is the zero Sockaddr.
- `Error syscall_sendmsg(Int fd, Slice p, Slice oob, SyscallSockaddr to, Int flags)`
- `Int syscall_getsockopt_int(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)` – The Getsockopt functions: getsockopt(2) of a value of each type.
- `SyscallGetsockoptInet4AddrRet syscall_getsockopt_inet4_addr(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)`
- `SyscallIPMreq syscall_getsockopt_ip_mreq(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)`
- `SyscallIPv6Mreq syscall_getsockopt_ipv6_mreq(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)`
- `SyscallIPv6MTUInfo syscall_getsockopt_ipv6_mtu_info(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)`
- `SyscallICMPv6Filter syscall_getsockopt_icmpv6_filter(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_byte(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, uint8_t value)` – The Setsockopt functions: setsockopt(2) of a value of each type.
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_int(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Int value)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_inet4_addr(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, const uint8_t value[4])`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_ip_mreq(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallIPMreq *mreq)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_ipv6_mreq(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallIPv6Mreq *mreq)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_icmpv6_filter(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallICMPv6Filter *filter)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_linger(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallLinger *l)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_string(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Str s)`
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_timeval(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallTimeval *tv)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKET_CONTROL_MESSAGE`
- `Int syscall_cmsg_len(Int datalen)` – CmsgLen returns the value to store in the Len field of the Cmsghdr structure, taking into account any necessary alignment.
- `Int syscall_cmsg_space(Int datalen)` – CmsgSpace returns the number of bytes an ancillary element with payload of the passed data length occupies.
- `Slice syscall_parse_socket_control_message(Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – ParseSocketControlMessage parses b as an array of socket control messages.
- `Slice syscall_unix_rights(Alloc *a, Slice fds)` – UnixRights encodes a set of open file descriptors into a socket control message for sending to another process.
- `Slice syscall_parse_unix_rights(Alloc *a, SyscallSocketControlMessage *m, Error *err)` – ParseUnixRights decodes a socket control message that contains an integer array of open file descriptors from another process.
- `Int syscall_accept4(Alloc *a, Int fd, Int flags, SyscallSockaddr *sa, Error *err)` – Accept4: accept4(2), which takes SOCK_NONBLOCK and SOCK_CLOEXEC in flags, and is otherwise Accept.
- `SyscallIPMreqn syscall_getsockopt_ip_mreqn(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)` – GetsockoptIPMreqn and SetsockoptIPMreqn.
- `Error syscall_setsockopt_ip_mreqn(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, SyscallIPMreqn *mreq)`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_DATALINK` – The descriptor of SockaddrDatalink, a link layer address on the BSDs, and one as a Sockaddr, which borrows sa.
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_datalink_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrDatalink *sa)`
- `uint8_t syscall_getsockopt_byte(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)` – GetsockoptByte: getsockopt(2) of one byte.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_LINKLAYER` – The descriptors of SockaddrLinklayer, an AF_PACKET address, and SockaddrNetlink, and either as a Sockaddr, which borrows sa.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_SOCKADDR_NETLINK`
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_linklayer_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrLinklayer *sa)`
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_sockaddr_netlink_as_sockaddr(SyscallSockaddrNetlink *sa)`
- `SyscallUcred syscall_getsockopt_ucred(Int fd, Int level, Int opt, Error *err)` – GetsockoptUcred: the credentials of the peer of a Unix domain socket, with SO_PEERCRED.
- `Error syscall_bind_to_device(Int fd, Str device)` – BindToDevice binds the socket associated with fd to device.
- `Slice syscall_unix_credentials(Alloc *a, SyscallUcred *ucred)` – UnixCredentials encodes credentials into a socket control message for sending to another process.
- `SyscallUcred syscall_parse_unix_credentials(SyscallSocketControlMessage *m, Error *err)` – ParseUnixCredentials decodes a socket control message that contains credentials in a Ucred structure.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_NETLINK_ROUTE_ATTR`
- `Slice syscall_netlink_rib(Alloc *a, Int proto, Int family, Error *err)` – NetlinkRIB returns routing information base, as known as RIB, which consists of network facility information, states and parameters.
- `Slice syscall_parse_netlink_message(Alloc *a, Slice b, Error *err)` – ParseNetlinkMessage parses b as an array of netlink messages and returns the slice containing the NetlinkMessage structures.
- `Slice syscall_parse_netlink_route_attr(Alloc *a, SyscallNetlinkMessage *m, Error *err)` – ParseNetlinkRouteAttr parses m's payload as an array of netlink route attributes and returns the slice containing the NetlinkRouteAttr structures.

- `Error syscall_listen(Int s, Int n)` – Listen and Shutdown, which these two have through socketcall(2) and the others have generated.
- `Error syscall_shutdown(Int s, Int how)`
- `Error syscall_access(Str path, uint32_t mode)` – The path calls, each the at form with AT_FDCWD as Go does it.
- `Error syscall_chmod(Str path, uint32_t mode)`
- `Error syscall_chown(Str path, Int uid, Int gid)`
- `Int syscall_creat(Str path, uint32_t mode, Error *err)`

- `Error syscall_mkdir(Str path, uint32_t mode)`
- `Error syscall_mknod(Str path, uint32_t mode, Int dev)`
- `Error syscall_mkfifo(Str path, uint32_t mode)`
- `Int syscall_open(Str path, Int mode, uint32_t perm, Error *err)`
- `Int syscall_openat(Int dirfd, Str path, Int flags, uint32_t mode, Error *err)`
- `Int syscall_readlink(Str path, Slice buf, Error *err)` – Readlink returns the destination of the named symbolic link.
- `Error syscall_rename(Str oldpath, Str newpath)`
- `Error syscall_rmdir(Str path)`
- `Error syscall_symlink(Str oldpath, Str newpath)`
- `Error syscall_unlink(Str path)`
- `Error syscall_unlinkat(Int dirfd, Str path)`
- `Error syscall_faccessat(Int dirfd, Str path, uint32_t mode, Int flags)` – Faccessat: faccessat2 when there are flags, and when the kernel does not have it, the checks the C library makes, from the file's mode, owner and group, as Go does.
- `Error syscall_fchmodat(Int dirfd, Str path, uint32_t mode, Int flags)` – Fchmodat: fchmodat2 when there are flags.
- `Int syscall_epoll_create(Int size, Error *err)` – EpollCreate: EpollCreate1(0), and EINVAL for a size of 0 or less.
- `Error syscall_pipe(Slice p)` – Pipe and Pipe2: the two ends in p, a Slice of Int that has to have a length of 2, or it is EINVAL.
- `Error syscall_pipe2(Slice p, Int flags)`
- `Error syscall_utimes(Str path, Slice tv)` – Utimes, Futimesat and Futimes take a Slice of two SyscallTimeval, and UtimesNano two SyscallTimespec, the access time and then the modification time.
- `Error syscall_utimes_nano(Str path, Slice ts)`
- `Error syscall_futimesat(Int dirfd, Str path, Slice tv)`
- `Error syscall_futimes(Int fd, Slice tv)`
- `Str syscall_getwd(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Getwd: the working directory, from a.
- `Slice syscall_getgroups(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Getgroups: the supplementary group ids, a Slice of Int from a, nil if there are none.
- `Error syscall_mount(Str source, Str target, Str fstype, Uintptr flags, Str data)` – Mount: mount(2), with data passed as NULL when it is empty.
- `Error syscall_reboot(Int cmd)` – Reboot: reboot(2) with the two magic numbers.
- `Int syscall_getpgrp(void)` – Getpgrp: Getpgid(0).
- `Error syscall_getrlimit(Int resource, SyscallRlimit *rlim)` – Getrlimit and Setrlimit, through prlimit64 on this process.
- `Error syscall_setrlimit(Int resource, SyscallRlimit *rlim)`
- `Int syscall_sendfile(Int outfd, Int infd, int64_t *offset, Int count, Error *err)` – Sendfile copies up to count bytes from file descriptor infd to file descriptor outfd.
- `Error syscall_fcntl_flock(Uintptr fd, Int cmd, SyscallFlock_t *lk)` – FcntlFlock performs a fcntl syscall for the F_GETLK, F_SETLK or F_SETLKW command.
- `Int syscall_read_dirent(Int fd, Slice buf, Error *err)` – ReadDirent: Getdents.
- `Int syscall_parse_dirent(Alloc *a, Slice buf, Int max, Slice names, Int *count, Slice *newnames)` – ParseDirent parses up to max directory entries in buf, appending the names to names.

- `Uintptr syscall_syscall_n(Uintptr p, Slice args, Uintptr *r2, SyscallErrno *err)` – SyscallN executes procedure p with arguments args.
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_SYSCALL_DLL_ERROR`
- `Str syscall_dll_error_error(const SyscallDLLError *e)` – DLLError.Error, which is msg, and DLLError.Unwrap, which is err.
- `Error syscall_dll_error_unwrap(const SyscallDLLError *e)`
- `SyscallDLL *syscall_load_dll(Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – LoadDLL loads the named DLL file into memory.
- `SyscallDLL *syscall_must_load_dll(Alloc *a, Str name)` – MustLoadDLL is like LoadDLL but panics if load operation fails.
- `SyscallProc *syscall_dll_find_proc(SyscallDLL *d, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – FindProc searches DLL d for procedure named name and returns \*Proc if found.
- `SyscallProc *syscall_dll_must_find_proc(SyscallDLL *d, Alloc *a, Str name)` – MustFindProc is like DLL.FindProc but panics if search fails.
- `Error syscall_dll_release(SyscallDLL *d)` – Release unloads DLL d from memory.
- `void syscall_dll_free(Alloc *a, SyscallDLL *d)` – Gives back what syscall_load_dll and syscall_dll_find_proc took from a, without releasing the DLL.
- `void syscall_proc_free(Alloc *a, SyscallProc *p)`
- `Uintptr syscall_proc_addr(const SyscallProc *p)` – Addr returns the address of the procedure represented by p.
- `Uintptr syscall_proc_call(const SyscallProc *p, Slice args, Uintptr *r2, Error *err)` – Call executes procedure p with arguments a.
- `SyscallLazyDLL *syscall_new_lazy_dll(Alloc *a, Str name)` – NewLazyDLL creates new LazyDLL associated with DLL file.
- `Error syscall_lazy_dll_load(SyscallLazyDLL *d)` – Load loads DLL file d.Name into memory.
- `Uintptr syscall_lazy_dll_handle(SyscallLazyDLL *d)` – Handle returns d's module handle.
- `SyscallLazyProc *syscall_lazy_dll_new_proc(SyscallLazyDLL *d, Alloc *a, Str name)` – NewProc returns a LazyProc for accessing the named procedure in the DLL d.
- `Error syscall_lazy_proc_find(SyscallLazyProc *p)` – Find searches DLL for procedure named p.Name.
- `Uintptr syscall_lazy_proc_addr(SyscallLazyProc *p)` – Addr returns the address of the procedure represented by p.
- `Uintptr syscall_lazy_proc_call(SyscallLazyProc *p, Slice args, Uintptr *r2, Error *err)` – Call executes procedure p with arguments a.
- `Slice syscall_utf16_from_string(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – UTF16FromString returns the UTF-16 encoding of the UTF-8 string s, with a terminating NUL added.
- `uint16_t *syscall_utf16_ptr_from_string(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – UTF16PtrFromString returns pointer to the UTF-16 encoding of the UTF-8 string s, with a terminating NUL added.
- `Slice syscall_string_to_utf16(Alloc *a, Str s)` – StringToUTF16 returns the UTF-16 encoding of the UTF-8 string s, with a terminating NUL added.
- `uint16_t *syscall_string_to_utf16_ptr(Alloc *a, Str s)` – StringToUTF16Ptr returns pointer to the UTF-16 encoding of the UTF-8 string s, with a terminating NUL added.
- `Str syscall_utf16_to_string(Alloc *a, Slice s)` – UTF16ToString returns the UTF-8 encoding of the UTF-16 sequence s, with a terminating NUL removed.
- `Error syscall_read_file(SyscallHandle fd, Slice p, uint32_t *done, SyscallOverlapped *overlapped)` – ReadFile and WriteFile themselves, with the count in \*done.
- `Error syscall_write_file(SyscallHandle fd, Slice p, uint32_t *done, SyscallOverlapped *overlapped)`
- `Error syscall_close(SyscallHandle fd)` – Close: CloseHandle.
- `Error syscall_fsync(SyscallHandle fd)`
- `Error syscall_ftruncate(SyscallHandle fd, int64_t length)`
- `Error syscall_chdir(Str path)` – Chdir, Mkdir, Rmdir, Unlink and Rename: SetCurrentDirectory, CreateDirectory, RemoveDirectory, DeleteFile and MoveFile.
- `Error syscall_fchdir(SyscallHandle fd)` – Fchdir: makes the directory fd is open on the working directory, by its path, without the \\\\?\\ Windows puts in front of it.
- `Error syscall_fchmod(SyscallHandle fd, uint32_t mode)`
- `Error syscall_fchown(SyscallHandle fd, Int uid, Int gid)`
- `Str syscall_computer_name(Alloc *a, Error *err)` – ComputerName: GetComputerName, from a.
- `Int syscall_getpid(void)` – Getpid: GetCurrentProcessId.
- `Int syscall_getppid(void)`
- `Int syscall_getuid(void)` – Getuid, Geteuid, Getgid and Getegid: -1.
- `Int syscall_geteuid(void)`
- `Int syscall_getgid(void)`
- `Int syscall_getegid(void)`
- `SyscallHandle syscall_find_first_file(uint16_t *name, SyscallWin32finddata *data, Error *err)` – FindFirstFile and FindNextFile: FindFirstFileW and FindNextFileW into \*data.
- `Error syscall_find_next_file(SyscallHandle handle, SyscallWin32finddata *data)`
- `SyscallHandle syscall_create_file(uint16_t *name, uint32_t access, uint32_t mode, SyscallSecurityAttributes *sa, uint32_t createmode, uint32_t attrs, int32_t templatefile, Error *err)` – CreateFile: CreateFileW. A good handle comes with no error, even when Windows says ERROR_ALREADY_EXISTS, as it does when OPEN_ALWAYS opens a file that was there.
- `Error syscall_get_startup_info(SyscallStartupInfo *si)` – GetStartupInfo: GetStartupInfoW, which cannot fail.
- `Error syscall_reg_enum_key_ex(SyscallHandle key, uint32_t index, uint16_t *name, uint32_t *name_len, uint32_t *reserved, uint16_t *class_, uint32_t *class_len, SyscallFiletime *last_write_time)` – RegEnumKeyEx enumerates the subkeys of an open registry key.
- `uint32_t syscall_format_message(uint32_t flags, uint32_t msgsrc, uint32_t msgid, uint32_t langid, Slice buf, uint8_t *args, Error *err)` – FormatMessage is deprecated (msgsrc should be uintptr, not uint32, but can not be changed due to the Go 1 compatibility guarantee).
- `SyscallHandle syscall_create_io_completion_port(SyscallHandle filehandle, SyscallHandle cphandle, uint32_t key, uint32_t threadcnt, Error *err)` – Deprecated: CreateIoCompletionPort has the wrong function signature.
- `Error syscall_get_queued_completion_status(SyscallHandle cphandle, uint32_t *qty, uint32_t *key, SyscallOverlapped **overlapped, uint32_t timeout)` – Deprecated: GetQueuedCompletionStatus has the wrong function signature.
- `Error syscall_post_queued_completion_status(SyscallHandle cphandle, uint32_t qty, uint32_t key, SyscallOverlapped *overlapped)` – Deprecated: PostQueuedCompletionStatus has the wrong function signature.
- `Error syscall_load_cancel_io_ex(void)` – LoadCancelIoEx, LoadSetFileCompletionNotificationModes, LoadGetAddrInfo and LoadCreateSymbolicLink: look the function up, and say why if the system does not have it.
- `Error syscall_load_set_file_completion_notification_modes(void)`
- `Error syscall_load_get_addr_info(void)`
- `Error syscall_load_create_symbolic_link(void)`
- `Uintptr syscall_new_callback(void (*fn)(void))` – NewCallback converts a Go function to a function pointer conforming to the stdcall calling convention.
- `Uintptr syscall_new_callback_c_decl(void (*fn)(void))` – NewCallbackCDecl converts a Go function to a function pointer conforming to the cdecl calling convention.
- `SyscallSockaddr syscall_raw_sockaddr_any_sockaddr(SyscallRawSockaddrAny *rsa, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – RawSockaddrAny.Sockaddr: the Sockaddr for the address in rsa, made from a.
- `Error syscall_wsa_sendto(SyscallHandle s, SyscallWSABuf *bufs, uint32_t bufcnt, uint32_t *sent, uint32_t flags, SyscallSockaddr to, SyscallOverlapped *overlapped, uint8_t *croutine)` – WSASendto: WSASendTo to to, or with no address when to is the zero Sockaddr.
- `extern SyscallGUID syscall_wsaid_connectex` – syscall.WSAID_CONNECTEX: the GUID WSAIoctl looks ConnectEx up by.
- `Error syscall_load_connect_ex(void)` – LoadConnectEx: finds ConnectEx, through WSAIoctl on a TCP socket made for the purpose, the first time it is called, and says why it could not then and every time after.
- `Error syscall_connect_ex(SyscallHandle fd, SyscallSockaddr sa, uint8_t *send_buf, uint32_t send_data_len, uint32_t *bytes_sent, SyscallOverlapped *overlapped)` – ConnectEx: an overlapped connect to sa, sending send_data_len bytes from send_buf once it is connected.
- `extern Slice syscall_oid_pkix_kp_server_auth` – syscall.OID_PKIX_KP_SERVER_AUTH, OID_SERVER_GATED_CRYPTO and OID_SGC_NETSCAPE: the object identifiers, as bytes with a NUL after them, for CertVerifyCertificateChainPolicy.
- `extern Slice syscall_oid_server_gated_crypto`
- `extern Slice syscall_oid_sgc_netscape`
- `Str syscall_translate_account_name(Alloc *a, Str username, uint32_t from, uint32_t to, Int init_size, Error *err)` – TranslateAccountName converts a directory service object name from one format to another.
- `SyscallSID *syscall_string_to_sid(Alloc *a, Str s, Error *err)` – StringToSid converts a string-format security identifier sid into a valid, functional sid.
- `SyscallSID *syscall_lookup_sid(Alloc *a, Str system, Str account, Str *domain, uint32_t *acc_type, Error *err)` – LookupSID retrieves a security identifier sid for the account and the name of the domain on which the account was found.
- `Str syscall_sid_string(SyscallSID *sid, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – String converts sid to a string format suitable for display, storage, or transmission.
- `Int syscall_sid_len(SyscallSID *sid)` – Len returns the length, in bytes, of a valid security identifier sid.
- `SyscallSID *syscall_sid_copy(SyscallSID *sid, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – Copy creates a duplicate of security identifier sid.
- `Str syscall_sid_lookup_account(SyscallSID *sid, Alloc *a, Str system, Str *domain, uint32_t *acc_type, Error *err)` – LookupAccount retrieves the name of the account for this sid and the name of the first domain on which this sid is found.
- `void syscall_sid_free(Alloc *a, SyscallSID *sid)` – Gives back a SID that syscall_string_to_sid, syscall_lookup_sid or syscall_sid_copy made from a.
- `SyscallToken syscall_open_current_process_token(Error *err)` – OpenCurrentProcessToken opens the access token associated with current process.
- `Error syscall_token_close(SyscallToken t)` – Close releases access to access token.
- `SyscallTokenuser *syscall_token_get_token_user(SyscallToken t, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GetTokenUser retrieves access token t user account information.
- `SyscallTokenprimarygroup *syscall_token_get_token_primary_group(SyscallToken t, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GetTokenPrimaryGroup retrieves access token t primary group information.
- `void syscall_token_info_free(Alloc *a, void *info)`
- `Str syscall_token_get_user_profile_directory(SyscallToken t, Alloc *a, Error *err)` – GetUserProfileDirectory retrieves path to the root directory of the access token t user's profile.

## burrow/testing.h

The sentences taken from Go are reproduced from the doc comments of Go's `testing` package, Copyright 2009 The Go Authors, BSD-3-Clause.

- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TESTING_INTERNAL_TEST`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TESTING_INTERNAL_BENCHMARK`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TESTING_INTERNAL_FUZZ_TARGET`
- `extern const Type *const TYPE_TESTING_INTERNAL_EXAMPLE`
- `void testing_init(int argc, char **argv)` – Init registers testing flags.
- `bool testing_short(void)` – Short reports whether the -test.short flag is set.
- `bool testing_verbose(void)` – Verbose reports whether the -test.v flag is set.
- `bool testing_testing(void)` – Testing reports whether the current code is being run in a test.
- `Str testing_cover_mode(void)` – testing.CoverMode and testing.Coverage.
- `double testing_coverage(void)` – Coverage reports the current code coverage as a fraction in the range \[0, 1\].
- `bool testing_match_string(Str pat, Str str, Error *err)` – The matcher used when nobody supplies one.
- `TestingM *testing_main_start(TestingMatchString match, Slice tests, Slice benchmarks, Slice fuzz_targets, Slice examples)` – MainStart is meant for use by tests generated by 'go test'.
- `int testing_m_run(TestingM *m)` – Run runs the tests.
- `void testing_m_free(TestingM *m)`
- `void testing_m_set_bare(TestingM *m, bool bare)` – Runs the tests on the calling thread rather than in goroutines.
- `void testing_main(TestingMatchString match, Slice tests, Slice benchmarks, Slice examples)` – Main is an internal function, part of the implementation of the "go test" command.
- `bool testing_run_tests(TestingMatchString match, Slice tests)` – RunTests is an internal function but exported because it is cross-package; it is part of the implementation of the "go test" command.
- `Str testing_t_name(TestingT *t)` – Name returns the name of the running (sub-) test or benchmark.
- `void testing_t_fail(TestingT *t)` – Fail marks the function as having failed but continues execution.
- `bool testing_t_failed(TestingT *t)` – Failed reports whether the function has failed.
- `void testing_t_fail_now(TestingT *t)` – FailNow marks the function as having failed and stops its execution by calling runtime.Goexit (which then runs all deferred calls in the current goroutine).
- `void testing_t_skip_now(TestingT *t)` – SkipNow marks the test as having been skipped and stops its execution by calling runtime.Goexit.
- `bool testing_t_skipped(TestingT *t)` – Skipped reports whether the test was skipped.
- `void testing_t_helper(TestingT *t)` – Helper marks the calling function as a test helper function.
- `void testing_t_cleanup(TestingT *t, Func f)` – Cleanup registers a function to be called when the test (or subtest) and all its subtests complete.
- `void testing_t_parallel(TestingT *t)` – Parallel signals that this test is to be run in parallel with (and only with) other parallel tests, and pauses until all non-parallel tests have finished.
- `bool testing_t_run(TestingT *t, Str name, TestingTFunc f)` – Run runs f as a subtest of t called name.
- `Context testing_t_context(TestingT *t)` – Context returns a context that is canceled just before Cleanup-registered functions are called.
- `bool testing_t_deadline(TestingT *t, int64_t *when)` – Deadline reports the time at which the test binary will have exceeded the timeout specified by the -timeout flag.
- `IoWriter testing_t_output(TestingT *t)` – Output returns a Writer that writes to the same test output stream as TB.Log.
- `void testing_t_attr(TestingT *t, Str key, Str value)` – Attr emits a test attribute associated with this test.
- `TestingTB testing_t_as_testing_tb(TestingT *t)`
- `#define testing_t_log(t, args)` – Log formats its arguments using default formatting, analogous to fmt.Println, and records the text in the error log.
- `#define testing_t_logf(t, format, args)` – Logf formats its arguments according to the format, analogous to fmt.Printf, and records the text in the error log.
- `#define testing_t_error(t, args)` – Error is equivalent to Log followed by Fail.
- `#define testing_t_errorf(t, format, args)` – Errorf is equivalent to Logf followed by Fail.
- `#define testing_t_fatal(t, args)` – Fatal is equivalent to Log followed by FailNow.
- `#define testing_t_fatalf(t, format, args)` – Fatalf is equivalent to Logf followed by FailNow.
- `#define testing_t_skip(t, args)` – Skip is equivalent to Log followed by SkipNow.
- `#define testing_t_skipf(t, format, args)` – Skipf is equivalent to Logf followed by SkipNow.
- `#define testing_t_log_v(t, ...)` – The operands written out, as fmt's \_v macros take them:
- `#define testing_t_logf_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_error_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_errorf_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_fatal_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_fatalf_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_skip_v(t, ...)`
- `#define testing_t_skipf_v(t, ...)`
- `int64_t testing_benchmark_result_ns_per_op(TestingBenchmarkResult r)` – NsPerOp returns the "ns/op" metric.
- `int64_t testing_benchmark_result_allocs_per_op(TestingBenchmarkResult r)` – AllocsPerOp returns the "allocs/op" metric, which is calculated as r.MemAllocs / r.N.
- `int64_t testing_benchmark_result_alloced_bytes_per_op(TestingBenchmarkResult r)` – AllocedBytesPerOp returns the "B/op" metric, which is calculated as r.MemBytes / r.N.
- `Str testing_benchmark_result_string(Alloc *a, TestingBenchmarkResult r)` – String returns a summary of the benchmark results.
- `Str testing_benchmark_result_mem_string(Alloc *a, TestingBenchmarkResult r)` – MemString returns r.AllocedBytesPerOp and r.AllocsPerOp in the same format as 'go test'.
- `bool testing_benchmark_result_extra(TestingBenchmarkResult r, Str unit, double *value)` – Extra records additional metrics reported by ReportMetric.
- `void testing_benchmark_result_free(TestingBenchmarkResult *r)`
- `TestingBenchmarkResult testing_benchmark(TestingBFunc f)` – Benchmark benchmarks a single function.
- `void testing_run_benchmarks(TestingMatchString match, Slice benchmarks)` – RunBenchmarks is an internal function but exported because it is cross-package; it is part of the implementation of the "go test" command.
- `bool testing_b_loop(TestingB *b)` – Loop returns true as long as the benchmark should continue running.
- `Int testing_b_n(TestingB *b)` – b.N, for the older form of the loop:
- `void testing_b_start_timer(TestingB *b)` – StartTimer starts timing a test.
- `void testing_b_stop_timer(TestingB *b)` – StopTimer stops timing a test.
- `void testing_b_reset_timer(TestingB *b)` – ResetTimer zeroes the elapsed benchmark time and memory allocation counters and deletes user-reported metrics.
- `void testing_b_set_bytes(TestingB *b, int64_t n)` – SetBytes records the number of bytes processed in a single operation.
- `void testing_b_report_allocs(TestingB *b)` – ReportAllocs enables malloc statistics for this benchmark.
- `void testing_b_report_metric(TestingB *b, double n, Str unit)` – ReportMetric adds "n unit" to the reported benchmark results.
- `int64_t testing_b_elapsed(TestingB *b)` – Elapsed returns the measured elapsed time of the benchmark.
- `bool testing_b_run(TestingB *b, Str name, TestingBFunc f)` – Run benchmarks f as a subbenchmark with the given name.
- `void testing_b_run_parallel(TestingB *b, TestingPBFunc body)` – RunParallel runs a benchmark in parallel.
- `void testing_b_set_parallelism(TestingB *b, int p)` – SetParallelism sets the number of goroutines used by B.RunParallel to p\*GOMAXPROCS.
- `bool testing_pb_next(TestingPB *pb)` – Next reports whether there are more iterations to execute.
- `Str testing_b_name(TestingB *b)` – Name returns the name of the running (sub-) test or benchmark.
- `void testing_b_fail(TestingB *b)` – Fail marks the function as having failed but continues execution.
- `bool testing_b_failed(TestingB *b)` – Failed reports whether the function has failed.

- `bool testing_b_skipped(TestingB *b)` – Skipped reports whether the test was skipped.

- `FstestMapFS fstest_map_fs_make(Alloc *a)` – An empty MapFS with its map from a.

- `FsFile fstest_map_fs_open(FstestMapFS fsys, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)` – Open opens the named file after following any symbolic links.
- `Slice fstest_map_fs_read_file(FstestMapFS fsys, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)`
- `FsFileInfo fstest_map_fs_stat(FstestMapFS fsys, Alloc *a, Str name, Error *err)`
